# ORVION MASTER GAP REGISTER

Status: **Permanent single source of truth for architectural findings.** Never recreate; only evolve. Every future review updates this file (add rows, update status/dates, never delete — resolved items are marked Resolved and kept for history).

Owner policy in force: **Earn-It suspended for design/assurance only.** Findings are classified **Architecturally Required** (evidence proves it belongs in a complete modern Travel ERP/CRM/Revenue platform → its *design* must exist now) or **Architecturally Optional** (genuine independent/optimization choice). **Implementation timing belongs only to the owner.** The phrase "can be added later" is not used.

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Decision-debt closure pass — six items labelled "BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION" or "OWNER DECISION" were not business decisions at all.** The owner directed that no open decision be left unresolved merely because a document labels it one, and that engineering resolve what engineering evidence can settle. Re-deriving each from the implementation rather than trusting its label closed **FIN-7** (the invoice state machine already exists and is enforced *in the RPCs* — `issue_invoice` refuses anything but `draft`, `record_payment` **derives** `paid`/`partially_paid` from amounts; what is missing is the second door, `invoices` having no `enforce_status_transition` trigger — BOOK-1/ADMIN-1/FIN-8/FIN-10/QUO-1's class, not a policy), **VERIFY-1** (canon 07 makes verifying and approving **one act**, already implemented through `approval_requests`; the two columns must never get a writer), **TRANS-1** (keep the guarded duplication — the cure needs a sparse or jsonb column, trading a machine-checked duplication for polymorphism no constraint can check), **LIC-1** (accept the residual: a 128-bit single-use token makes the attack the missing audit would detect infeasible, so an autonomous-transaction extension on the security path is a net loss), **DELIV-1 + PH8-2** (observability, not policy — the four states a health view would report already exist), and **QUO-4** (canon 28's own scope column reads "assigned/department" and the 2026-08-24 directive granted department continuity; behaviour already correct). **RET-1 reclassified**: NULL means retain-forever, the scan's WHERE is unsatisfiable, so the safe answer is already in force and it is not blocking. **SUP-4c narrowed to one question** — `tenants.default_currency_code` and a tenant-maintained `exchange_rates` with `effective_at` already exist, so nothing needs inventing; only the conversion INSTANT is a policy choice. **VOID-1 narrowed on external evidence**: Egypt's ETA permits cancellation within 7 days and requires credit notes thereafter, so the register's "void OR credit note" dichotomy was false. **DOC-LC-3 split** — the integrity half is fixable without deciding un-archiving. Three new findings: **TAX-1** (`external_submission_status_code` is guarded against a catalog type that does not exist, so the column can only ever be NULL), **GOV-15** (TRANS-1 carried two blocks disagreeing on Status *and* Owner, invisible to Check 2 because both are open forms) and **GOV-16** (nothing binds the register's open rows to the manifest's decision line — the whole DC-* family and PH8-3…8 are invisible at boot). Full analysis in the "Decision-debt closure pass" section at the end of this file. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-09-04 (**Post-reconciliation remediation — three guard/registry defects, two of them found by attacking the previous audit's own conclusions.** The 2026-09-04 forensic audit raised three observations; investigating them from the repository rather than accepting them changed two. **OBS-2 was not "three findings missing a summary row"** — 59 of 98 detail blocks have no row and that is the register's own convention; the real defect was **GOV-11**, Check 2's detail-block status reader matching **0 of 96** blocks since 2026-08-29 because it expected a bullet form the register never writes, with a second half (the cross-Master pass never reading blocks at all) found only by mutating the first fix. **OBS-3 was not merely "documented and intentional"** — the audit missed that origin's `RBAC-3` table row and the local `RBAC-5` detail block are the SAME finding, so one finding sat under two ids while `RBAC-3` also headed two blocks for a different finding (**GOV-12**). **GOV-13 is new and was missed entirely:** the merge deleted two pgTAP files and left three live references naming them, one of them a register row citing a deleted test as its proof; Check 1 resolved document tokens only and is now widened. **CANON-31-1** adds the grant model to canon 31. **GOV-14** records that the audit itself left no repository artifact. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-09-03 (**Git divergence reconciled — two legitimate histories, one authoritative `main`.** Two sessions diverged from `4b67d3f` on 2026-09-02 without fetching: one produced COLD-2/COLD-3/SUP-2/SUP-3/RBAC-3/SUP-4a and pushed them; the other produced GOV-10 and the RECOVER-1 recovery + closure and could not push. Merged with `--no-ff`, no rebase, no force. **Three semantic duplicates resolved deliberately, none of which git flagged as a conflict on its own:** `GOV-10 ≡ COLD-2` is ONE defect found twice — a second copy of the comparison would have counted one divergence as two issues, a guard lying about magnitude — so ONE implementation is kept and both rows are retained and cross-referenced; **ADR-0027** was written twice for one decision and is now ONE record; and the two independently-added Check 18s were separated, COLD-3 keeping **Check 18** and RECOVER-1's ledger evidence renumbered to **Check 19**. The four SUP/RBAC migrations were proven byte-identical across both histories by git blob hash, so the merged tree carries 189 migrations matching Primary exactly. No RBAC/SUP work and no RECOVER-1 work was discarded. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-09-03 (**The four orphaned migrations, and the authorization refactor nobody could see.** `202607059600`–`202607059900` were applied to Primary on 2026-09-02 and **never present in THIS CLONE**: Primary held 188 migrations while this working copy held 184, and every guard reported CLEAN because none of them compares the repository's migration COUNT against Primary's. Recovered byte-identical from Primary's ledger and verified by md5. Seven rows added — **RECOVER-1** (the parity class itself), **SUP-2**, **SUP-3**, **SUP-4a**, **SUP-4b** (the one genuine owner decision), **RBAC-5** and **RBAC-6**. **RBAC-2's 2026-08-29 resolution is superseded**: it concluded `MANAGE_PERMISSIONS` "gates a capability ORVION deliberately does not offer to tenants", and RBAC-5 made that permission the gate on per-user capability administration. **CORRECTED 2026-09-03 (reconciliation):** the four were never orphaned from the REPOSITORY — they were committed and pushed as `b72caff`/`46a0cfc`/`1193643` while this clone worked forward from `4b67d3f` without fetching. The recovery was nonetheless exact: git blob hashes prove the reconstructed files are byte-identical to the originals. What the incident actually demonstrates is a FETCH gap, not an orphaning — and Primary-vs-clone drift is still the class RECOVER-1 names. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-09-02 (**RBAC-3 refactor (ADR-0027); SUP-4 split into SUP-4a FIXED and SUP-4b OPEN.** The owner authorised a full RBAC rebuild if the evidence supported one; it did not. `role_permissions` was the only edge into `permissions` — one missing user->permission edge — while 197 enforcement sites already delegate to a single function, so the grant model was refactored and the enforcement plane left untouched. SUP-4 turned out to be two things: a schema defect derivable from canon 30 (the only money column in ORVION without a currency), now fixed, and a genuine business decision about what the ceiling refuses, still open. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-09-02 (**SUP-3 DECIDED by the owner and implemented; SUP-4 added.** The owner made supplier credit-limit management its own permission, `MANAGE_SUPPLIER_CREDIT`, granted to owner/ceo/finance_manager and orthogonal to both ASSIGN_SUPPLIER and VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS. SUP-4 records the enforcement half the owner also asked for and which was deliberately NOT invented: ORVION has an authoritative supplier PAYABLE but no authoritative definition of what counts against the LIMIT, because the limit is currency-less and the payable is per-currency. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-09-02 (**SUP-2 and SUP-3 added** — the Batch-6 table-by-table audit's next slice. SUP-1 withheld `suppliers.credit_limit_amount` on READ and left its WRITE on a permission three roles hold without the read one, so for exactly those three the withholding did nothing. Two packages had each recorded the other as covering that half. SUP-3 records the commercial question SUP-2 deliberately did not answer. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-09-02 (**COLD-3 added** — the handoff pointer was never checked against the lifecycle it points into: the repository was CLEAN while `manifest.md` named a `[x] Complete` Change Request as the active one, and equally CLEAN while it named a CR file that has never existed. Closes the Complete-sync safeguard that `future-backlog.md` had carried since SPEC-024/027. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-09-02 (**COLD-2 added** — the Check-7 blind spot on `ai-map.json`'s `active_change_request`, the field the boot sequence branches on, together with the previously unrecorded `last_completed` repair of `4b67d3f`. Rows added on 2026-09-01 — MEAS-4, REG-1, OWNER-1, PD-23, COLD-1, META-1 — arrived without this header moving, which is the same drift the 2026-08-29 entry below describes about itself. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-08-29 (**Program reconciliation before Phase 10.** Eleven rows added, none of them a new engineering defect in the product: seven findings that were DEFINED in Master documents but never registered here — **A3**, **LIC-1**, **BLOCKED-4**, **BLOCKED-5**, **CANON-26-1**, **DEAD-1**, **DEAD-2** — plus **GOV-2…GOV-6**, the governance and guard defects the reconciliation itself surfaced. Five of the seven are ids `manifest.md` raises as open owner decisions on a line promising their definitions live here, so the boot sequence pointed at a dead end. **Check 11** now fails the build on that class. The header date was itself the drift it describes: rows dated 08-24 through 08-29 sat under a "Last updated: 2026-08-21" line. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-08-21 (**Foundation freeze gate, continued** - **RPC-2 RESOLVED** by SPEC-134 from canon rather than invention, and without amending canon 28: sub-records follow the create_passenger precedent, suppliers use the one supplier capability canon defines, campaigns use the tenant-scoped permission that already exists. Four more entities now have governed write paths; tables with no RPC insert path 35 -> 26. Contact data on contact methods and suppliers is now canonicalized the same way customers is. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-08-21 (**Foundation freeze gate** - RPC-1 advanced to **10 of 37 permissions closed**: SPEC-131 (Tasks) and SPEC-132 (customer-service domain), plus the first end-to-end employee walkthrough driving the real auth chain. New row **RPC-2** isolates the one genuinely irreducible part: four entities have no permission defined at all, so no RPC can enforce one without fabricating canon. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-08-21 (**Foundation gate, continued** — **RPC-1 programme started**: SPEC-131 delivers the governed Task write path, closing 3 of 37 unenforced permissions and establishing the pattern. The row now enumerates the exact remaining work and records the evidence that none of it needs a business decision. **REL-1 resolved** by SPEC-130; new row **REL-2**. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-08-21 (**Foundation gate, continued** — **REL-1 resolved** by SPEC-130: the polymorphic entity link on tasks/notifications/approval_requests is now paired, vocabulary-controlled, existence-checked and tenant-checked. The polymorphic *shape* was kept deliberately — canon 29 states "any business entity", so the typed-FK alternative was rejected as contradicting domain intent rather than adopted for convenience. New row **REL-2** records the one thing SPEC-130 deliberately did not decide. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-08-21 (**Foundation gate** — **TENANT-1 discovered and resolved**: every tenant-scoped FK permitted a cross-tenant reference (189 FKs, 50 child tables, 26 parents, zero composite keys) — found not by inspection but by a single failing assertion in SPEC-128, then generalised. **ATTR-1 resolved** by SPEC-128: conversion identity is now snapshotted at creation, so customer edits and retries can no longer rewrite what a historical event reported. Both behaviourally verified on Primary. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-08-21 (**Second health gate — CRM coherence & future-readiness** — four rows added, all from defects reproduced behaviourally: **VOCAB-1** (the catalog was canonical but not authoritative at the point of use — `tasks` accepted `TOTALLY_MADE_UP`, `suppliers` accepted `MADE_UP_SUPPLIER`, `conversations` accepted `carrier_pigeon`), resolved for the ungoverned set by SPEC-127; **RPC-1** (35 of 72 tables have no RPC write path, which means SEC-1 option (b) is not implementable today — a correction to this register's own prior framing); **REL-1** (unconstrained polymorphic references on tasks/notifications/approval_requests, while `document_links` solves the same problem correctly); and **ATTR-1** (conversion identity derived from mutable customer fields at claim time, through an optional lead path). **CAT-4 narrowed** — now resolved for the 12 trigger-covered tables, open only for RPC-written columns. Prior entry follows.)

Previously: 2026-08-21 (**Health gate** — four rows added and one re-scored, all from defects **reproduced behaviourally before being fixed**, not inferred: **CAT-2** (tenant catalog extension structurally broken — one tenant's code blocked another's, and the error leaked its existence), **CAT-3** (casing/whitespace variants of a catalog code all coexisting — the "Egypt/egypt/EGYPT" failure mode in ORVION's own catalog), **NORM-1** (customer identity matched by exact case-sensitive equality, so `Ahmed@Gmail.com` and `ahmed@gmail.com` were two customers and the phone-uniqueness guard fell to a single space), and **CAT-4** (catalog deactivation not enforced at lookup — tripwire installed rather than fixed, impact currently zero). **CAT-1 was re-scored Low→Medium on new evidence** — proven reachable by an ordinary insert, not latent — and then resolved. All resolved by SPEC-126. Prior entry follows.)

## Validation reclassifications (2026-07-11 session 4 — authoritative)
The 9-stage evidence pipeline (`ARCHITECTURE_PROOF_LOG.md`) moved these rows OUT of accepted-required status. Their register rows below remain for traceability but are **superseded by these statuses**:
- **DC-13** UUIDv7 → **DEFERRED / PENDING** (out of Batch 0; benefit only at millions of rows, Supabase=PG17). See `PENDING_ARCHITECTURE_FINDINGS.md` RJ-1.
- **DC-6** sensitive-read log, **DC-8** self-healing, **DC-10** opening-balance domain, **DC-20** custom-fields, **DC-22** data-residency(KSA driver), **DC-24** tenant-custom-roles, **DC-25** retention → **PENDING (OPTIONAL / NEEDS MORE EVIDENCE)**.
- **DC-12** kept REQUIRED but **simplified** to `passengers.mahram_passenger_id` self-FK.
- **DC-21** kept REQUIRED but **presentation-layer** (store Gregorian, render Hijri) — near-zero schema impact.
- **Accepted-required (unchanged):** DC-1,2,3,4,5,7,9,11,14,15,16,23,27,28 + all inherited R/A/B/BF/CDD/N/INV/RC/OPS.
- **Net:** Batch-0 required-now structural touches = **R1–R8 + DC-1 only.**

Field model: the table below carries the queryable columns. The **per-ID detail blocks** below carry the full field set (Evidence · Business/Technical/Architectural justification · Affected tables/relationships/RPCs/events/catalogs/dropdowns/permissions/dashboards/reports/integrations/AI/MCP/automation/docs/ADR/canon/roadmap · Migration · Additive? · Root cause · Dependencies · Safest order). Inherited findings reference their source report for verbose fields to avoid loss or duplication.

Legend — **Req/Opt:** R = Architecturally Required · O = Architecturally Optional. **Mig:** M = modifies built object · A = additive. **Status:** OPEN · DESIGN-READY · RESOLVED · VERIFIED. **Cert:** 🔒 foundation-lock (cheapest before data) · 🛡 pre-production hardening · ➕ additive capability · 📋 backlog/ops · ✅ resolved.

| SEC-1 | `authenticated` holds direct `INSERT`/`UPDATE` on tenant tables, so a logged-in user of any ORVION role can write straight through PostgREST — bypassing `app.authorize()`, the state machines and event emission. RLS scopes **rows**, not **permissions**; every write RPC enforces RBAC inside the function while the tables stay directly writable. Materially higher risk if a client-side builder (WeWeb) becomes the frontend, since the browser holds the anon key and the user JWT. Write-side counterpart to AUDIT-3 (read side) | access | High | R | 5 | A | ✅ | **✅ RATIFIED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1). The recommendation below was ACCEPT WITH REFINEMENT, and the owner accepted it — RLS carries row scope; capability enforcement sits on the writable surfaces; business mutation rules stay in the owning RPC; integrity is path-independent. RPC-only writes were REJECTED.** This row carried "AWAITING OWNER RATIFICATION" for four days after that decision, while the **OWNER-1** row three rows down already recorded SEC-1 as decided and removed from the manifest's open list — the register contradicting itself about its own highest-profile finding. **Check 2 could not see it in either direction:** it reads a row as OPEN only on a cell that is exactly the padded literal `\| OPEN \|`, and as resolved only on a cell LEADING with `✅`/`RESOLVED`/`IMPLEMENTED`, so a status cell opening `**EVALUATED … AWAITING OWNER RATIFICATION**` is invisible to both halves of the comparison. Corrected here; the evaluation that earned the decision is kept verbatim below because it is the evidence. Remaining work under this ID is ENGINEERING (**SEC-1b** INSERT path, **SEC-1c** UPDATE path, **SUP-1** supplier-credit read half — all closed), never a question. Original evaluation follows: The owner's proposed model (RLS = row scope; RPCs = business mutation; direct DML only where structural; DB-level invariants path-independent; no cosmetic RPCs; RLS never a substitute for authorization) was tested against the live system rather than judged by opinion. **It is not a change of direction — it is the model ORVION has already converged on**, and each clause is separately evidenced: RLS row-scope (21 `scope_isolation` policies); RPC authority (71 endpoints); *no* cosmetic RPCs (RPC-1 found 35 tables without one and the programme REVOKED grants or added permission triggers instead of minting RPCs); RLS-alone rejected (**SEC-3** — `tenant_id` alone let any employee grant themselves owner). **Measured writable surface: 54 tables. 35 carry a capability trigger; 15 more are permission-gated in RLS `WITH CHECK`; `document_versions` is guarded by DOC-3's integrity trigger; the residual 3 are the canon-34 Human Identity tables, INTENTIONAL.** So clause 6 is already true and clause 3 is already close to true. **THE REFINEMENT — clause 3 needs a decision procedure, because "has no business invariant" is not decidable by inspection.** FIN-8, FIN-10 and QUO-1 each looked fine until someone asked *what does the RPC refuse that the table does not?* Proposed operational test, which is what actually found all three: **a rule that compares an AGGREGATE ACROSS ROWS cannot be a CHECK constraint and will therefore live in exactly one function unless deliberately extracted.** Measured: only two `app` functions aggregate-and-raise-and-write (`record_payment`, `add_quotation_item`), both now closed; `create_journal_entry` accumulates in a loop variable and is that filter's known blind spot, closed by FIN-8. **SECOND REFINEMENT — the exemption asymmetry, decided three times and now explicit:** authorization guards exempt `auth.uid() is null` (canon 35 principle 6); **integrity constraints must NOT** — a ledger corrupted by a migration is exactly as corrupt as one corrupted by a tenant user. FIN-8, FIN-10 and QUO-1 each pin this by assertion. **NOT RECOMMENDED: Option A (revoke `authenticated` INSERT/UPDATE wholesale).** It would require converting 56 SECURITY INVOKER functions to DEFINER — replacing RLS as the row authority with 56 hand-written tenant checks, which is the second authorization system canon 35 forbids. | — RATIFIED; no owner action outstanding. Engineering never needed the ratification to continue, and the refined model is what every package since has implemented | remediation-2026-08-21; evaluated api-3-sec1-2026-08-29; ratified OWNER-1 2026-09-01 | 08-21 | 09-01 |
| SEC-3 | **Privilege escalation via `user_role_assignments`.** `authenticated` held INSERT/UPDATE on the RBAC tables, whose only policy was `tenant_id = app.current_tenant_id()`. One INSERT — `(my tenant, my user id, role 'owner', 'tenant')` — satisfied that policy and made any employee an owner, defeating every permission check and the whole of AUDIT-3. Same shape on `user_branch_assignments`, `users`, `branches`, `departments`, `tenants`, `subscriptions`, `catalog_values`. **Distinct from SEC-1**: SEC-1 is a write that bypasses lifecycle rules on the caller's own data; this is a write that changes *who the caller is* | access | Critical | R | 5 | A | ➕ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-24 (SPEC-138).** Each table's write commands now additionally require the permission its own write RPC already authorized (`MANAGE_USERS` / `MANAGE_BRANCHES` / `MANAGE_DEPARTMENTS` / `MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS` / `MANAGE_SUBSCRIPTION`), taken from the RPC's own `authorize()` call so the two cannot drift. SELECT unchanged. Proven in `22_write_authority_test.sql` | — | foundation-programme-2026-08-24 | 08-24 | 08-24 |

SPEC-137's department continuity made this sharper, not softer: a colleague can legitimately open a colleague's booking item, and the margin came with it. Canon 31 records `commission_rate` as the reserved basis for sales commission, so the exposure is one employee's earnings to another | access | High | R | 5 | A | ➕ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-24 (SPEC-139).** The rule was already canon and needed no new permission — canon 28 scopes `VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS` as finance-roles-plus-"assigned related only". The table-level SELECT grant on `booking_items` is replaced by an explicit column list, and the numbers are re-served through `app.item_financials`, a SECURITY DEFINER accessor applying the rule per item. `app.booking_item_profit` stays SECURITY INVOKER so RLS remains the only authority over which rows appear. | SPP-1/SPP-2 | **The payment-proof table gated neither reads nor inserts, while its parent gated both.** Read live from `pg_policy`: `subscriptions.scope_read` = tenant AND `VIEW_SUBSCRIPTION_STATUS`, `subscriptions.scope_insert` = tenant AND `MANAGE_SUBSCRIPTION`; `subscription_payment_proofs.scope_read` = tenant ONLY and `.scope_insert` = tenant ONLY. **SPP-1**: every tenant user, down to a trainee, could read every payment proof — when the agency paid, who uploaded it, the reviewer's notes and the decision — while `subscriptions` itself is restricted to Owner/CEO. **SPP-2**: any tenant user could FORGE a pending payment proof by direct DML with no permission at all; they could not approve it (update was already gated) but a fabricated proof is audit pollution and a plausible way to mislead the Platform Owner into approving an unpaid renewal. **Third occurrence of the sibling-table class**, after FIN-1 and DOC-1/DOC-3 | security | **High** | R | 6 | A | ➕ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-27 (WP-04-C, `202607054800`).** Found by a FAILING assertion in this package's own new test, not by inspection — and it is a defect **WP-04-B should have caught**, since that package added the review path, catalog trigger and narrowed gate to this very table without comparing its RLS to its parent. Earlier tests missed it because no test had ever asked an ordinary EMPLOYEE to read a payment proof; every prior fixture used Owner or postgres. Fixed to parity: read requires `VIEW_SUBSCRIPTION_STATUS`; insert requires **`MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS`, deliberately NOT `MANAGE_SUBSCRIPTION`** — the one place the two tables must legitimately differ, because `MANAGE_SUBSCRIPTION` is held by no role (platform authority, SPEC-157) and requiring it would make proof upload impossible for everyone and destroy the only route back from a lapsed subscription. `platform_review_payment_proof` is SECURITY DEFINER so the Platform Owner still reviews proofs it could never read as a tenant user — the intended asymmetry. Guard: `47_payment_proof_lifecycle_test.sql` 17-19, whose first version was itself vacuous (the employee's `INSERT…SELECT` read zero rows from the table SPP-1 had just hidden) and was fixed to hand over concrete ids | none | wp-04c-document-storage-2026-08-27 | 08-27 | 08-27 |

| DOC-1/DOC-3 | **The document storage path was caller-supplied, and a version could be rewritten by direct DML.** Proven live: `app.upload_document` AND `app.add_document_version` both took `p_storage_path` as a parameter, and `authenticated` holds INSERT/UPDATE on `public.document_versions` directly — three paths on which the caller chose where a document points inside object storage. Harmless only because `storage.buckets` = 0 on Primary; the moment a bucket exists a caller can write under another tenant's prefix, defeating the very storage policy meant to stop them. Separately (DOC-3), nothing forced anyone through the RPC: `document_versions` RLS admits any row whose PARENT DOCUMENT is visible, so a version's `storage_path` could be inserted or UPDATEd arbitrarily with no permission check, no sequencing and no event — an invoice, passport or payment proof could be made to point at a different object while readers saw the same document id. The WP-00 audit-forgery class, one domain over | security | **High** | R | 6 | A | ➕ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-27 (WP-04-A, `202607054400`).** Fixed by derivation rather than validation, the shape SPEC-155 and WP-00 already settled: `app.document_storage_path` is the single source of the object key (tenant-first so storage policy can isolate on segment 1; provider-independent by design), and `app.enforce_document_version_integrity` derives `version_number`/`storage_path`/`uploaded_by` on INSERT and freezes a version's identity on UPDATE. No caller string enters the key at all — the human filename stays in `file_name`, removing path traversal and extension spoofing from the design. Direct DML now costs `CREATE_DOCUMENT_VERSION`, verified safe first (every role holding UPLOAD_DOCUMENT already holds it). Both RPCs dropped-and-recreated so no stale overload still accepts a path. | PLAN-1 | **Subscription plan gating does not exist.** `feature_entitlements` had zero rows and NO reader — no function in `app` or `reporting` references it — while canon 28 states "Plan denial overrides user role permission" and canon 09/17 define the matrix in full. The three plans were seeded; what each grants was not. `usage_counters` is likewise empty, so the numeric ceilings have no counter behind them | architecture | Medium | R | 5 | A | ➕ | **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 (owner-decision resolution review) — the ‘three undefined Limited ceilings’ were a MISREADING of canon, not an owner decision. Canon 28 marks FIVE features Limited (Basic Reporting/Starter; Automation, Integrations, Offline Conversion, Multi Branch/Professional), and uses `Limited` throughout its ROLE columns to mean SCOPE-RESTRICTED (`CREATE_LEAD … Limited … branch/department`), never a number. Canon 17's separate `Plan Numeric Limits` table holds every numeric ceiling — seven rows — and it already supplies the numbers for two of the five (`Automations 100`, `Branches 3`, both seeded correctly as `max_*` metric rows). The other three have NO row in canon 17 and NO usage metric (`usage_metric_code` holds exactly six: users, branches, monthly_bookings, monthly_leads, storage_gb, automations), so there is nothing to count and no ceiling to state. `app.plan_limit`'s own comment fixes the encoding: ‘NULL means no ceiling … never a large number a caller might compare against’, which is what Enterprise uses. Superseded text: PARTIALLY RESOLVED 2026-08-24 (SPEC-141 data + SPEC-146 enforcement) — the FEATURE half only; the numeric-ceiling half and the three undefined "Limited" ceilings remain OPEN, see the end of this cell.** 66 entitlement rows seeded from canon 28 + canon 17, with `feature_code` constrained. Enforcement is NOT wired, on canon's own instruction — canon 35 principle 8: subscription gating "is a distinct concern from tenant isolation ... decided at implementation, not here". The gate sits inside `app.has_permission` — the one function every RLS policy and every `app.authorize()` already calls — so the RPC path, the direct PostgREST read path, the direct write path, future n8n calls and a future UI are all covered without cooperating. Proven on all three surfaces in `30_plan_gating_test.sql`. `app.tenant_capabilities()` gives a UI/n8n the capability picture without inferring it from a failed write. **Still open**: numeric ceilings are readable (`app.plan_limit`) but nothing counts against them — `usage_counters` is empty and counting is a separate, additive mechanism. Subscription-*state* gating (`read_only` write restriction) remains distinct per canon 35 §8. Also open: canon marks Basic Reporting (Starter), Integrations and Offline Conversion (Professional) as "Limited" without defining a ceiling anywhere — an owner business decision, seeded uncapped rather than guessed | owner: the three undefined "Limited" ceilings; engineering: where the gate sits | foundation-programme-2026-08-24 | 08-24 | 08-24 |
| DUP-1 | **Duplicate prevention was check-then-insert only.** Every duplicate rule lived inside an RPC as `select ... if found then raise ... else insert`, with no index behind it. Two holes: a direct PostgREST write skips the RPC entirely, and two concurrent RPC calls both pass the SELECT before either INSERTs — the ordinary case of two employees answering the same enquiry at once. Canon 05 requires the customer primary phone to be "unique inside the company", and neither the rule nor its "approved exception" was enforced; the exception left no trace at all. Three further gaps are integration correctness rather than hygiene: `attribution_clicks` had no dedupe on gclid/gbraid/wbraid, so a replayed tag fire becomes a duplicate click — and the conversion mapper joins leads to their click, propagating it outward as a **double-counted conversion to Google**; `marketing_campaigns` resynced into duplicates; a replayed WhatsApp thread opened a second conversation. `exchange_rates` allowed two rates for one pair at one instant, so "the latest rate at or before X" had two answers | integrity | High | R | 5 | A | ➕ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-24 (SPEC-142).** 12 unique indexes added, each scoped to what is actually a mistake and each asserted in both directions. `customers.duplicate_phone_approved` lets canon 05's rule and its exception both hold, and records that the exception was taken. `passengers.passport_number` deliberately left unconstrained — the same traveller can legitimately appear under a corporate and a personal account; detection via `app.find_customer_duplicates` is the right tool there | — | foundation-programme-2026-08-24 | 08-24 | 08-24 |

| PAR-2 | **`scripts/verify_storage_end_to_end.ps1` permanently rewrote `app.document_retention_days` on every run.** It overrides the function to exercise retention, then "restored" it by RETYPING an equivalent one-liner (`as 'select null::integer'`) instead of putting back the migration's `$fn$…$fn$` body. Behaviour identical; TEXT different — and the parity guard compares `pg_get_functiondef`. So every run left local permanently unequal to the repository, in exactly one function. pgTAP does the same override but ROLLS BACK, which is why only the HTTP suite persisted it | verification integrity | **High** | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29. This is the ROOT CAUSE that PAR-1, PAR-1a and PAR-1b were all circling** — three sessions chased this one function, and PAR-1b concluded local "had been hand-modified mid-session": close, but wrong in the way that mattered. It was not a hand edit; it was this script, deterministically, every run — which is why the drift kept returning after each diagnosis. One of those sessions then read the polluted local body and pushed it to Primary. Fixed by capturing `pg_get_functiondef` BEFORE the override and replaying it verbatim, so the restore cannot drift from the migration however the migration changes. **Proven:** after `db reset` the surface hash is `d98abbdd…`; after the fixed suite it is still `d98abbdd…`, matching Primary read live. Two assertions added (43 → 45): the definition is restored verbatim, and the migration's `revoke execute … from public` survived the replace. **Sibling sweep:** all six HTTP suites checked for persistent DDL — only this one had any | — | codex-audit-reconciliation-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| AUD-01 | **Future-dated current-state evidence.** An entire reconciliation was stamped ONE DAY AHEAD across 13 files and 43 places — gap-register rows, a `GOVERNANCE.md` version bump, a session report's filename and `Date:` field — while the commit carrying them was authored on the real date | verification integrity | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29, and the defect was mine.** The date had been ASSUMED from the shape of the session rather than read from the clock, and no mechanism checked it. A future-dated record is worse than a stale one: it claims evidence that could not yet have been gathered, and it sorts ahead of records that are genuinely newer. All 43 stamps corrected; the session report renamed. **Check 12** now fails the build on any date later than today anywhere in the repository, and cross-checks the clock against the newest commit's author date. **No exemption list, deliberately** — the check flagged its own explanatory comment on the first run because that comment quoted the offending date; the fix was to describe the date rather than quote it, since every exemption is a place the next future date can hide | — | codex-audit-reconciliation-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |

Exit 1 = drift, unreachable, or unproven") *and* `AGENTS.md §4` Stage B step 8b, which already stated that without `-PrimaryFingerprint` "Primary is reported UNPROVEN, which is not CLEAN". The governance was right; the script did not honour it, so the script changed. Three distinct outcomes now, because "not measured" is not a kind of "passed": **exit 0 CLEAN** (everything claimed was measured), **exit 1 DRIFT** (measured and disagreed), **exit 2 UNPROVEN** (Primary never contacted). The graph now declares itself the map of the **Batch 0–5 structural chains**, where a wrong order means an expensive migration rather than a rescheduled package. **`repository-index.md` was checked in the same pass and is a FALSE POSITIVE** — regenerating it produced a byte-identical file | — | codex-audit-reconciliation-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |

Gutting that narrative would destroy the engineering history the programme depends on for continuity. **The concrete harm of duplication is status disagreement**, and that is exactly what AUD-04's cross-Master pass now measures, so the boundary is enforced where it matters instead of by prose discipline. `GOVERNANCE.md §2` amended to state the boundary explicitly rather than leaving it to be inferred | — | codex-audit-reconciliation-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |

| DOC-LC-1 | **Canon 26's Document Lifecycle State Machine was never wired.** `app.status_transitions` held **zero** rows for `documents`, and `app.enforce_archive_authority` — the guard attached to that table — returns early unless `is_archived` changes. It watches the BOOLEAN; `lifecycle_status_code` was governed by nothing but `enforce_catalog_codes`, which asks whether a code EXISTS, never whether the MOVE is legal | authorization / lifecycle | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29 (`202607057200`). REPRODUCED FIRST, with the control that makes it conclusive:** a `trainee` holding `ARCHIVE_DOCUMENT = false`, who could SEE a colleague's document (RLS gives the department queue — so the denial is not vacuous), was refused by `app.archive_document` and **succeeded by direct DML in the same transaction**, leaving `lifecycle=archived / is_archived=false`. Same shape as FIN-6 one domain over: a guard present, working, watching the wrong column. **Impact is not cosmetic** — BOTH document write paths read `lifecycle_status_code='archived'` as a refusal condition, so `add_document_version` AND `archive_document` both refuse afterwards: any tenant user could **permanently freeze a colleague's passport**, in an agency whose commonest way to lose a departure is an expired passport. Fixed by registering the two transitions ORVION actually performs and attaching `enforce_status_transition`. `archive_document` already charged ARCHIVE_DOCUMENT, so **no role gained or lost anything** — the assertion that matters most (a guard stopping the manager too would pass every denial while breaking the business). Also closes `archived → active`, which canon lists nowhere. Guard: `69_document_lifecycle_test.sql` (19) + 6 HTTP assertions incl. the real attack shape, `PATCH /rest/v1/documents` | — | doc-lifecycle-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| DOC-LC-2 | **Canon 26 defines `active → superseded`; ORVION produces no such state.** Every writer of `documents.lifecycle_status_code` was checked (`add_document_version`, `archive_document`, `expiring_documents`, `financial_documents`, `upload_document`, `upload_subscription_payment_proof`) — **not one sets `superseded`**. `add_document_version` moves `current_version_id` and flips `is_current` on the VERSION rows; the DOCUMENT stays `active` | canon vs implementation | Low | R | 6 | — | 📋 | **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 (census pass 2) — a VERSION supersedes, a DOCUMENT does not; the implementation is right and canon 26 is wrong. Canon amendment recommended; no code change either way. Superseded text:** **BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION (canon amendment or implementation).** Not an oversight: SPEC-110 recorded the divergence as an Engineering Observation in Phase 7 and canon 32's Phase 7 entry still carries it. The `document_superseded` EVENT *is* produced — by `documents_emit_superseded`, keyed on `current_version_id` changing — so in the implemented design "supersede" is an **event about the version pointer**, never a document-level status. DOC-LC-1 therefore deliberately did **not** register the transition: doing so would let direct DML move a document into a state nothing writes and nothing reads, inventing a capability to satisfy a document. Pinned by assertions 3 and 5 of `69_document_lifecycle_test.sql`, the second measuring the absence of a producer rather than asserting it | owner: does a DOCUMENT supersede, or only a version? Amend canon 26, or build the explicit op SPEC-110 reserved | doc-lifecycle-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| DOC-LC-3 | **`documents` carries TWO representations of one concept** — `lifecycle_status_code` and the older `is_archived` boolean — with no synchronization. An **authorized** ARCHIVE_DOCUMENT holder can move the boolean alone, producing `archived/false`: a document nothing can re-version (both write paths read the STATUS) while everything reading the BOOLEAN reports it as not archived | data integrity / duplicate model | Medium | R | 6 | M | 📋 | **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 (owner-decision resolution review) — ENGINEERING. There is no canonical contradiction: the two authorities govern DIFFERENT COLUMNS. Canon 26 governs `lifecycle_status_code` (active/superseded/archived, no path back — settled). `app.enforce_archive_authority` governs the generic `is_archived` boolean, which exists on THIRTEEN tables with the same trigger, and says only that RESTORING COSTS THE SAME PERMISSION AS ARCHIVING — a statement about authority, never about the document lifecycle. The fix is therefore a one-directional implication, `lifecycle_status_code = 'archived'` IMPLIES `is_archived = true`, which removes NEITHER authority: canon 26 keeps its no-un-archive rule, and the boolean stays bidirectional on all 13 tables and on documents whose lifecycle is still `active`. Pure integrity; no business input. Engineering task, scheduled. Superseded text: BLOCKED — CANONICAL CONTRADICTION.** Reproduced live after DOC-LC-1 shipped. No *unauthorized* path splits them any more — both columns now cost ARCHIVE_DOCUMENT — so this is an integrity defect, not an escalation. Not fixed here because the fix requires deciding whether un-archiving exists at all, and **two authorities disagree**: canon 26 lists no transition back into `active`, while `app.enforce_archive_authority` states in terms that "restoring is the same authority as archiving". Synchronizing the fields in either direction deletes one of those positions, which is a business/canon call, not an engineering one. Pinned by assertions 18–19 of `69_document_lifecycle_test.sql` so the behaviour cannot change unnoticed while the contradiction is open | owner: does un-archiving exist? Then collapse the two fields to one, or make them move together | doc-lifecycle-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |

| FIN-8 | **The double-entry invariant lived in ONE function.** `app.create_journal_entry` enforced "at least two lines, exactly one of debit/credit per line, debits = credits, total > 0" — and nothing else did. `authenticated` holds INSERT and UPDATE on `journal_entries` and `journal_entry_lines`; the RLS policies charge `CREATE_JOURNAL_ENTRY`, the same permission the RPC charges | financial integrity | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29 (`202607057300`). REPRODUCED as a `finance_manager` (aal2):** the RPC refused an unbalanced entry and a direct INSERT of ONE line carrying a 1,000,000 debit and no credit succeeded in the same transaction, emitting no event. Never a privilege escalation — a holder of the right permission reaching the right table through a door with no invariant behind it. `journal_entry_lines_debit_xor_credit_check` is PER-ROW and structurally cannot express a statement about a SET of rows. Fixed with `deferrable initially deferred` CONSTRAINT triggers on both tables, checking at COMMIT — the only moment the invariant is meaningful, since the entry is momentarily an entry with zero lines. The three rules are **copied from the RPC**, not chosen. **No session-less exemption, deliberately**: this is data integrity, not authorization, so unlike `enforce_status_transition` the platform does not escape it either. Consequence stated plainly: creating an entry through the PostgREST TABLE endpoints becomes impossible — there was never a way to build a *valid* one that way, only an invalid one. Guard: `70_journal_entry_balance_test.sql` (17) incl. a non-vacuous-harness control, + 8 HTTP assertions | — | api-3-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| FIN-9 | A **transaction-capable** direct-DML client (psql, `service_role`) can still create a VALID balanced journal entry with **no `journal_entry_created` event**, because the event is emitted by the RPC and not by the table | audit completeness | Low | R | 6 | A | 📋 | **OPEN — recorded, not papered over.** Post-FIN-8 the entry must at least be *correct*, and the PostgREST table path can no longer create one at all (one request, one transaction, cannot hold an entry plus two balanced lines), so the residue is a transactional client only. **Not fixed by adding an event trigger**: it would double-emit for every RPC-created entry, and a duplicated audit record is its own defect. The honest fix is either an idempotent emitter or moving emission to the table — both are wider than this package. Same class as **EVT-2** | engineering: where journal event emission belongs | api-3-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| CUST-1 | **`app.merge_customer_identity` re-pointed NOTHING.** It archived the source, wrote its audit row, emitted a CRITICAL `customer_identity_merged` event and returned the target id — while every booking, invoice, payment, lead, quotation, passenger, note, conversation and complaint stayed attached to the now-archived duplicate | data integrity / CRM core | **High** | R | 6 | M | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29 (`202607057400`). A REGRESSION INTRODUCED BY AN EARLIER FIX.** The re-pointing loop took the local column as the FIRST column of each foreign key — correct when written, because the FKs were single-column `customer_id`. **TENANT-1 (SPEC-128, 2026-08-21) made every tenant-scoped FK composite** `(tenant_id, customer_id)` to close a cross-tenant hole, so from that moment the loop generated `update public.customer_notes set tenant_id = <a CUSTOMER id> where tenant_id = <another CUSTOMER id>` — matching zero rows on all sixteen referrers. **Silent since 2026-08-21, with a convincing audit trail.** REPRODUCED as `owner`: a note on the source was still on the source afterwards. Fixed by pairing `conkey` with `confkey` by ordinal position and taking the column whose partner is `customers.id`, plus a tenant predicate (the function is SECURITY DEFINER and RLS-blind) and **fail-closed** if a referrer cannot be resolved. **The fix also had to solve the collision it exposes** — `customer_contact_methods` has unique indexes on `(…, customer_id, type, value)` and `(…, customer_id, type) WHERE is_primary`, and two customers being merged are duplicates *of each other*, so re-pointing alone raises on the most ordinary merge there is. Resolved without inventing policy: a source value the target already holds is deleted (not lost — it is on the survivor), and a colliding source PRIMARY is **demoted, not discarded**, because the target is the identity that survives. Guard: `71_customer_identity_merge_test.sql` (19) + 8 HTTP assertions | — | api-3-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| CUST-2 | **The guard that existed could not have caught CUST-1.** The only tests naming `merge_customer_identity` were `07_event_vocabulary_registry_test` (its event code is registered) and `53_api_surface_test` (the endpoint exists). AUDIT-1/SPEC-120 had already broken this function once — an unregistered event code aborted every call — and the guard added in response verified the **vocabulary**, not the **behaviour** | guard integrity | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29 by `71_customer_identity_merge_test.sql`**, whose assertion 12 is the behavioural test that was missing: the source's note must be ON THE TARGET after the merge. **The lesson generalises and is worth more than the instance:** a guard on the *name of an event a function emits* cannot notice that the function did nothing else. When a fix is prompted by a symptom (a bad event code), the guard tends to be written against the symptom rather than the capability. Assertions 1–3 also pin the ROOT CAUSE conditions — that the FKs are composite, and that the function no longer reads the first key column — so the regression cannot silently return by the same route | — | api-3-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |

| FIN-10 | **The second instance of FIN-8's class, found by looking for it rather than tripping over it.** `app.record_payment` refuses to allocate more to an invoice than it is worth — and takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the invoice first, so the author knew it was a statement about a SET of rows. Nothing enforced it on any other path | financial integrity | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29 (`202607057500`). REPRODUCED** as a `finance_manager` holding RECORD_PAYMENT — the same permission the RPC charges: invoice total 1000, RPC allocated 400, RPC then REFUSED 900, and a direct INSERT of a 900 allocation succeeded — **1300 allocated against a 1000 invoice, still reporting itself `partially_paid`**. `reporting.customer_outstanding` derives `paid_amount` and `outstanding_balance` from this data, so the customer's balance is wrong in the direction that matters commercially. `payment_allocations` already has `CHECK (allocated_amount >= 0)` — per-row, structurally unable to say anything about a SUM across two tables. Fixed with deferred constraint triggers on **both sides of the inequality**: allocations can exceed the total by growing, or by the total SHRINKING beneath them (`invoices.total_amount` is writable by a CREATE_INVOICE holder), and a trigger on the allocations alone would have left the second route open. Guard: `72_invoice_allocation_ceiling_test.sql` (16), incl. a non-vacuous-harness control | — | api-3-sec1-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| QUO-1 | **A quotation's headline price and its line items could disagree by any amount.** `app.add_quotation_item` recomputes `quotations.total_amount` from the items on every insert — so the total is DEFINED as that sum — and maintains it on that one path only, while `quotation_items` is directly writable by all six CREATE_QUOTATION roles including ordinary `employee` | financial integrity / correctness | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29 (`202607057600`). The third instance of the class, and the first that is a DERIVED VALUE rather than a refusal.** REPRODUCED as an employee: RPC added a 1000 item (total 1000); a direct INSERT of a 5000 item left **total=1000 / items=6000**; a direct UPDATE left **total=1000 / items=5001**. A quotation is a price offered to a customer — an underquote the agency may have to honour, or an overquote that loses the sale — and `advance_quotation` reads `total_amount` when the quotation is sent and accepted, so the wrong number is the one that travels into the booking. **RECOMPUTED rather than refused**, which is where this differs from FIN-8/FIN-10: those guard invariants where rejecting the write is the only correct answer; this is a derived value with no independent source (`quotations` has no discount, override or adjustment column — asserted, not assumed), so refusing a legitimate line to protect a number the database can simply compute would be the larger change, not the safer one. Guard: `73_quotation_total_derivation_test.sql` (11) | — | api-3-sec1-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| FIN-11 | Direct-DML allocation leaves `invoices.status_code` unchanged (the RPC advances it to `partially_paid`/`paid`) and emits no event | audit / side-effect completeness | Low | R | 6 | A | 📋 | **OPEN — recorded, not assumed away.** Same shape as **FIN-9** one table over. Post-FIN-10 the resulting state must at least be *correct* (allocations can no longer exceed the total), so the residue is a missing side effect rather than a wrong number. Deliberately not fixed with a status trigger here: `record_payment` already sets the status, so a trigger would have to be idempotent with it — and FIN-9 asks the identical question about journal events. The two want ONE answer about where side effects belong, not two bolted-on producers | engineering: where invoice status and event emission belong | api-3-sec1-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |
| GOV-7 | **The consumer-impact rule, made durable.** CUST-1 proved that a structurally correct migration can silently break a consumer that derived behaviour from the old shape, and that no guard connected the two | governance / guard integrity | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-29.** Governed by extending `AGENTS.md §3 5b` — the existing cross-path impact sweep — with a **second question**: *which code CONSUMES, PARSES or DERIVES FROM the structure this package changed?* Question 1 asks what the new rule now catches; question 2 asks what the new SHAPE now breaks, and CUST-1 proved they are not the same. **Guarded rather than merely written down, and deliberately narrowly:** the consumers that can change meaning WITHOUT THEIR OWN SOURCE CHANGING are those whose behaviour is derived from the catalog. Measured live — ORVION has **exactly one** (`merge_customer_identity`) — so `71_customer_identity_merge_test.sql` assertion 20 pins that set by name. A second catalog-driven function cannot arrive without failing an assertion that tells its author to bring a BEHAVIOURAL test, which is exactly what was missing. **No new governance document was created** | — | api-3-sec1-2026-08-29 | 08-29 | 08-29 |

| PAR-3 | **The parity guard compared 233 objects out of 3,341, and was blind to every object type the last four packages actually shipped.** `check_database_parity.ps1` proved the migration ledger and the FUNCTION surface. Triggers, RLS policies, RLS *enablement*, grants, constraints, columns, views, indexes and `app.status_transitions` rows were never compared between local and Primary | verification integrity | **High** | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30. REPRODUCED as a controlled mutation on a clean local reset** (never on Primary): dropping `payment_allocations_within_invoice_total` — FIN-10's financial ceiling, a High-severity fix shipped the day before — left **every gate reporting success**: `check_repository_consistency.ps1` CLEAN exit 0, `check_database_parity.ps1` **CLEAN exit 0**, `MASTER_API_CONTRACT.md` "matches the live surface", `verify_database.sql` ALL CHECKS PASSED. Only `72_invoice_allocation_ceiling_test.sql` failed — and pgTAP runs against LOCAL only, so nothing at all could have detected the same drift on Primary. Fixed with **Check L4/P4** over ten surfaces, the SQL held in **`scripts/parity_surface.sql` which BOTH SIDES RUN** — not two hand-copied queries, which is the PAR-1a mistake one layer up. `-PrimaryStructureHash` joins the other two in what UNPROVEN requires (AUD-05's rule), so an unmeasured value cannot quietly count as a passed one. **Then the fix was attacked and found incomplete:** `pg_policies` lists a policy whether or not row security is ON, so `ALTER TABLE public.invoices DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` removed the entire tenant boundary while leaving the combined hash **byte-identical** — `relrowsecurity`/`relforcerowsecurity` added as a tenth surface. `ordinal_position` added to columns because CUST-1 was a positional assumption. **Proven both directions:** mutated → exit 1 naming triggers and constraints; `db reset` → exit 0. Local = Primary at `4bca45c73e058581190505ac878688fc`, **3,341 objects**, read independently from both | — | verification-methodology-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |
| PAR-4 | **Three set-level invariant tests proved the violation was refused, but never that the NAMED trigger is what refused it.** A test that passes for the wrong reason stops failing the moment the real enforcer is removed | verification integrity | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30.** The direct consequence of PAR-3: since pgTAP was the ONLY layer that noticed FIN-10's trigger vanish, it is worth knowing exactly which assertion does the noticing. Tests **70** (FIN-8), **72** (FIN-10) and **73** (QUO-1) each gained a mutation pair — drop the named trigger inside a SAVEPOINT, assert the violation now **SUCCEEDS**, roll back, assert it is **refused again**. One half without the other proves half of it. This is standard mutation testing ("defect injection") applied to the enforcer rather than to the code, and it costs no new file, no new script and no new authority. Suite **899 → 905**. Two initial failures were themselves informative and are recorded rather than tidied away: test 70's probe died on TENANT-1's composite FK (23503) and test 72's on 42501, because each ran under the session a PREVIOUS assertion had left behind — the exact "denied" vs "never attempted" confusion `AGENTS.md §6` warns about, met in practice | — | verification-methodology-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |
| VER-1 | **The API contract's `http` column claimed execution and measured source text.** "whether a suite actually calls the endpoint over the wire" was derived by regex over `verify_*.ps1` files | documentation accuracy | Low | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30, and deliberately with the SMALLER fix.** Audited first rather than assumed: all 46 endpoints then marked covered do have a live invocation, so there was **no current false positive**. The one concrete way to manufacture one is a COMMENTED-OUT call, so comment lines are now stripped before matching (count stayed 46, proving the change non-destructive). The contract now states `http` as a **REPOSITORY fact, not an execution fact**, and §6 records that it describes ONE database — the local one it was generated from — and is not evidence about Primary. **A runtime-emitted coverage file was considered and REJECTED:** it would be stale whenever the suites had not just run, trading a theoretical false positive for a guaranteed one | — | verification-methodology-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |
| VER-2 | Mechanical vacuous-test detection was designed, measured against the suite, and **REJECTED on evidence** | verification integrity | Low | R | 6 | — | 📋 | **INTENTIONAL — not built, and the measurement is why.** `AGENTS.md §6` bans vacuous security tests in prose; the question was whether a guard could enforce it. The demonstrated shape (a `lives_ok`/`throws_ok` wrapping `INSERT … SELECT`, which silently inserts zero rows) occurs **31 times across 15 of 73 files**, nearly all legitimate — a detector flagging those would cry wolf, which the repository guard's own header forbids. A tightened variant requiring a `count()` control flagged exactly one file, `62_action_attribution_test.sql`, and inspection showed a **false positive**: its insert is controlled by a scalar `is()` on the inserted row, which is an equally strong control. What actually distinguishes a vacuous test — whether the acting role can SEE the rows being selected — is semantic and not statically decidable. **PAR-4's mutation pattern is the mechanism that does work**, because it observes behaviour instead of matching shapes | — | verification-methodology-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |

| BOOK-1 | **A closed booking could earn new revenue, and unaudited.** `app.create_booking_item` refuses to attach an item to an archived, completed or cancelled booking, and `app.link_passenger_to_booking_item` refuses a passenger on such a booking (or on an item that is itself cancelled/no_show/archived). Each rule lived in exactly one function; `booking_items` carries nine triggers and not one is a capability guard | financial integrity / audit | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30 (`202607057700`). Found behind endpoints that ALREADY had HTTP evidence** — the case for auditing capability rather than status codes. REPRODUCED as an ordinary **employee holding CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM, ENTER_COST and ENTER_SELLING_PRICE**, the same permissions the RPC charges, against a booking cancelled through the legal path: RPC refused, direct INSERT **succeeded** with selling 5000 / cost 3000, gross 2000, and `commission_rate` **0.10 derived automatically** by `booking_items_derive_commission_rate`. `gross_profit = selling - cost` and `commission = max(gross,0) x 10%` are ratified, and nothing derives the BOOKING'S state into that calculation — so a trip that never happened produced 200 of commission while the booking beside it read `cancelled`. `booking_item_created` is emitted by the RPC, so the direct path was also **unaudited**. Why the guards missed it: the RLS `WITH CHECK` requires the PARENT TO BE VISIBLE, not the actor permitted, and `guard_booking_item_financials` charges its permissions only when amounts are non-zero, checking scope against `new.sales_owner_user_id` — **a value the caller supplies**. Fixed with BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE triggers carrying the RPCs' own rules verbatim; **no session-less exemption** (integrity, not authorization) and SECURITY DEFINER because under INVOKER the check's own SELECT would be RLS-filtered, making the guard weakest against exactly the caller it must stop. **NOT the aggregate-across-rows subclass** — SEC-1's clause-3 filter would not have found this; the rule spans TABLES, which is why that filter is a lead and not a sieve. Guard: `74_booking_item_lifecycle_test.sql` (16, incl. PAR-4's load-bearing pair) + 8 HTTP assertions | — | api-3-booking-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |
| BOOK-2 | `booking_item_passengers.selling_amount_override` / `cost_amount_override` are written by `link_passenger_to_booking_item` and **read by nothing** | data model / financial lineage | Low | R | 6 | A | 📋 | **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 (owner-decision resolution review) — CANON ALREADY DEFINES IT; it was never an owner decision, and canon was not exhausted before escalating (AGENTS.md §6). `31_schema_draft.md`, the schema draft that DEFINES this table, states the rule in terms: ‘selling_amount_override and cost_amount_override, when populated, represent this passenger's individual price/cost WITHIN THE SHARED booking_item. When null, the passenger's share is treated as an EVEN SPLIT of the parent booking_item's selling_amount/cost_amount.’ So the overrides are a DECOMPOSITION of the item total with an even-split default — not independent prices — and the shares partition the item amount. This matches universal GDS/PNR practice, where fares are quoted per passenger and the booking total is their sum. The derivable invariant (sum of shares = item amount, share = override or even split) is INV-1..4's class. Engineering task, scheduled. Superseded text: OPEN — recorded, deliberately NOT invented into a rule.** Measured against `pg_proc` and `pg_views`: no reporting view, no profit function and no commission path consumes either column. So there is currently no aggregate-across-rows invariant to enforce — whether the per-passenger overrides must SUM to the item's amounts, or are independent per-passenger prices, is a genuine business question that cannot be derived from canon or from any consumer. Manufacturing a sum rule here would have been inventing policy to make a finding, which is the mirror of inventing one to shrink a backlog. Needs either an owner decision or the first consumer, whichever arrives first | **owner: what do the per-passenger overrides MEAN relative to the item total?** | api-3-booking-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |

| IDENT-1 | **An unverified email was the only proof of identity required to claim a tenant membership.** `app.activate_membership()` links a pre-provisioned `public.users` row to the caller by matching `auth.users.email`, justified in its own comment: *“the caller’s auth.users row exists only after Supabase verified this email, so the match is an authorization proof”* | **security / account takeover** | **Critical** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30 (`202607057800`). THAT COMMENT IS FALSE AGAINST THIS REPOSITORY’S OWN CONFIG.** `supabase/config.toml` sets `enable_confirmations = false`, so a GoTrue signup creates the `auth.users` row IMMEDIATELY with `email_confirmed_at` NULL — and that column appeared **nowhere in any migration, test or script**. REPRODUCED on a clean local reset: a tenant pre-provisions its CEO (`create_tenant_user` accepts a null `p_auth_user_id` and logs `has_auth_link:false`, so unlinked rows are an intended flow); an attacker signs up with the same email string, never confirms, calls the RPC — and **claims the CEO membership, then holds APPROVE_FINANCE, VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS and MANAGE_USERS**, with `current_tenant_id()` resolving to the victim tenant. **Fixed IN THE FUNCTION, NOT A TRIGGER, and the layer was chosen on measurement:** (1) the alternate paths are already closed — before claiming, the attacker has no membership, so RLS hides every row: a direct `UPDATE users SET auth_user_id` affected **0 rows** and a direct INSERT was refused **42501**, making the RPC the only reachable path; (2) **Q2 — 49 test files create `auth.users` and NOT ONE sets `email_confirmed_at`**, so a trigger would break all 49 and would also impose confirmation on the ADMIN provisioning path, which rests on a different trust basis. `banned_until`/`deleted_at` refused in the same breath, not speculatively: **a JWT issued before a ban stays valid until it expires**. `email_confirmed_at` deliberately, not the generated `confirmed_at` (= least(email,phone)), which a phone-confirmed identity would satisfy without owning the mailbox. Guard: `75_human_identity_family_test.sql` (24) + 15 HTTP | — | api-3-identity-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |

Found by continuing after the first fix looked correct.** `users_tenant_email_key` is `UNIQUE (tenant_id, email)`; the match is `lower(email) = lower(...)`. REPRODUCED: a tenant legally holding both `ceo@case.test` and `CEO@case.test` — legal precisely because the constraint sees two people — makes the claim target BOTH rows, and `users_tenant_auth_key` then refuses with a raw **23505**. The invitee is **permanently unable to onboard**, cannot fix it themselves, and sees an error naming neither cause nor remedy. It fails closed, so availability rather than security. **Fixed at the CONSTRAINT layer, not in the function:** making the claim “deterministic” would paper over the real fault, that two rows for one human existed at all — an email is one identity, and the ambiguity leaks into every later question (whose lead, whose commission, which one do we email). A `unique index (tenant_id, lower(email))` moves the failure from claim time (unrecoverable) to provisioning time (actionable). **Cross-path sweep:** the old constraint is referenced nowhere but its own DDL and two prose comments — grepped, not assumed — and a live check found **zero** existing case-variant groups. The old constraint is deliberately KEPT: the new index subsumes it, and dropping a named constraint is consumer risk for no gain. **One of the two false comments was in this session’s own new migration**, inherited verbatim before it was tested | — | api-3-identity-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |

| CM-1 | `app.add_customer_contact_method` demoted "primary" across ALL channels while the index encodes one primary PER channel | correctness | Medium | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30 (`202607058300`).** The rule is written down in `202607052100` where the index is created — *"Two primary PHONES for one customer ... is the same class of defect"* — and encoded as `(tenant_id, customer_id, contact_method_type_code) WHERE is_primary`: one primary per channel. REPRODUCED as an `employee` holding CREATE_CUSTOMER: adding a primary EMAIL silently set the customer's primary PHONE to `is_primary = false`. `app.merge_customer_identity` reads `is_primary` when deciding which methods survive a merge, so the flag is consumed. Fixed in the function — its own UPDATE is what was over-broad; nothing else sets `is_primary = true`, and the index already constrains the direct path correctly, per channel | — | api-3-customer-data-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |
| PLACE-2 | `app.current_placement()` does not test `starts_at <= now()`, so a not-yet-started placement is returned | correctness | Low | O | 6 | — | 📋 | **UNPROVEN — recorded, deliberately not fixed.** `app.eligible_lead_handlers` tests `starts_at <= now()`; `current_placement` does not, so the two diverge on future-dated placements. **No harm was reproduced**, and `app.assign_user_branch` defaults `starts_at` to `now()`, so a future-dated placement is reachable only by direct DML. Adding the condition would CHANGE existing behaviour in a case no evidence showed to be harmful, which is why PLACE-1's fix was kept strictly additive. Trigger to revisit: a reproduced wrong branch stamp, or a scheduled-placement feature | — | api-3-customer-data-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |
| LIC-2 | **The single-use licence activation code could be redeemed TWICE, concurrently** | security / correctness | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30 (`202607058400`).** `app.redeem_license_token` read the row, checked `consumed_at is null`, then updated `where id = v_row.id` — a check-then-act with no guard on the act. Under READ COMMITTED the second redeemer blocks on the row lock and, when it unblocks, PostgreSQL re-evaluates only `id = ...`, which still matches. **What makes it a defect rather than a theoretical race is that the repository states the opposite twice**: the function comment (*"replay is closed by consumed_at regardless of auditing"*) and `43_license_activation_test.sql` assertion 11 (*"the SAME code cannot be used twice"*). Both true SEQUENTIALLY, false CONCURRENTLY — LESSON 4. REPRODUCED with two live psql sessions on a committed fixture: both returned success; `security_events` = **2** `license_token_redeemed` rows for ONE token, one consumed row, and the subscription activated twice. The evidence is not merely incomplete but internally inconsistent — the row says consumed once, the spine says twice. Closed with a compare-and-swap (`and consumed_at is null`, raising on `not found`) using the SAME generic message, because "you lost the race" would be as much of an oracle as "already used". **The function is the COMPLETE enforcement layer here, and that was measured, not assumed:** `authenticated` holds NO grant on `tenant_license_activations` and its RLS is `platform_only`, so unlike BOOK-1/ASGN-1/CM-2 there is no second door to guard. Re-ran the identical experiment after the fix: one session succeeds, the other raises, `security_events` = 1 | — | api-3-subscription-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |
| LIC-3 | **The only way back from `read_only` was unavailable on the plan most likely to need it** | correctness / canon-09 | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30 (`202607058500`).** `app.upload_subscription_payment_proof` charges `MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS` (no feature gate) but INSERTs into `documents` and `document_versions`, whose guards charge `UPLOAD_DOCUMENT` and `CREATE_DOCUMENT_VERSION` — and **both carry `required_feature_code = documents`, which `feature_entitlements` sets FALSE for `starter`.** So the entry-level plan could not file the proof that pays for the plan, while canon 09/28 (via this register's DOC-2 row) call that upload *"the only way back from read_only"*. WP-04-B narrowed the SUBSCRIPTION gate for exactly this case; the PLAN gate was never considered. REPRODUCED with a discriminating experiment — two tenants identical but for the plan, same `owner` role, same aal2 claim, same call: professional `has_permission(UPLOAD_DOCUMENT)`=TRUE and the upload SUCCEEDS; starter FALSE and it is REFUSED, while `MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS`=TRUE on both. The plan is the only variable. Fixed by charging `MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS` for `payment_proof` documents in both guards — the permission the RPC already charges, and the only one not gated on the entitlement the tenant is trying to restore: **paying for your plan cannot itself be a plan feature.** The `starter` entitlement was deliberately NOT changed — what a plan includes is a commercial decision belonging to the owner | — | api-3-subscription-2026-08-30 | 08-30 | 08-30 |

REPRODUCED: a plain `employee` on a professional plan, holding **no** MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS, INSERTed a `documents` row with `document_type_code = payment_proof` marked confidential — `INSERT 0 1`. The RPC requires MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS; the table charged only UPLOAD_DOCUMENT, which every ordinary role holds. Harm is SPP-2's: a fabricated proof planted in the tenant's confidential set, which a Platform Owner reviewing renewals could be misled by. | CAP-1 | `app.tenant_capabilities` is the only one of eight subscription readers that does not apply the "latest row wins" rule | consistency | Low | O | 6 | — | 📋 | **NOT REPRODUCIBLE TODAY — recorded, deliberately not fixed.** `plan_allows`, `plan_limit`, `subscription_allows_write`, `enforce_subscription_write_gate`, `platform_activate_subscription`, `upload_subscription_payment_proof` and `process_subscription_lifecycle` all order by `created_at desc limit 1`; `tenant_capabilities` joins `subscriptions` with no ordering at all, so a tenant with two rows would receive duplicated and possibly conflicting capabilities. **Measured before recording:** `app.provision_tenant` is the ONLY inserter, `platform_activate_subscription` UPDATEs rather than inserts, and **no role holds MANAGE_SUBSCRIPTION**, so a second row is unreachable through any shipped path and no harm could be reproduced. | TASK-1 | **An ordinary employee could reassign a task by direct DML, and unaudited** | authorization | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30 (`202607058600`).** `app.assign_task` charges **ASSIGN_TASK** (branch_manager, ceo, department_manager, owner); `public.tasks` is served by PostgREST with UPDATE to `authenticated` and its capability guard charges **CREATE_TASK**, which `employee` and `senior_employee` also hold. **The two are different role sets, which is what makes this a real privilege gap rather than the theoretical one ASSIGN_LEAD/REASSIGN_LEAD turned out to be.** REPRODUCED in one transaction as an ordinary employee: the RPC refused with `permission denied: ASSIGN_TASK` and `update public.tasks set owner_user_id = <manager>` returned **UPDATE 1**, with `task_assigned` events numbering **0** — unauthorized AND unaudited, since the event comes from the RPC. | SUP-1 | **Three of `link_internal_supplier`'s rules were absent from its table door** | integrity | Low | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-08-30 (`202607058600`).** Each reproduced as an ASSIGN_SUPPLIER holder whose RPC call had just been refused: (a) a provider department not IN the provider branch — RPC refused, direct INSERT returned **INSERT 0 1**; (b) the requester, derived by the RPC from the item, accepted verbatim by the table (a link naming Giza as requester for a Cairo item); (c) a link on a legally-CANCELLED booking item — RPC refused, direct INSERT succeeded. **Severity is Low and the reason is MEASURED: `public.internal_supplier_links` has NO consumer** — nothing reads it but its own RPC and `guard_write_capability`; no view, no reporting surface. So the confidentiality boundary IS enforced and the endpoint's guard is not a privacy boundary for non-confidential financial documents. **What this adds to SPEC-154-B:** the decision is not only "who may hold the permission" but "should the RLS branch exclude financial TYPES regardless of the confidential flag" — and canon 28 states BOTH *"assigned employee may view financial documents directly related to their lead/booking"* AND *"financial documents require stricter visibility"*, which the binary permission cannot express. With the mutation assertion last, every `ok` line is still emitted — `pg_prove` counts them and reports PASS — while `finish()` prints `Looks like you planned N tests but ran N-1`. Found in **test 80 from a previous session**, where it had been shipping unnoticed, and reproduced in test 82 while writing it. **Both files were also missing PAR-4's closing move:** they proved the guard could be removed and never proved it came back. One fix serves both — re-assert after the rollback, which restores the count AND completes the pattern. Class: a warning the harness printed and a green summary hid, which is the MEAS-1 family applied to the test harness itself | — | session-2026-08-31-spec-154b-and-decision-memory | 08-31 | 08-31 |

Two CHECKs — the narrowest layer, since each is decidable from the row alone. Bounds copied from the RPC rather than chosen: `>= 0` for price because a zero-price line is real (an included transfer, a waived fee), `> 0` for quantity because a zero-quantity line is not a line.
