| ATTR-2 | **Eight actor columns are still accepted from the caller — and the detector that found them was still shaped like a name** | integrity / attribution | Medium | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-01 (`202607059300`) — and the count was wrong in BOTH directions.** Of the eight, **four were engineering-owned and are closed**: `payments.received_by`, `customer_identity_merges.merged_by`, `booking_items.cancelled_by` and `booking_items.no_show_recorded_by`. **Two more were never in the list at all**, because the predicate that produced it asks for columns ending `_by` and these do not: **`lead_interactions.user_id`** (who made the call) and **`customers.first_registered_user_id`** (who first took the customer on). Both were reproduced and both are closed. **And the remaining four are not open work of the same kind** — `invoices.voided_by` / `journal_entries.voided_by` are **VOID-1**, an open OWNER decision (voiding is unimplemented, and deriving an attribution for an act nobody can perform would dress a missing capability as a solved one — migration `202607056500` reached this conclusion first and it still holds); `payments.verified_by` is **VERIFY-1**; and `tenant_license_activations` is granted to `postgres` and `service_role` ONLY, so no tenant caller can reach it by any door. **The whole class was decided by measurement, not by the suffix**: every one of the six closed columns has EXACTLY ONE producer, that producer already recorded the caller, and NONE accepts an actor as a parameter — so a direct write naming somebody else was unreachable through every authorized path. **`payments.received_by` in particular was NOT assumed** (this row had flagged it as possibly a legitimate business fact): `app.record_payment` and `app.record_supplier_payment` set `received_by = created_by = v_actor` with no parameter between them, no view or function READS the column, canon 31 says only "received_by nullable", and no permission distinguishes receiving from recording — so "A received the cash, B recorded it" is not expressible in ORVION today, and if it is ever wanted it is a new capability with a parameter and a permission, not a silent default. **Reproduced with live positive AND negative controls, six scenarios**, the sharpest being a finance_manager who genuinely holds RECORD_PAYMENT inserting a payment that recorded the EMPLOYEE as receiver while ATTR-1's trigger corrected `created_by` in the same statement — one column derived, the other accepted verbatim. `guard_financial_capability` watches `amount` and nothing else, so rewriting who received money needed **no capability at all** (`UPDATE 1`). `booking_items` is tied to the ACT rather than merely re-attributed: an employee had stamped a canceller on an item still `confirmed`, and re-attributing that would have traded one false statement for another. Guard: `87_actor_attribution_second_door_test.sql` (29), PAR-4 mutation-tested, plus the cross-path sweep proving all five RPCs still attribute identically. Original text follows, kept because it is what specified the work: **RECORDED AND PINNED, NOT YET FIXED.** Found by widening `83_actor_attribution_test.sql` assertion 22 from a hand-written name list to *every* column ending `_by`: `booking_items.cancelled_by`, `booking_items.no_show_recorded_by`, `customer_identity_merges.merged_by`, `invoices.voided_by`, `journal_entries.voided_by`, `payments.received_by`, `payments.verified_by`, `tenant_license_activations.consumed_by`. **This is the same detector failure twice over:** the first sweep enumerated five names, found ONE gap (FX-2) and looked finished; adding `assigned_by` produced FX-3; adding `reviewed_by` produced FX-4; asking the schema instead produced these eight. Not fixed in this package because each needs its own reproduction and a semantic judgment the others did not — `payments.received_by` may be a legitimate BUSINESS fact (which staff member physically received the cash, recordable on another's behalf) rather than a session actor, and guessing would invent policy. Assertion 22 now pins the inventory and **fails in either direction**, so a new unattributed column fails it and each fix must delete its own line | — | session-2026-08-31-batch6-care | 08-31 | 08-31 |

The ATTR-2 migration was first written with aligned assignments, so the detector would have reported its own fix as absent and the next author would have "fixed" it by un-aligning code: a guard constraining FORMATTING instead of BEHAVIOUR, which is the **MEAS-1** class. The new predicate is structural — every FK to `public.users` on a table `authenticated` can write, with no BEFORE trigger deriving it — so an actor column is caught **whatever it is called**, and its pinned set is a CLASSIFIED inventory rather than an exemption list: a new entry means somebody must classify it | — | session-2026-09-01-attr2 | 09-01 | 09-01 |

Canon DOES define a two-person money workflow — canon 07's *Transfer Proof Approval*: "the responsible employee uploads the transfer receipt… Finance verifies the bank account… Finance approves or rejects" — but ORVION implements it through **`approval_requests`**, whose `requested_by` and `reviewed_by` are already derived by `app.derive_approval_requester` and `app.derive_approval_reviewer`. | PARENT-1 | **Four RPCs refuse a write because of the PARENT row's state, and not one of those rules existed on the table door** | integrity | **Medium** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-01 (`202607059400`), DEPLOYED.** QUO-2 closed one instance of this; the care/conversation slice found it is a CLASS, and the class was **derived from the catalog rather than listed**: for every `app.*` function that reads a parent's registered `app.status_transitions.status_column` and then INSERTs into a DIFFERENT table, does any BEFORE INSERT trigger on that table read the same column? Twelve pairs came back unguarded and **reading the function bodies reduced them to four** — the other eight merely READ the parent state (`record_lead_interaction` reads `lead_status_code` only to decide the `assigned → contacted` transition and refuses nothing on it), which is why static analysis was treated as the lead and the source as the verdict (**MEAS-1**). **All four REPRODUCED live**, each with the RPC as positive control and a caller who genuinely holds the capability, so every refusal is the state rule and not a permission: `create_booking` refused *"only an accepted quotation can produce a booking (status: draft)"* / direct INSERT **0 1** and the booking cited an offer the customer never accepted; `request_finance_approval` refused *"cannot request finance approval on a cancelled/no_show/archived booking item"* / **INSERT 0 1** opened a `pending` request against a cancelled line; `add_document_version` refused *"cannot add a version to an archived document"* / **INSERT 0 1** versioned a closed record; `send_conversation_message` refused *"conversation is closed; reopen it before sending a message"* / **INSERT 0 1** put a message on a finished engagement without even moving the parent's `updated_at`. **ONE function, four triggers, not four copies** — the rule is a single rule with four subjects, and four copies is how TRANS-1's duplication happened. Every field read through `to_jsonb(new) ->> '...'` (the SPEC-159-A / PP-4 record-binding hazard); SECURITY DEFINER so an RLS-filtered parent read cannot weaken it (**BOOK-1**); an unmapped table RAISES; **BEFORE INSERT only, deliberately** — freezing the child to the parent's *later* state would break `review_finance_approval` deciding a request whose item was since cancelled, and an integration reconciling `external_message_id` after closure; no session-less exemption, per **ADR-0025** (integrity, not authorization), matching `guard_quotation_item_parent_editable`. Messages copied **verbatim** from each RPC so the two doors cannot give one caller two accounts of the same rule. **Cross-path sweep:** all six writers of the four tables are SECURITY INVOKER interactive RPCs — no SECURITY DEFINER, batch, scheduled or integration writer exists, and `orvion_integration` holds no grant on any of them. Guard: `88_parent_state_on_every_door_test.sql` (25), PAR-4 mutation-tested, plus **5 HTTP assertions** — the care suite already refused the RPC on a closed thread and already POSTed to the table, and the gap survived because the two were never asked at the same moment. | SEC-1c | **`guard_write_capability` was attached BEFORE INSERT ONLY on thirteen tables — the exact mirror of SEC-1b, in the direction nobody checked** | **security / write capability** | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-01 (`202607059100`), deployed and parity-verified.** REPRODUCED first on a clean local reset at 179 migrations (local ledger `1f64a99ca835e0a54a222944c1aadcf5` = Primary), non-vacuously. SEC-1b found the ceiling counting UPDATE-only triggers and opened twelve tables on INSERT; it never asked the inverse. Measured live: `guard_write_capability` fires `BEFORE INSERT` and **not** `BEFORE UPDATE` on `approval_requests`, `bookings`, `complaints`, `conversations`, `customer_notes`, `customers`, `documents`, `leads`, `passengers`, `quotations`, `service_requests`, `suppliers`, `tasks`. For ten of them RLS `WITH CHECK` still carries a **scope** condition; for **three — `customers`, `passengers`, `suppliers` — the UPDATE `WITH CHECK` is `tenant_id = current_tenant_id()` and nothing else**, so UPDATE is ungoverned beyond tenant isolation. **Reproduced as a `trainee`:** `has_permission('CREATE_CUSTOMER')`=f, `('CREATE_PASSENGER')`=f, `('MANAGE_SUPPLIERS')`=f, `('VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS')`=f; the rows were **visible** first (1 each, so no assertion is vacuous); an INSERT on the same table in the same session was **REFUSED** (`permission denied: one of CREATE_CUSTOMER is required`) — which is what proves the actor is genuinely unprivileged and that the difference is INSERT vs UPDATE rather than the fixture; then `UPDATE 1` on each of **`customers.full_name`**, **`passengers.full_name`**, and **`suppliers.credit_limit_amount` 1000 → 999999**. `suppliers.credit_limit_amount` is the finance-sensitive field whose *visibility* is already an open owner decision, so a trainee can rewrite the exposure ceiling they arguably should not even read. The other ten are **UNPROVEN** — their scope predicate narrows the actor set and each needs its own probe before any claim. **✅ FIXED 2026-09-01 (`202607059100`), owner-approved.** **The permission was DERIVED, not chosen:** canon 28 defines NO general edit permission — repository-wide the only mutation-shaped permissions it names beyond CREATE_* are `EDIT_LOCKED_COST` and `UPDATE_BOOKING_ITEM_STATUS`, both narrow and field-specific — so demanding an EDIT_* that canon never defined would have been inventing policy. Two existing facts settled it instead: **twelve tables already carried this guard as INSERT OR UPDATE with the same object-class permission**, and `app.status_transitions.permission_key` already records which permission may mutate each object. Hence **UPDATE set = object-class permission UNION that table's transition permissions**, and `v_perms` was already a `text[]` evaluated as "any of", so no new mechanism was needed. A blanket CREATE-only rule was **rejected on measured evidence**: finance_manager holds ISSUE/CANCEL/REFUND/REISSUE_BOOKING and NOT CREATE_BOOKING, holds ARCHIVE_DOCUMENT without being a general uploader, and holds APPROVE_FINANCE without CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM — it would have broken `advance_booking`, `archive_document` and FIN-2's `review_finance_approval`. **One authority is a RELATIONSHIP, not a permission, and only HTTP caught it:** `record_lead_interaction` reads *"the assigned handler, OR ASSIGN_LEAD"*, so a trainee assigned a lead may log a call while holding none of the lead permissions; the first draft removed that, pgTAP was **entirely green**, and `verify_lifecycle_branches.ps1` failed — the standing argument for testing both doors. Mirrored verbatim, and it does not reopen the defect because the reproduced trainee was assigned nothing. **Re-scan: the class is closed** — 13 → 0, with the three canon-34 identity tables remaining as SEC-1's declared intentional residue (reaffirmed by PD-10/AUTH-1). Guard: `85_write_capability_on_update_test.sql` (14, incl. both reproducers refused and finance's booking write still permitted) + the drift assertion in test 67 that fails if the guard stops accepting any transition permission canon records | — | owner-decision-review-2026-09-01 | 09-01 | 09-01 |
| MEAS-2 | **Check 11 proved an id was MENTIONED in the register, while reporting that it was DEFINED there** | verification integrity | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-01, and mutation-tested both ways.** Discovered while investigating PP-1. Check 11 tests `$registerRaw -notmatch '\bID\b'` and prints "defines no such finding" on failure — but a match anywhere in 1,000 lines of prose satisfies it, including a cross-reference inside a *different* finding's row. **PP-1 resolves ONLY because DOC-2's row ends "New: **PP-1**, **PP-2**"** — a mention, not a definition: PP-1 has no row, no detail block, no status, no evidence and no decision statement. An independent measurement (first cell of a table row, or a `###` heading, sibling-aware for `SPP-1/SPP-2` and `DC-1/R7` forms) run over all 29 manifest ids returns exactly one non-subject: PP-1. | RBAC-5 | **A capability could be granted to a ROLE and to nothing else — no user-level grant, revoke or deny existed** | authorization architecture | **High** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ REFACTORED 2026-09-02 (`202607059800`), DEPLOYED. Decision recorded as ADR-0027.** **ID CORRECTED 2026-09-04 (GOV-12) — read this before citing an older commit.** This row was minted as **`RBAC-3`** on 2026-09-02 by the session that shipped `202607059800`, and `RBAC-3` was **already in use** in this same file for a different, unrelated finding — *"an employee cannot assign leads"* (investigated 2026-08-28, INTENTIONAL), which heads two detail blocks. The other side of the 2026-09-02 divergence independently recorded THIS finding as the detail block **`RBAC-5`** and wrote the ID NOTE explaining why. The `d02b702` merge kept both, because they sit in different regions of the file and git saw no conflict — leaving one finding under two ids while one id meant two findings. The register is the SSOT for finding ids (`GOVERNANCE.md §2`) and the RBAC-5 block had already adjudicated it, so applying that decision here is implementation, not a new choice. **Nothing was renumbered for tidiness:** the migration and test prose still say `RBAC-3`/`RBAC-4` and are deliberately left byte-identical to what Primary executed — the ID NOTE on the RBAC-5 block is the map from those to the register. The owner relaxed the standing "do not redesign RBAC" constraint and asked for an evidence-based Preserve / Refactor / Rebuild choice against a fully capability-oriented end-state. **The measurement decided it:** `role_permissions` is the ONLY foreign key into `public.permissions`, so there was **no path from a user to a permission except through a role** — that single missing edge is what made per-user grant, per-user revoke, deny and "role as an overridable bundle" impossible. Meanwhile `app.has_permission` is resolved by **60 RLS policies, 61 triggers and 76 functions**: the enforcement plane was already unified and already correct. **REBUILD REJECTED on that evidence** — it would rewrite 197 enforcement sites to reach identical behaviour, each one a chance to drop a rule earned by a real defect (SEC-1b, SEC-1c, PAR-4, BOOK-1, ADMIN-1, FIN-10, SUP-2): maximum risk, zero security gain. **PRESERVE REJECTED too** — the end-state was not reachable, and every "this user, minus this capability" would have become a new role, which is the role explosion OWASP names. Delivered: `user_permission_grants` (user x permission x grant/deny, lifecycle columns copied from `user_role_assignments`, SPEC-138 per-command RLS, composite tenant FKs per TENANT-1, **no DELETE grant** so the zero-DELETE invariant and the audit trail both survive); `has_permission` resolving **deny-override > user grant > role grant, then the plan gate**; `capability_group` + `action_kind` metadata DERIVED from canon 28's own section headings and naming convention (68 of 72 placed by canon; three left NULL rather than guessed); and `app.effective_permissions` as the explainer. **Deny-override is adopted, not invented** — AWS IAM explicit deny, Azure RBAC deny assignments, Azure DevOps. **Permissions were deliberately NOT moved into JWT claims**: Supabase's own guidance carries only a role reference, and ORVION resolving the actor from `public.users` means a revocation binds on the next statement, so claims would ADD a staleness window it does not have. **No privilege expanded** — with the table empty `has_permission` returns exactly what it did before, pinned as an assertion, not asserted in prose. Gives `MANAGE_PERMISSIONS` (RBAC-2's dead permission) its first enforcement and `permission_granted`/`permission_revoked` (EVT-2's class) their first producer. **`user_permission_grants.user_id` is classified as a SUBJECT column**, not an actor one — it names whose capability is granted, exactly as `user_role_assignments.user_id` does; the actor is `created_by`, derived by trigger. Recorded here before `83_actor_attribution_test.sql` assertion 23 was edited, per that assertion's own rule. | SUP-4a | **`suppliers.credit_limit_amount` was the only money column in ORVION with no currency beside it** | finance model / schema conformance | **Medium** | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-02 (`202607059900`), DEPLOYED.** The owner asked for the ceiling-enforcement defect to be resolved and, failing an authoritative exposure definition, for a NARROWER rule to be implemented if one existed without inventing semantics. One did, and it is derivable from canon rather than from the business. **Measured:** eleven `public` tables carry a money-amount column and **ten carry `currency_code` beside it**; `suppliers` was the only one that did not. Canon 30's money standard is explicit — *"Amounts should be stored as numeric"*, *"Currency code should be stored separately"*, `currency_code text not null`, *"values must reference `currencies.code`"*. So this was ORVION's own standard unapplied to exactly one column, not an undecided business rule. **Why it was the blocker:** `app.supplier_balance` returns `outstanding_payable` **one row per currency** (reproduced live: EGP 8,000 and USD 600 for one supplier) against a currency-less scalar ceiling of 10,000 — the comparison was not merely undecided, it was ill-formed. Fixed by `credit_limit_currency_code` referencing `currencies(code)` ON DELETE RESTRICT, with a CHECK binding the two halves together so an amount without a currency (or a currency without an amount) is refused on both doors. **No backfill decision taken** — the column is nullable and Primary holds zero business rows, so nobody's data was assigned a denomination it never stated. **Three guards caught defects in this migration as it was written:** `verify_database.sql` CHECK 7 rejected the FK's default NO ACTION; `10_grant_model_test` assertions 5 and 7 caught the PUBLIC EXECUTE that a freshly-CREATEd wrapper inherits after a drop; and the CHECK itself rejected five test fixtures that set an amount with no currency. `app.create_supplier` and `app.supplier_credit` carry the currency (the 6-arg overload DROPPED per SPEC-156, not left as a misleading contract). Durable guard: `90_..._test.sql` assertion 16 asks `information_schema` **with no exemption list**, so the next money column added without a currency fails there | — | owner-directive-2026-09-02 | 09-02 | 09-02 |
| SUP-4b | **Nothing refuses an operation for exceeding a supplier credit ceiling** | finance capability | Medium | R | 6 | A | ✅ | **✅ DECIDED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-04 AND IMPLEMENTED (`202607060100`), DEPLOYED TO PRIMARY.** The three open questions are answered, and the answer to the first makes the other two moot. **WHICH OPERATION: none is refused.** The owner ruled the ceiling **WARNS** — *"EXCEEDING the limit MUST NOT block operations"*, *"No new entry/addition/action should be prevented merely because the ceiling has been exceeded"*, *"The intended behavior is WARNING/ALERT, NOT REFUSAL/BLOCKING"*. **WHAT MAY OVERRIDE IT: nothing needs to** — an override permission exists to bypass a refusal, and there is no refusal, so none was minted. That is why the ADR-0020 shape this row previously recorded as the likely form was deliberately NOT copied: it is the shape of a *blocking* rule with an override, and the owner chose a non-blocking one. **WHETHER IT BINDS ON ALL SUPPLIERS: all of them** — already DERIVED (no type/status exclusion exists) and unchanged; a NULL ceiling remains no ceiling, pinned by assertion. **The CONCURRENCY question recorded here is dissolved rather than answered:** it mattered only because a *blocking* ceiling must serialise against the rows it sums. A warning cannot wrongly refuse anything, so no lock, no deferred constraint trigger and no FIN-10-style aggregate re-read were introduced — deliberately the simplest thing that satisfies the decision. **Delivered:** an AFTER-row probe on the only two tables that can move exposure (`booking_items`, `payments`); `app.evaluate_supplier_credit_threshold` comparing exposure to the ceiling **in the ceiling's own currency**; two canon-27 event types; and one notification per recipient to the **Company Owner and Finance Manager**, resolved by ROLE because this is who gets TOLD, not who is ALLOWED — authorization is untouched. **Idempotency uses the event ledger, not a new column or status vocabulary** (the owner forbade a colour/status system): an alert is suppressed while the supplier's most recent threshold event is already `exceeded`, and the `cleared` event on falling back is what lets a later breach speak again. **`supplier_credit` now also returns `exposure_amount` and `threshold_exceeded`** — one boolean a UI can render red, behind the unchanged VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS gate. **NOTHING GAINED A REFUSAL, and that is proven rather than asserted:** the over-ceiling write lands and is counted. **Two residues isolated rather than guessed — SUP-4c** (cross-currency "or equivalent") and **CUST-3** (a customer-side threshold). | SUP-4c | **"100,000 EGP *or equivalent*" — ORVION has no way to say what a non-EGP exposure is equivalent to** | finance model / business policy | Medium | O | 6 | — | 📋 | **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 (owner-decision resolution review) — ENGINEERING, not an owner decision. Authoritative external practice settles the rate instant: credit exposure against a limit is converted into the LIMIT's currency at the CURRENT SPOT rate (12 CFR 32.9's mark-to-market; the same rule in counterparty credit-limit systems). Canon 14 locks a rate at issuance for the TRANSACTION and routes corrections through an Exchange Rate Adjustment — that is the ACCOUNTING instant, and it belongs to DC-11. A control and a transaction are different computations, so canon's silence here was never a gap. `credit_limit_currency_code` is already the base currency, so no tenant base currency is needed. Engineering task, scheduled.** **Superseded text:** **OPEN — BUSINESS DECISION, isolated from SUP-4b rather than guessed.** The owner's threshold wording says "100,000 EGP or equivalent". SUP-4b implements everything that does NOT require an answer: a ceiling denominated in **any** currency is compared against exposure **in that same currency**, which is ADR-0020/ADR-0021's shipped per-currency rule. So a USD ceiling already works against USD exposure — "equivalent" in that sense is done. **What is NOT implemented is collapsing a supplier's multi-currency exposure into one comparable number**, and it was not implemented because ORVION cannot currently do it honestly: **`public.exchange_rates` exists as a table and NO function in the database reads it** (measured — zero readers), `tenants.default_currency_code` is read by nothing, and a supplier reproduced live in SUP-4a's investigation carried EGP 8,000 and USD 600 simultaneously. Answering this needs three things canon does not provide: a **base currency** per tenant, a **rate source and as-of rule** (booking date? lock date? today?), and whether a converted figure may drive an alert at all when the rate moves under it. **All three are commercial, not derivable** — inventing an FX mechanism to satisfy a threshold is exactly the redesign `AGENTS.md §3` forbids. **Not blocking:** every supplier whose ceiling and exposure share a currency is fully covered today | **owner: base currency + rate source + as-of rule for cross-currency exposure** | owner-directive-2026-09-04 | 09-04 | 09-04 |
| CUST-3 | **The owner's threshold wording said "at a customer or supplier"; customers have no ceiling to exceed** | finance capability / business policy | Low | O | 6 | — | 📋 | **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 — IMPLEMENTED (`202607060300`).** Owner-decided, then built: a nullable, tenant-supplied ceiling on `customers` (`credit_limit_amount numeric(19,4)` + `credit_limit_currency_code`, both-or-neither, non-negative), `MANAGE_CUSTOMER_CREDIT` (owner/ceo/finance_manager, plan-gated `finance_lite`), and warning-only evaluation emitting `customer_credit_threshold_exceeded`/`_cleared` with a `customer_balance` notification and a `pending` email delivery row — that catalog value's FIRST producer. **Unlike SUP-4b it CONVERTS foreign-currency exposure** into the ceiling's currency through the new generic `app.exchange_rate_as_of` (the first reader `public.exchange_rates` has ever had) and REPORTS any currency it cannot price rather than dropping it. See the implementation section at the end of this file. **Superseded text:** **✅ DECIDED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-04 (governed implementation session)** — **YES: ORVION WILL offer a customer receivable credit-ceiling capability.** Nullable and tenant-supplied, mirroring the shipped supplier pattern where structurally appropriate; **warning-only**, never blocking; **no numeric default invented**; no collection or dunning behaviour. An ENGINEERING task, no longer a question. **Superseded text:** **OPEN — BUSINESS DECISION, deliberately NOT folded into SUP-4b.** The owner's SUP-4b directive described exposure as *"our financial dues at a customer or supplier"*. Investigated rather than assumed: **`public.customers` carries ZERO credit columns** (measured against `information_schema`) — no limit, no currency, no term. `app.customer_balance` exists and is authoritative for what a customer OWES, and `notification_type = 'customer_balance'` is already registered in the catalog with no producer — so the *notification* half has a home, but **there is nothing to compare a customer balance against.** A customer threshold is therefore a NEW business rule requiring new schema (a receivable ceiling, its currency, and whether it is per customer or per plan/segment), not an extension of the supplier one. **Implementing it inside SUP-4b would have been silently converting one sentence of context into a second commercial policy**, which the owner's own instruction forbade. Recorded so the wording is not lost. **Note the asymmetry is real, not an oversight:** a supplier ceiling caps what ORVION owes; a customer ceiling caps what ORVION is owed, and the two have different collection, credit-risk and dunning consequences | **owner: whether customers get a receivable ceiling at all, and what it caps** | owner-directive-2026-09-04 | 09-04 | 09-04 |

| PD-23 | **The quota class is structurally inert: `usage_counters` has no producer, no reader and no rows, and `app.plan_limit` has no caller** | architecture | Medium | R | 6 | — | 📋 | **MEASURED 2026-09-01; implementation deliberately NOT started, and that is the finding.** Proven structurally rather than by a name grep: **no function anywhere in the database references `usage_counters`** — not an INSERT, not an UPDATE, not even a read — its only trigger is `moddatetime`, and it holds 0 rows. `app.plan_limit` exists and **nothing calls it**. Detector attacked before the zero was accepted: no view references the table, the single `EXECUTE format` function is a reference-integrity helper, and the only objects named `usage%` are the table and its three indexes. **The boolean half IS enforced** — `app.has_permission` composes `app.plan_allows`, so plan denial covers the RPC, PostgREST read and write paths at once (SPEC-146). **Why implementation was not justified now, derived rather than deferred by preference:** canon 28 §Plan Gating Enforcement already states the position — *"Ceilings are readable, not enforced. `app.plan_limit` exposes them; `usage_counters` is empty and counting is a separate additive mechanism"* — and canon 09 says the numeric limits *"must be reviewed before pricing is finalized"*, which has not happened. Enforcing them now would harden provisional commercial numbers into blocking behaviour with no client, zero business rows on Primary, and one metric (`max_automations`) counting n8n workflows in an instance holding zero. **Semantics differ per metric and must not be built as one thing:** `max_users` / `max_branches` / `max_storage_gb` are CURRENT-STATE (freeing a slot restores capacity, so a direct count is authoritative and a stored counter would be denormalised state that can drift), while `max_monthly_leads` / `max_monthly_bookings` are PERIODIC CONSUMPTION, which is the shape `usage_counters`'s `period_start`/`period_end` columns were built for. **Security checked and sound:** `authenticated` holds SELECT only on `usage_counters` (no INSERT/UPDATE), RLS is `tenant_isolation`, so the counter is not user-forgeable if it ever becomes authoritative. **Class re-scan:** of 23 tables with no function-INSERT producer, all but three are migration-seeded reference data or intentional direct-DML tables; the genuinely inert accumulators are this one, `notification_deliveries` (DOC-EXP-1) and the two canon-34 identity tables (AUTH-1) — all already recorded, so **no new class exists**. **Trigger to build:** pricing finalisation, or the first tenant approaching a ceiling on real data | owner (already recorded under PLAN-1): the three "Limited" ceilings canon leaves undefined | pd-23-2026-09-01 | 09-01 | 09-01 |
| COLD-1 | **A moving list restated outside its SSOT goes stale, and it had happened four times — three of them presenting settled work as a live blocker** | continuity / cold-start | **High** | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-01 and GUARDED (Check 14).** Found by booting the repository as a fresh session would and asking what it would misunderstand. Four instances of one class: (1) **canon 32's Phase-8 gate list** — already corrected once for naming a resolved AUDIT-3, its replacement went stale in turn, naming DOC-LC-1 (resolved), "API-3's remaining endpoints" (closed at 71/71), SPEC-154-B (shipped) and **SEC-1** (decided) as what keeps the gate shut; (2) **`manifest.md`''s `Next capability`** carried a second copy of the owner-blocker list, so after **OWNER-1** reconciled the real list two lines above it, the same file asserted both that SEC-1 was decided and that it "needs the owner"; (3) **`MASTER_EXECUTION_PLAN.md` item 8** still read "SEC-1 write-path model — remains the open owner decision"; (4) stale suite figures in the same manifest line. **Cold-start impact is the reason this is High, not the wording:** a fresh LLM reading any of the three would treat a settled architecture as an open question and could re-escalate it, or worse, propose the RPC-only write model the owner explicitly REJECTED. **Fixed by deleting the restatements, not refreshing them** — each now points at the one list that is maintained (`MASTER_EXECUTION_PLAN.md` Batch 6 for engineering, the manifest''s single `Open owner decisions` line for decisions), the same conclusion GOV-5, REG-2 and ROAD-1 reached. **Check 14** makes the worst half mechanical: every id on the manifest''s open-decision enumeration must still be OPEN in the register, with the decided-set derived from the register''s own status cells and no exemption list. **The new check immediately found two defects in my own artifacts and one in itself**, all fixed rather than exempted: **PLAN-1**''s status cell led "RESOLVED" while its body and Owner column say the numeric-ceiling half is open (now "PARTIALLY RESOLVED"); **SEC-1c**''s cell led with a reproduction narrative instead of its verdict (now leads "✅ FIXED"); and the check read a *citation* of the OWNER-1 evidence row as a listed blocker, corrected to parse only the enumeration after "Genuinely open:". Mutation-tested: re-listing SEC-1c on the open line is reported, removing it returns CLEAN | — | cold-start-2026-09-01 | 09-01 | 09-01 |
| META-1 | **A 14-mutation battery against the repository guard found five current-state facts that nothing verified** | verification integrity | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-01 (Checks 15 and L5), both mutation-tested.** The guards were treated as hypotheses rather than evidence: fourteen realistic mutations were injected, each proven to change the semantic state, then the guard was run and restored. **Nine caught** — migration count and ledger fingerprint (Check 9), broken reference (1), stale latest-report pointer (10), phase disagreement (6), escaped register row (13), decided-id-back-on-the-open-list (14), and two next-step/date mutations via ai-map freshness (7). **Five missed, one coherent blind spot: the manifest publishes current-state FIGURES that nothing owned.** `Suite **86 files / 1154 assertions**`, the HTTP total, the client-RPC count, the table count and — the serious one — **the structural-surface hash could be corrupted to zeros and BOTH guards stayed CLEAN**: the repository guard opens no database, and the parity guard never read the manifest. That is the one document a cold start reads to answer *"is Primary synchronized?"*. **Fixed with two derived checks, no hand-maintained lists:** **Check 15** compares the manifest''s suite figures against the test files themselves (every file carries a literal `select plan(N)`; 86 files summing to 1156, matching the pgTAP run exactly) and its RPC count against the GENERATED contract; **Check L5** in the parity guard compares the manifest''s published function and structural hashes against the live database, deliberately against LOCAL rather than the caller-supplied Primary values, because laundering a supplied value into a published fact is GUARD-1. **Two misses left unmechanised WITH REASONS rather than silence:** the HTTP total requires running the suites and is LOCAL RUNTIME evidence a file-only guard must not claim; and "75 tables" is the smoke test''s assertion, a different measurement from the contract''s tenant-reachable count, so comparing them would manufacture a false failure. **Check 15 then caught real, unplanted drift on its first run** — the suite had grown to 1156 while the manifest still said 1154. Also proven this pass: **test 85''s central assertion is load-bearing**, by dropping `suppliers_guard_write_capability` and observing the trainee''s UPDATE succeed (`UPDATE 1`), then refuse again on rollback — now pinned permanently as a PAR-4 pair (assertions 15-16) | — | meta-review-2026-09-01 | 09-01 | 09-01 |
| COLD-2 | **The cold-start handoff field no guard compared — and the sibling comparison that had been inert for forty commits** | continuity / cold-start | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-02 (Check 7, mutation-tested across 14 runs).** `scripts/generate-ai-map.ps1` extracts **four** live-state fields from `manifest.md`; Check 7 compared **three**. The uncompared one was `live_state.active_change_request` — the field `AGENTS.md §4` Stage A **branches** on (step 4 reads the active SPEC and hands over to its Minimum Reading List, step 5 goes to the roadmap instead) and the entirety of the agent handoff (`AGENTS.md §6`, `CR_LIFECYCLE.md §9`: *"never through chat"*). So the one live-state field with a **control-flow** consequence for a fresh session was the one field with no comparison behind it. **Proven blind in BOTH directions before anything was changed:** with ai-map naming a SPEC that does not exist while the manifest said `None.`, and with the manifest naming a SPEC that ai-map did not carry, the guard printed CLEAN at exit 0 — a cold-start agent would have chased a Change Request that was never approved, or silently skipped the one actually in flight. The field also has a recorded forgetting history: clearing it on Complete was omitted in **SPEC-024** and **SPEC-027**, and `future-backlog.md` still carries the resulting process-safeguard row. **Root cause — the guard's coverage was a record of past INCIDENTS rather than a statement about the generator's contract:** `phase` (2026-07-17, INC-2), `next_capability` (2026-08-17) and `last_completed` (2026-09-01) were each added only *after* that field had already drifted in production, so the field that had not yet been observed drifting had nothing written for it. Same family as **GOV-4** (an id pattern blind to every id minted after it was written) and **MEAS-1** — a guard written against the first instance takes that instance's shape. **Second half, previously unrecorded:** commit `4b67d3f` repaired the `last_completed` comparison, which had been gated on a literal-SPEC-id shape and was therefore **inert for roughly forty commits** after the field stopped naming a SPEC id — and the drift it exists to catch **shipped** in `302c7cb`. That repair landed with no register row, no session report and no health-document update, so the repository held the fix but not the knowledge; both halves are registered here as one class. **Fix:** a single comparison block on the *identical* extraction contract as `last_completed` (`Get-Field`'s anchor and label, single-line capture, trimmed, whitespace collapsed so reflowing cannot cry wolf) — no new mechanism, no maintained list of SPEC ids, and no other ai-map key brought under comparison. Check 7 now covers every live-state field the generator extracts, which is a claim about the contract rather than about which drifts happened to be noticed. **Attacked in 14 runs that report each of the four sub-comparisons separately, so field isolation is MEASURED:** a one-character mutation (`None.` vs `None`) fails, proving the comparison is genuinely active at the `None.` value rather than skipping it; a real SPEC path on both sides is CLEAN and diverging from it fails, proving it does not stop working the moment a Change Request is actually approved; each pre-existing comparison still fires only on its own field; and an unrelated `live_state.source` mutation stays CLEAN. **Limit stated at the point of implementation rather than left to be assumed:** this catches manifest↔ai-map **divergence** from either side, never a missed clear-on-Complete — a manifest still naming a finished SPEC, with ai-map regenerated to agree, is CLEAN here and always will be. That is a different invariant and remains unguarded, tracked in `future-backlog.md` and `GOVERNANCE.md §11`. Corrected in the same pass: `MASTER_REPOSITORY_HEALTH.md §2b` described itself as the single discoverable list of every automatic check while stopping at Docs-12 of the 17 the script runs. **DUPLICATE-DISCOVERY NOTE (reconciliation 2026-09-03): COLD-2 and GOV-10 are ONE defect, found twice** — see GOV-10's row for the full account. Of the two byte-equivalent implementations the merged guard kept **GOV-10's**, on one measurable difference: its warning echoes BOTH values (`ai-map says X but the manifest says Y`) where this one names neither, and a guard that prints what it saw is diagnosable without re-running it. This row's block was dropped from the TREE, never from the RECORD — the finding, its 14-run mutation evidence and its limit statement all stand | — | session-2026-09-02-cold-start-handoff-guard | 09-02 | 09-02 |
| COLD-3 | **The handoff pointer was never checked against the lifecycle it points into — the repository was CLEAN while naming a completed Change Request as the active one** | continuity / cold-start | Medium | R | 6 | COLD-2 | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-02 (Check 18), mutation-tested across 15 runs.** COLD-2 made Check 7 compare `manifest.md`'s `Active Change Request` with `ai-map.json` by value. That proves the two artifacts **AGREE**; it never asked whether what they agree on is **TRUE**, and nothing else did either. **Proven before any change:** with the manifest pointing at `changes/SPEC-125-remediation-governance-reconciliation.md` — whose own Status section is `[x] Complete` — and `ai-map.json` regenerated to match, the guard printed **CLEAN at exit 0**; so did a manifest pointing at `changes/SPEC-999-never-created.md`, a file that has never existed, because Check 1 deliberately excludes the `SPEC-NNN.md` placeholder shape from reference linting and so nothing had ever resolved this path. `AGENTS.md §4` Stage A step 4 **branches** on this field, so a cold-start session was one stale pointer away from being handed finished work as its assignment, or sent to a file that is not there. **The failure is recorded, not hypothetical:** the pointer-clear was omitted on Complete in **SPEC-024** and **SPEC-027**, and `future-backlog.md` had carried a process-safeguard row ever since — proposing a Claude Stop/PostToolUse hook, which would have run only on a workstation configured for it. **Authority, established from the repository rather than assumed:** the CR's own `## Status` section, defined by `changes/TEMPLATE.md` and governed by `CR_LIFECYCLE.md` §3/§4/§5/§8, with §9's `Approve`/`Complete` commands flipping that box and moving this pointer in the same breath. **Nothing else assigns a CR a status** — `MASTER_EXECUTION_PLAN.md` names SPEC ids 29 times and gives none of them a state — and all **151** CR files were surveyed: every one has exactly one `## Status` section with exactly one checked box, so the model is coherent and no owner decision arises. **Status is READ, never copied:** no CR status enters `manifest.md` or `ai-map.json`, so the manifest stays the sole current-state authority holding only the pointer, and no second source of truth is created. No maintained list of SPEC ids exists or is possible — the subject is whatever path the manifest names; the legal vocabulary is read from `TEMPLATE.md` so a vocabulary change is caught rather than silently accepted, and the terminal pair is `CR_LIFECYCLE.md §4`'s. `Draft` is deliberately not flagged (non-terminal, and §9's `Execute` already refuses one). **Fifteen mutation runs, each echoing the on-disk state before its verdict** so a CLEAN line can never be confused with a mutation that failed to land: the SAME CR file flips CLEAN→FAIL→CLEAN purely on its own Status box across `Draft`/`Approved`/`In Progress` vs `Complete`/`Cancelled`; a missing file, an absent Status section, zero checked boxes, two checked boxes and an out-of-vocabulary word each FAIL. **The open-CR case earned its place by catching a real defect in the check itself** — a single regex match returns a PowerShell scalar string, so `[0]` indexed the first *character* and a CR `In Progress` was reported as status `I`. That is an over-firing false positive the closed-CR and missing-file cases could never have revealed, because both expect FAIL. **Independence proven in both directions** (`AGENTS.md §5a` evidence discipline): a valid open CR with a stale ai-map fails Check 7 alone with Check 18 silent, and a closed CR with a synchronized ai-map fails Check 18 alone with all four Check-7 comparisons silent | — | session-2026-09-02-clear-on-complete | 09-02 | 09-02 |
| GOV-10 | **Check 7 compared three of the four `live_state` fields it copies, and the uncompared one was the cold-start handoff pointer** | continuity / cold-start | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-02, mutation-tested in both directions.** `scripts/generate-ai-map.ps1` extracts FOUR fields from the manifest into `ai-map.json` — `phase`, `active_change_request`, `last_completed`, `next_capability`. Check 7 is named "ai-map freshness vs manifest" and compared three of them: phase by token (2026-07-17, INC-2), `next_capability` by value (2026-08-17), `last_completed` by value (2026-09-01, after it had been inert for forty commits). **`active_change_request` was compared by nothing, and it is the load-bearing one:** `AGENTS.md §4` step 4 branches the ENTIRE boot sequence on it — not `None` ⇒ open that SPEC and let its Minimum Reading List take over; `None` ⇒ fall through to the roadmap — and `AGENTS.md §6` + `CR_LIFECYCLE.md §9` make it the only sanctioned handoff channel between sessions. A stale non-`None` value sends a cold-starting agent into a closed Change Request; a stale `None.` hides an open one and the agent silently starts different work. **The forgetting history is demonstrated, not hypothetical:** the `Complete SPEC-NNN` pointer-clear was omitted twice (SPEC-024, SPEC-027) and `reports/future-backlog.md` still carries the safeguard entry that omission earned. **ROOT CAUSE — coverage decided field-by-field instead of over the block.** Each field entered Check 7 on the day its own drift shipped, so the check's coverage is a record of which drifts have already been paid for, not of what it claims to measure; a field nobody had yet been burned by was simply unguarded while the check's name promised the whole `live_state` block. Same shape as **GOV-4** (Check 2's id pattern, blind to every id minted after it was written), **MEAS-1**, **VER-1** and **PAR-3** — a guard whose description outruns its measurement. **FIX:** the missing comparison, extracted and normalised by EXACTLY the contract `last_completed` established (generate-ai-map.ps1's `Get-Field` shape — single line, trimmed — whitespace collapsed so reflowing cannot cry wolf), proven identical to the generator's own extraction across four probes including a real SPEC path and a decorated/trailing-whitespace value. No new mechanism, no maintained SPEC-id list, and no other ai-map key brought under comparison. **Attacked before acceptance, in both directions and at both values:** the guard was repaired and PROVEN TO FAIL on an intentionally stale artifact BEFORE anything was regenerated; mutating the manifest alone fails, mutating the map alone fails, each restore returns CLEAN, and the comparison is demonstrably live at `None` as well as at a real SPEC path — the state it will actually sit in for most of its life is the state a `-notmatch`-style guard would have been silently inert in. Phase, `last_completed` and `next_capability` re-proven unaffected. **DOCUMENTATION RECONCILED IN THE SAME UNIT OF WORK, because the guard's description was wrong in BOTH directions.** `MASTER_REPOSITORY_HEALTH.md §2b` is the declared "single discoverable list of every automatic check": its **Docs-7** row claimed `Last Completed` coverage throughout the ~40 commits that comparison was inert and never mentioned `active_change_request` at all (claiming MORE than the implementation), while the table itself stopped at **Docs-12** although the guard has carried Checks 13–17 since 2026-08-30 (claiming LESS). Both corrected; Docs-13…17 written from the implementation, not from memory. Same pass removed two stale counts in that file — "the 11-check consistency guard" (17) and "68 files" for a suite now at 89 — by pointing at the owner instead of refreshing the number (**GOV-5**). **RESIDUAL, stated rather than hidden:** like the other three comparisons, this one measures DISAGREEMENT and not PRESENCE — it is silent if the manifest loses the field entirely. Not fixed here because adding a presence assertion for one field and not the other three would leave the same asymmetry one level down, which is the defect this row records. **DUPLICATE-DISCOVERY NOTE (reconciliation 2026-09-03): GOV-10 and COLD-2 are ONE defect, found twice.** Two sessions diverged from `4b67d3f` without fetching and each wrote the same missing comparison — GOV-10 at 17:59 and COLD-2 at 21:21 on 2026-09-02 — arriving at the same regex, the same variables and the same normalisation, differing only in the warning text. Both rows are KEPT, because deleting either would erase the evidence of how the duplication happened, which is a process finding in its own right. The merged guard carries exactly ONE implementation: two would fire on the same divergence and count one defect twice, a guard lying about magnitude (MEAS-1) | — | handoff-pointer-2026-09-02 | 09-02 | 09-02 |

(3) **Two `Check 18`s and two `ADR-0027`s** were minted for different things under the same identifier, and the ADR pair **auto-merged clean** into one file with two headings and no conflict marker to notice. A register row pointing at a test that does not exist is a finding whose evidence cannot be re-run. All three repointed to the surviving originals with correct assertion counts. **Check 1 widened** to resolve `NN_*_test.sql` in Living docs AND `scripts/*.ps1` — the same invariant (a reference that does not resolve), not a new check number. `history/` and `changes/` stay excluded: an immutable dated report naming a file that existed then is HISTORY (`GOVERNANCE.md §4`). **Proven both directions:** a nonexistent test reference flags (exit 1), an existing one does not, probe file restored byte-identical | — | session-2026-09-04-obs-remediation | 09-04 | 09-04 |
| GOV-14 | **A full forensic audit produced no repository artifact — its entire result existed only in the conversation** | continuity / process | Medium | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ CONTROL APPLIED 2026-09-04 (`AGENTS.md §6`).** The 2026-09-04 post-merge forensic audit re-proved the migration ledger live against Primary, reconstructed the authorization model from the live catalog and re-ran the consistency guard — and wrote **nothing** to the repository, because the instruction said change no file and `§6`'s rule is phrased as "every meaningful session ends with a session report", which a read-only audit does not obviously read itself into. A fresh session inherited none of it, and this session re-ran that work to answer the same questions. **The rule already existed** (`AGENTS.md §2/§6`, `GOVERNANCE.md §2/§6` — memory is a cache; the repository is long-term memory), so no parallel rule was created: its one home was extended to state that a read-only session is still a session, and that a no-mutation instruction makes the report *the only durable artifact the session can leave* rather than a step to skip. **Same class as COLD-2's own root cause** — a rule whose coverage is a record of past incidents rather than a statement of its contract | — | session-2026-09-04-obs-remediation | 09-04 | 09-04 |
| CANON-31-1 | **Canon 31 documented neither the per-user grant table nor the two permission-catalog columns its own migration mints** | canon / schema documentation | Low | R | 6 | — | ✅ | **✅ FIXED 2026-09-04.** `202607059800` created `public.user_permission_grants` and added `permissions.capability_group` / `permissions.action_kind`; `31_schema_draft.md` carried none of the three while documenting every sibling (`users`, `user_role_assignments`, `user_branch_assignments`, `roles`, `role_permissions`, `permissions`). Canon 24–33 owns schema INTENT (`GOVERNANCE.md §2`), so the authorization model's central table existing only in migrations, tests, an ADR and the generated API contract left canon unable to state what the grant model IS. **Inherited, not merge-caused** — proven by the canon-31 blob being identical across origin-tip, local-tip and HEAD: origin's `1193643` updated canon 31 for SUP-4a's currency column and never for the grant table. Added at intent level with the resolution order, the plan gate's terminal position, the no-DELETE rule and the tenant-composite FKs, pointing at the migration for as-built truth rather than restating it. **`capability_group` and `action_kind` are documented explicitly as metadata and never security boundaries** — nothing in the authorization decision reads either | — | session-2026-09-04-obs-remediation | 09-04 | 09-04 |

* BF-4 severity High iff the launch tenant is VAT-registered (likely KSA/GCC).

---

## Detail blocks (full field set — new & consolidated findings)

> Inherited findings (A*, B*, BF*, CDD*, RC*, FOE*, N*, INV*, R1–R6, R8) carry their verbose fields in their source reports (`engineering-audit`, `business-stress-test`, `design-evolution-plan`, `architecture-synthesis`). Reproduced here only where this ARB added or changed a field. The DC-series and DC-1/R7 detail below is authoritative.

### DC-1 / R7 — Money precision
- **Root cause:** money type chosen (`numeric(14,2)`) before the multi-currency reference model (`currencies.decimal_places`) was reconciled with storage scale; no test asserts scale ≥ currency precision. **Why missed:** all prior reviews checked table/column existence, never scale.
- **Business:** financial inaccuracy/un-reconcilable balances in GCC/Umrah/Hajj (KWD/BHD/OMR/JOD = 3-dp). **Technical:** truncation on write; the 3 derived RPCs inherit it. **Architectural:** money is cross-cutting to finance/booking/marketing.
- **Affected — tables:** all 22+ money columns (invoices, payments, refunds, payment_allocations, journal_entry_lines, booking_items, quotation_items, campaign_daily_metrics, offline_conversions, chart_of_accounts opening, exchange_rate_adjustments, subscription/plan amounts). **RPCs:** customer_balance, supplier_balance, booking_item_profit, all finance write RPCs. **Events:** none. **Catalogs:** none. **Permissions:** none. **Reports/dashboards:** all finance. **ADR:** amend/append (money-storage standard). **Canon:** `30` conventions, `31` schema. **Roadmap:** Batch 0.
- **Migration:** yes (ALTER TYPE scale). **Additive?** No — modifies built columns; must precede finance data. **Order:** Batch 0, after pgTAP (DC-16). **Design:** `numeric(19,4)` + rounding via `currencies.decimal_places`. **Req/Opt:** R.

### DC-2 — Write idempotency
- **Root cause:** RPCs designed for authorization/state-correctness, not client-retry semantics; idempotency solved only at integration edges. **Business:** duplicate payments/bookings/invoices on retry. **Technical:** no dedup on inbound writes. **Affected:** new `idempotency_keys` table; optional param on mutating RPCs; no catalog/event/permission. **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 1 (with first client). **Req:** R.

### DC-3 — Concurrency control
- **Root cause:** CHECK constraints mistaken for concurrency guarantees; no locking discipline documented. **Business:** oversold departures/allotments; lost updates. **Affected:** hold/issue RPCs (FOR UPDATE / advisory locks); optional `row_version` pattern; inventory tables (CDD-2). **Migration:** additive (logic + optional column). **Order:** Batch 1, before inventory ships. **Req:** R.

### DC-4 — PII erasure boundary
- **Root cause:** consent (inbound) modeled; erasure (outbound GDPR Art.17) owned by no domain; events already ID-based (erasure-safe) but CRM PII is raw. **Affected:** PII columns in customers/passengers → satellites or crypto-shred; `app.erase_party`; `party_erased` event; erasure_reason catalog; ERASE_PARTY permission. **Migration:** additive + possible PII relocation. **Order:** boundary decided Batch 0, implemented Batch 1. **Req:** R. **Evidence:** validated pattern (pseudonymization/crypto-shredding) — see cert report sources.

### DC-5 — Document binary storage
- **Root cause:** all reviews at relational layer; Supabase Storage is a separate surface; `financial_documents` enforces stricter-than-row visibility the byte layer must mirror. **Affected:** Storage buckets + Storage RLS keyed to tenant/visibility; Edge upload relay (size/MIME/AV); signed URLs. **Integrations:** Supabase Storage, AV scanner. **Migration:** additive (config). **Order:** Batch 2 with DML grants. **Req:** R.

### DC-6 — Sensitive-read audit
- **Root cause:** event philosophy records business milestones (writes), not reads; read-audit is a security control that fell between events and security_events. **Affected:** `sensitive_access_log`; guarded read RPCs for passports/financial-docs/PII; VIEW_ACCESS_LOG permission. **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 1. **Req:** R.

### DC-7 — Ticketing deadline
- **Root cause:** BF-1 captured post-issuance references; the pre-issuance fare-hold expiry was never enumerated. **Business:** agents track ticketing time limits in WhatsApp; PNRs lost. **Affected:** `booking_items.ticketing_deadline` (folds into R4); pg_cron sweep → `ticketing_deadline_approaching`/`expired` events + notifications. **Migration:** additive column (in R4). **Order:** Batch 0 with BF-1. **Req:** R.

### DC-8 — Self-healing / reconciliation
- **Root cause:** all reviews design-time; runtime operational integrity owned by no domain; outbox retries delivery but nothing heals stuck internal state. **Affected:** scheduled reconciliation RPCs (ADR-0018), `reconciliation_runs` log, `reconciliation_finding` event. **Automation:** pg_cron. **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 1. **Req:** R.

### DC-9 — Timezone anchor
- **Root cause:** SLA/business-hours tables checked for existence, not for the tz reference their math needs. **Affected:** `branches.timezone` (IANA); `process_lead_sla` window computation. **Migration:** additive column. **Order:** Batch 1. **Req:** R.

### DC-10 — Opening balances
- **Root cause:** provisioning (ADR-0016) assumes greenfield; onboarding an operating agency (existing AR/AP/cash/allotments) has no home. **Business:** historical balances stay in Excel; day-one balances understated. **Affected:** `opening_balance_batches` + opening AR/AP entries via posting_rules → Opening-Balance-Equity account; `app.import_opening_balances`; opening_balance_status catalog; opening_balance_posted event; MANAGE_OPENING_BALANCES permission. **Depends on:** dimensions/periods/posting (CDD-5). **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 4. **Req:** R.

### DC-11 — Realized FX gain/loss
- **Root cause:** multi-currency validated at balance level; settlement-posting path untraced. **Affected:** posting_rules template + FX gain/loss GL accounts on cross-currency allocation; reads payment_allocations.exchange_rate. **Depends on:** CDD-5 auto-posting. **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 4. **Req:** R.

### DC-12 — Passenger relationships (mahram)
- **Root cause:** passenger model reviewed for identity/documents, not inter-passenger graph; BF-2 covers group leader, not mahram compliance. **Business:** Saudi Umrah/Hajj mahram declaration; family grouping. **Affected:** `passenger_relationships` (passenger_id, related_passenger_id, relationship_type_code, is_mahram); passenger_relationship_type catalog (mahram/spouse/parent/child/sibling/guardian/group_leader). **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 5 with BF-2. **Req:** R.

### DC-13 — UUIDv7 keys
- **Root cause:** ADR-0002 optimized for uniqueness/guessability, never insert-locality at volume; random v4 fragments B-tree on append-heavy tables. **Affected:** PK default on events, security_events, conversation_messages, attribution_clicks, campaign_daily_metrics, notification_deliveries. **ADR:** amend ADR-0002. **Depends on/pairs with:** B8 partitioning. **Migration:** modifies PK default — cheapest before data. **Order:** Batch 0. **Req:** R (retrofit-risk).

### DC-14 — Tenant offboarding
- **Root cause:** reviews focused on isolation in, not exit out; GDPR portability + SaaS churn unowned. **Affected:** `app.export_tenant` (service_role→Edge over outbox), retention policy, `app.purge_tenant`; MANAGE_TENANT_LIFECYCLE permission; tenant_exported/purged events. **Depends on:** CDD-7 outbox. **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 5. **Req:** R.

### DC-15 — service_role blast radius
- **Root cause:** audit validated `authenticated` isolation; `service_role` treated as trusted-by-design, unbounded and unmonitored. **Affected:** principle — every service_role/SECURITY DEFINER RPC takes explicit tenant_id + asserts it; consider constrained platform DB role; log service_role access to DC-6. **ADR:** append (least-privilege principle). **Migration:** additive (principle + guards). **Order:** Batch 2. **Req:** R.

### DC-16 — pgTAP harness
- **Root cause:** verification is smoke-test + manual psql; no regression net — a precondition for safely executing R1–R8/DC-1/DC-13 built-table retrofits; also nothing asserts RLS coverage (V5 latent trap: RLS depends on tenant_id NOT NULL). **Affected:** test tree + CI step; invariant tests (RLS on every tenant table, money currency-correctness, append-only triggers present). **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 0, first. **Req:** R.
- **Status: ✅ IMPLEMENTED (SPEC-113, 2026-07-11).** `supabase/tests/**` + `supabase test db` in CI. RLS-coverage (negative-checked), append-only, and money-currency invariants live; money-currency runs as a `todo` that surfaces DC-1 (have:22 want:0) without breaking the build. Removing that `todo` wrapper is the acceptance test for the DC-1 fix. Extended by SPEC-114 (tenant_id index coverage) and SPEC-115 (function search_path).

### SEC-1 — Function search_path hardening (discovered 2026-07-11, continuous review)
- **Finding:** `app.forbid_mutation()` was the one of 55 app functions not pinning `search_path` (CODING_STANDARDS violation; mutable-search_path hardening gap in the append-only guard). **Status: ✅ IMPLEMENTED (SPEC-115).** Body unchanged, `set search_path=''` added; pgTAP `05_function_search_path_test` now guards all app functions permanently.

### OPS-2 — Smoke-test non-gating (discovered 2026-07-11, continuous review)
- **Finding:** `scripts/verify_database.sql` raises on the first broken invariant but did not set `ON_ERROR_STOP`, so psql exited 0 on failure — the smoke-test was silently non-gating for every caller (local + never run in CI). **Status: ✅ IMPLEMENTED (SPEC-116).** File now self-arms `\set ON_ERROR_STOP on` (verified: pass→0, fail→3) and CI runs it after every `db reset`.

### DC-17 — Realtime publication scope
- **Root cause:** reviews were data-at-rest; Realtime is a delivery surface for shared inbox/live dashboards. **Affected:** explicit per-table realtime publication list + RLS-authorized channels. **Migration:** additive config. **Order:** Batch 5 (Engagement). **Req:** R.

### DC-18 — pgvector semantic layer
- **Root cause:** AI ambition (RAG/semantic search) needs embeddings; not enabled. **Affected:** enable pgvector; `embeddings(owner_type, owner_id, vector, model)`. **Migration:** additive. **Order:** Batch 5 / backlog. **Opt:** O.

### V-series (verifications, not gaps — recorded for audit trail)
- **V1 RESOLVED:** RLS covers all 5 marketing/offline tables via the dynamic NOT-NULL-tenant_id loop (`202607043300` §1). Earlier "no RLS" worry overturned. ✅
- **V2:** `tenant_isolation` uses bare `app.current_tenant_id()` → A1 confirmed. 
- **V4:** `events`/`security_events` immutability trigger exists (`forbid_mutation`) → partially closes B4. ✅ for those two tables.
- **V5:** RLS coverage silently depends on `tenant_id NOT NULL` → guarded by DC-16 test.
- **V6 (ARB-07-11):** schema has **0 views / 0 materialized views** — the read-model layer (reporting/RI/dashboards) does not yet exist; RC-4 introduces it. Recorded, not a defect.

### S-EVENT — Event vocabulary unenforced (verified 2026-07-15 audit; fix = N1, deferred to implementation phase)
- **Evidence (source-verified twice):** `events.event_type_code` is `text not null` in `202607042600_create_event_and_notification_tables.sql:13` with **no FK and no CHECK**; a repository search for a `create table … event_type(s)` catalog returns **none** — no event-type catalog exists. `app.record_event`'s `p_event_type_code` is passed by call sites as a computed variable, so a mistyped code is silently accepted. Emitted codes already drift from canon-27 (e.g. `internal_supplier_linked` vs canon `internal_supplier_link_created`; `receipt_issued` vs `receipt_created`; `invoice_issued`/`invoice_paid`/`supplier_payment_recorded`/`refund_requested` absent from canon-27).
- **Assessment:** this is the substantive justification for **N1** (event_type registry validated by `record_event`) — it is arresting *live* drift, not speculative. **Classification: the fix is a new schema object + ADR-0006 amendment = architectural capability, OUT of scope for the Repository Recovery phase.** Recorded here so it is not re-discovered; implement under N1 when the implementation phase resumes, together with a one-authority canon-27 ↔ emitters reconcile.

### AUDIT-3 — RLS scope granularity (discovered + independently re-verified 2026-08-16, pre-Phase-8-completion audit)
- **Evidence (source-verified twice — live `pg_policies` on Primary, and `202607043300_create_rls_policies.sql`):** every tenant-owned table carries exactly one RLS policy, `tenant_isolation`, `for all ... using (tenant_id = app.current_tenant_id())`, scoped `roles: {authenticated}`. Any authenticated member of a tenant can read/write every row of that tenant's data in a table through a direct query. Role-gating of business *actions* is correctly enforced at the RPC layer (`app.authorize('<PERMISSION>')`), but that protects only calls through those specific RPCs, not ad-hoc reads. `28_permissions_matrix.md` already defines `branch`/`department`/`assigned` scope types as part of the intended permission model; no RLS predicate implements them today.
- **Why it matters now, not later:** no employee-facing UI or direct-read surface exists yet, so there is no present exposure. But retrofitting scope-aware RLS onto tables that already hold real tenant data is exactly the class of expensive structural migration `AGENTS.md §3`'s Fundamental-Domain-Structure test exists to prevent. There is currently no roadmap phase named "Employee UI" (checked `32_execution_roadmap.md` — the roadmap ends at Phase 10, Automation & Integrations) — this finding has no phase to attach to yet, so it is recorded here rather than silently deferred.
- **Owner decision required:** whether scope enforcement becomes (a) additional RLS predicates keyed off the permission-matrix scope types, (b) mandatory RPC/view-only reads for any future employee UI (no direct table access), or (c) a hybrid. Reserved for the owner per `AGENTS.md §3`'s Owner-Decision tier — not decided here.
- **Trigger:** re-evaluate at the Design Review for whichever phase first introduces employee-facing reads beyond the current RPC surface (an Employee UI phase, if/when one is added to the roadmap, or any earlier PostgREST-direct-read consumer).

### AUDIT-4 — Customer-level consent record (discovered + independently re-verified 2026-08-16, pre-Phase-8-completion audit)
- **Evidence (source-verified twice — live schema query, and migration source):** `customers.marketing_opt_in` (`202607042100_create_crm_core_tables.sql:20`) is a single `boolean not null default false` with no grant timestamp and no per-purpose breakdown. `attribution_clicks.consent_ad_user_data`/`consent_ad_personalization` (`202607048700_r5_attribution_capture.sql`) are real, well-implemented, and correctly gate conversion delivery in the database (SPEC-119) — but they are scoped to a single click event, not durable per-customer state.
- **Why it matters:** GDPR/PDPL-grade consent, and Meta's WhatsApp opt-in requirement specifically, need timestamped, purpose-scoped, withdrawable consent records. Distinct from — but adjacent to — **DC-22** (data-residency/jurisdiction) and **DC-25** (retention/archival strategy); this finding is about the permission-to-contact mechanism itself, not where data lives or how long it's kept. Does not duplicate either existing row.
- **Owner decision required:** consent-table scope (which purposes: marketing, WhatsApp messaging, data-processing generally) and taxonomy (reuse the existing `granted/denied/unspecified` vocabulary already proven at `attribution_clicks`).
- **Trigger:** before WhatsApp Cloud API integration (Phase 10) requires trackable opt-in; not required for Phase-8 (Google Ads) completion, whose consent gate is already correctly implemented at the click level.

### AUDIT-5 — Channel-concept fragmentation across 4 catalogs (discovered + independently re-verified 2026-08-16, pre-Phase-8-completion audit)
- **Evidence (source-verified twice — live `catalog_values` query, cross-checked against migration-declared intent):** `contact_method_type` (8 values, splits social networks individually), `channel_code` (6 values), `notification_channel` (3 values), and `platform_code` (6 values) each independently model an overlapping "how did we reach / get reached" concept — `whatsapp` alone appears as a distinct code in three of the four catalogs at different granularity.
- **Why it matters:** a future "performance by channel" dashboard needs a mapping table or hardcoded reconciliation across four vocabularies — a reporting-layer cost, not a correctness defect today; nothing is broken, nothing miscounts.
- **Assessment:** Architecturally Optional — no evidence this fragmentation causes present harm; the four catalogs currently serve genuinely different table contexts (contact methods, conversation channels, notification delivery, integration platform). Consolidation is a normalization improvement, not a required capability.
- **Trigger:** document a channel-taxonomy mapping (or unify) at the Design Review for the first channel-performance dashboard — not before, per Earn-It.

### PH8-1 — A `pending` delivery is unrecoverable (discovered + source-verified 2026-08-17, Phase-8 pre-build workflow review)
- **Evidence (source-verified against migration `202607049200_offline_conversion_core.sql`):** `app.claim_conversion_deliveries` excludes any conversion holding a delivery whose `delivery_status_code in ('pending','sent')` (lines 145–149), and `app.record_conversion_delivery_result` raises unless the delivery is currently `pending` (lines 217–220). The retry design therefore recovers `failed` deliveries only — a `failed` row is retired to `retried` and a fresh attempt is opened on the next claim (lines 157–165). A row that is claimed but never acked stays `pending` forever. Verified by grep that **no** stale/timeout/reaper logic exists anywhere in `supabase/migrations/**`.
- **Why it matters:** the outbox is designed for a stateless caller (ADR-0023) that can crash. Any n8n run interrupted between `Claim Deliveries` and `Ack Result` — deploy, timeout, OAuth expiry mid-run, rate-limit abort, n8n incident — permanently strands that conversion: it is never re-claimed, never retried, never acked, and never counted as failed. It is invisible to the retry ceiling because the ceiling counts delivery rows, not outcomes. Silent, unbounded revenue-attribution loss with no operator signal.
- **Not a Phase-8 blocker for a supervised manual run** (an operator watching one execution can see the interruption), but it must be resolved before the workflow is activated on its 15-minute schedule and left unattended.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-20 — owner-approved lease / visibility timeout (SPEC-123, migration `202607050100`).** `app.claim_conversion_deliveries` now terminalizes every `pending` delivery older than a **30-minute lease** to `failed` (stamped `LEASE_EXPIRED: …`, emitting `offline_conversion_failed` with payload `expired_lease: true`) before it claims, so the existing retry machinery recovers the conversion unchanged. Chosen over a separate `requeue_stale_*` RPC because the sweep needs no orchestration, so the `§2` workflow contract stays exactly 7 nodes. No schema change and no new status vocabulary — `failed` already exists in the `offline_conversion_delivery_status` catalog; `created_at` is the lease start (the row is INSERTed at claim time), so no column was added. Expired attempts **count toward the ceiling of 5**, so a crashed worker consumes an attempt and cannot bypass the limit; a ceiling-exhausted expired lease terminalizes to `failed` rather than looking in-flight forever; a `sent` delivery is never touched; and a late "zombie" ack is refused by the unchanged `record_conversion_delivery_result`. **Lease derivation (not arbitrary): lease > enforced workflow timeout > worst-case run** — 30 min is 3x the 10-minute `Timeout Workflow` the workflow is now required to set, and 6x the ~5-minute worst-case 50-row run; recovery latency is at most the lease plus one 15-minute tick. Full reasoning, the live two-session concurrency proofs, and the index measurement: `changes/SPEC-123-conversion-delivery-lease.md`. Permanent guard: `supabase/tests/09_conversion_delivery_lease_test.sql` (17 assertions). **Deployed to Primary 2026-08-20 and verified live there** — 90 migrations, the deployed function behaviorally exercised. It was also applied to Secondary that day under the dual-deployment rule since **permanently revoked** (owner-ratified 2026-08-20: `PlatPlusHub/CRM` → Primary only); that apply was **fully reverted on Secondary and verified**, so **PH8-1 is resolved on Primary only — which is correct, because Primary is the sole CRM environment** (`MASTER_INTEGRATION_CATALOG.md §0/§4`).
- **Residual, deliberately not absorbed into SPEC-123:** the lease converts *silent permanent loss* into *at-least-once delivery*, opening a narrow double-count window (crash after Google ingests but before the ack). The mitigation is workflow-side, not database-side — see **PH8-5**. Also unchanged: a conversion whose runs crash five times is now visibly `failed` instead of invisibly `pending`, but is still undelivered, and no delivery-health surface exists for an operator to notice it (**PH8-2** owns that observability gap).

### PH8-2 — Non-consented conversions are silently dropped with no operational surface (discovered 2026-08-17, Phase-8 pre-build workflow review)
- **Evidence (source-verified):** `app.claim_conversion_deliveries` joins `attribution_clicks` with `ac.consent_ad_user_data = 'granted'` (migration `202607049200`, lines 141–143). `attribution_clicks.consent_ad_user_data` is nullable and its CHECK permits NULL (migration `202607048700`, lines 11–13), so `denied`, `unspecified`, and `NULL` all fail the join identically. Such conversions are created by the mapper, are never claimed, produce no delivery row, and emit no event.
- **Why it matters:** the in-DB consent gate is correct and must stay (it is the guarantee no orchestration bug can ship unconsented data — the reason it lives in the database at all). The gap is **observability, not enforcement**: nothing anywhere distinguishes "delivered", "failed after 5 attempts", and "never eligible because consent was absent". An operator investigating "why is Google under-reporting our conversions?" has no surface that answers it, and a GTM/consent-capture regression that silently zeroes consent on every click would look identical to having no conversions at all.
- **Owner decision:** whether non-consented conversions warrant an operational surface (a `reporting` view is the cheap answer, consistent with the Phase-9 Tier-A pattern) or are intentionally silent. Recording, not deciding.

### PH8-3 — No E.164 normalization; phone identifiers will almost never reach Google (discovered 2026-08-17)
- **Evidence (source-verified):** Google's Data Manager API requires E.164 formatting before SHA-256 hashing (current official docs, fetched 2026-08-17). `customers.primary_phone` is `text` with no format constraint (migration `202607042100`, line 16); `app.claim_conversion_deliveries` returns it raw (`cu.primary_phone`, line 183). No E.164 normalization exists anywhere in `supabase/migrations/**` or in canon.
- **Why it matters:** Egyptian numbers are conventionally stored locally (`01xxxxxxxxx`), which cannot be converted to E.164 without assuming a country code. The build spec (`MASTER_INTEGRATION_CATALOG.md §2a`) correctly drops such numbers rather than guessing a country — the safe behaviour — but the consequence is that in practice **only email will be sent as a user identifier**, measurably weakening Google's Enhanced-Conversions match rate. This is a data-model gap surfacing at the integration edge, not a bug in the workflow.
- **Owner decision:** a default-country-code policy (tenant-level default is the natural home — ORVION is single-country today but explicitly multi-tenant by design, so hardcoding `+20` in an n8n node would be the wrong place). Interacts with the eventual customer-facing UI, which is where phone input should be normalized at entry.

### PH8-4 — `qualified_phone_call` has no producer (discovered 2026-08-17)
- **Evidence (source-verified):** `app.map_outcomes_to_conversions` consumes exactly four event types and maps them to four conversion types — `lead_qualified→qualified_lead`, `booking_created→booking_created`, `payment_recorded→payment_received`, `booking_issued→ticket_issued` (migration `202607049300`, lines 55–56 and 76–81). `qualified_phone_call` is a valid seeded `offline_conversion_event_type` and now has a live Google Ads Conversion Action (`7724520652`), but nothing in the pipeline produces it. It can only originate from a direct `app.record_offline_conversion` call, which requires an authenticated user holding `MANAGE_MARKETING_CAMPAIGN` — i.e. a UI or telephony integration that does not exist yet.
- **Why it matters:** low. Nothing is broken; the Conversion Action simply stays at zero. Recorded so a future session does not mistake the empty action for a delivery defect, and so the telephony/UI work that would light it up is traceable to canon 21's "qualified_phone_call" intent.
- **Trigger:** first call-tracking/telephony integration, or the first employee-facing UI able to mark a call qualified.

### PH8-5 — No deduplication key is sent to Google; the PH8-1 lease makes double-counting possible (discovered 2026-08-20, SPEC-123 three-perspective review)
- **Evidence (source-verified + external):** the `§2` send step (step 4) builds the Data Manager payload from click IDs, hashed `userIdentifiers`, action, value/currency, `conversion_at` and consent — **no transaction/order identifier**. Google's own documentation is explicit that `transactionId` is the deduplication key: *within the same conversion action, Google Ads uses the `transactionId` to deduplicate conversion events sent from different sources, including Data Manager API ingestion requests* (`developers.google.com/data-manager/api/devguides/events/send-events`; `support.google.com/google-ads/answer/6386790`, verified 2026-08-20).
- **Why it matters, and why it is new:** before SPEC-123, a redelivery could only follow a `failed` ack — i.e. Google had rejected the event and ingested nothing, so retrying was safe. The lease deliberately introduces a second redelivery path: a run that crashes *after* Google ingests the event but *before* the ack now has its delivery reclaimed and re-sent. That is the correct trade (at-least-once beats silent permanent loss) but it is only *safe* if Google can deduplicate, and today it cannot. The failure is silent and inflates reported conversions, which then feeds Smart Bidding.
- **Fix (no ORVION code change required):** send `offline_conversions.id` as `transactionId` on every event. It is already a stable, unique, immutable per-conversion UUID that survives every retry and reclaim, and `app.claim_conversion_deliveries` already returns it as `conversion_id`. This is a **workflow-side** change, recorded as mandatory build-spec correction 4 in `MASTER_INTEGRATION_CATALOG.md §2a`; verify the exact field name against current Google docs at build time, as `§2` already requires for the rest of the payload.
