- **OWNER-APPROVED 2026-09-01 (PD-05), with its limit stated rather than glossed.** Re-verified against Google's current documentation: `transactionId` deduplicates **within the same conversion action**, and for offline import it is optional-but-recommended. **What `offline_conversions.id` DOES guarantee:** the same local conversion record cannot be counted twice however many times ORVION re-sends it — which is exactly the PH8-1 lease risk, the only duplication ORVION can itself create. **What it does NOT guarantee, and must not be claimed:** deduplication against a conversion the *website tag* already reported. Google matches only on an exact `transaction_id` string, so a `gen_random_uuid()` ORVION invented will never equal a tag-generated id; differing prefixes, suffixes or whitespace defeat it too. Cross-source dedup therefore requires a **shared** transaction identity minted where the customer transacts and carried into ORVION — which does not exist today: `offline_conversions` has no external/order-id column and nothing upstream supplies one. That is a *distinct*, unopened question about where a shared transaction identity is born, not a defect in this fix, and it is deliberately not being designed here (no web tag is deployed and no conversion has ever been delivered).
- **Status:** nothing is broken today — the workflow does not exist yet, and no conversion has ever been delivered. This must be applied when the workflow is built, before `validateOnly` is ever flipped to false.

### PH8-6 — `offline_conversion_send_attempted` / `offline_conversion_retried` are canon-required and registered but never emitted (found 2026-08-15 integration-catalog cross-check; registered here 2026-08-20)
- **Evidence (source-verified across all 90 migrations, 2026-08-20):** `_ORVION_CANONICAL/26_state_machines.md` (Offline Conversion Delivery → "Required Events") mandates five events — `offline_conversion_created`, `offline_conversion_send_attempted`, `offline_conversion_sent`, `offline_conversion_failed`, `offline_conversion_retried` — and all five are seeded into the `event_type` registry by migration `202607049100`. A repository-wide scan of every `app.record_event(...)` call finds only **three** ever emitted: `offline_conversion_created` (`202607049200`, line 92), `offline_conversion_sent` and `offline_conversion_failed` (`202607049200`, line 232), plus `offline_conversion_failed` again from the SPEC-123 lease sweep (`202607050100`, line 93). `offline_conversion_send_attempted` has no producer anywhere; the `failed → retried` transition inside `app.claim_conversion_deliveries` updates `delivery_status_code` directly without calling `app.record_event`, so `offline_conversion_retried` has no producer either.
- **Dynamically reconfirmed, not only static:** the 2026-08-15 local pipeline validation drove a real failure → retry → success cycle and then counted both event types at **0** (`MASTER_INTEGRATION_CATALOG.md §4`, "Internal ORVION-side pipeline validation").
- **Why it matters:** the delivery pipeline's own telemetry is incomplete — attempt counts and retry counts cannot be derived from the event stream, only from `offline_conversion_deliveries` row state. This is an observability gap, not a correctness or data-loss defect: no delivery outcome, retry ceiling, or CRM state depends on either event. It becomes more visible once SPEC-123's lease starts terminalizing expired leases, because the recovery path a `retried` event would narrate is exactly the one now exercised automatically.
- **Distinct from existing findings — checked before registering:** **N1 / S-EVENT** cover the *inverse* direction (making `record_event` reject codes absent from the registry — implemented by `202607049100`); **AUDIT-1** covered codes that were emitted but never registered (fixed by SPEC-120). Neither covers codes that are registered and canon-required but never emitted. No existing row covered this; it had lived only as prose in `MASTER_INTEGRATION_CATALOG.md §4`, which is why it is registered here (findings SSOT — `GOVERNANCE.md §2`).
- **Resolution direction is undetermined and deliberately not chosen here.** Two coherent options exist and both are technical/architectural rather than business-policy: (a) emit the two missing events, which is a `create or replace` on the affected `app` functions (hence `Mig = M`); or (b) amend canon `26` to the three events the design actually needs, if the other two are judged redundant with delivery-row state. Deciding between them is a design step for the owning CR, not something this registration presumes.
- **Severity basis (not independently re-scored):** carried from the repository's own recorded assessment of the same finding — "*Not blocking Phase-A validation; flagged for a future CR under the existing discovery-to-guard duty*" (`MASTER_INTEGRATION_CATALOG.md §4`).
- **Trigger:** the next CR that touches the offline-conversion delivery surface, or the first operational need for attempt/retry telemetry from the event stream.

### DEL-1 — the database cannot destroy a storage object (discovered 2026-08-27, WP-04-D, live-verified on Primary and local)
- **Category:** platform capability · **Severity:** High (it bounds the whole retention design) · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: storage deletion stays outside PostgreSQL, via the authorized executor.** Engineering task (WP-04-E); the platform constraint below is unchanged and is the reason the answer is what it is. **Superseded:** BLOCKED — MISSING PLATFORM CAPABILITY · **Owner:** engineering (WP-04-E)
- **Evidence (live, both environments):** `storage.protect_delete()` is installed by Supabase as a `BEFORE DELETE` trigger on **both** `storage.objects` and `storage.buckets`, raising `42501` — *"Direct deletion from storage tables is not allowed. Use the Storage API instead."* — unless the session GUC `storage.allow_delete_query` is `'true'`; its own `HINT` states the reason: *"This prevents accidental data loss from orphaned objects."* Independently, `pg_net` is **not** in `pg_extension` on either environment (`moddatetime`, `pg_cron`, `pg_stat_statements`, `pgcrypto`, `supabase_vault`, `uuid-ossp`, `plpgsql`, plus `pgtap` locally), so the database cannot reach the Storage HTTP API either. Pinned by assertion 12 of `49_document_retention_test.sql`.
- **Root cause:** object bytes live in S3, not in PostgreSQL. `storage.objects` is only the index of them, and Supabase deliberately prevents that index being edited out from under the bytes.
- **Impact:** no `retention_expired` or `orphan_object` finding can be *acted upon* from inside the database. Detection, authority, retention policy and audit are all complete; execution is not.
- **Rejected remediation, recorded so it is not re-proposed:** a `SECURITY DEFINER` function carrying `set storage.allow_delete_query = 'true'`. It works around a security control the platform installed deliberately (against the owner's standing instruction), and it would not even achieve the goal — deleting the row destroys ORVION's only record of the object's name while the bytes survive, manufacturing exactly the orphan the package exists to detect.
- **Remediation:** WP-04-E — an external executor (Edge Function or n8n) authenticating as `service_role`, performing the byte operation through the Storage API and reporting back through `app.platform_resolve_storage_finding`.
- **Test requirement:** the executor must be proven idempotent under retry, unable to act on a restricted tenant's finding, and unable to touch an object outside the finding's own tenant prefix.

### RET-1 — the document retention period is undecided (discovered 2026-08-27, WP-04-D)
- **Category:** business policy · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** `app.document_retention_days()` returns `NULL`. Egyptian record-keeping obligations for travel documents are not in canon and are not inventable from evidence.
- **Decision required:** how long a **superseded** document version is retained before it may be destroyed. Not how long a current version is kept — a current version is never eligible at any age.
- **Why it does not block the technical work:** NULL means *retain forever*. The retention scan's `WHERE` clause is unsatisfiable while it is NULL, so nothing is ever selected for destruction — the "retention cannot accidentally become delete-immediately" property is the **default**, not a validation someone must remember to run. Asserted by `49_document_retention_test.sql` §2 (a 400-day-old superseded version is not marked) and §1 (the function returns NULL or ≥ 1, so a mistaken `0` fails the suite rather than being silently absorbed).
- **Remediation when decided:** one line of one migration. Nothing else changes.

### RET-2 — a departed tenant's stored data is retained indefinitely (discovered 2026-08-27, WP-04-D cross-path review)
- **Category:** business policy · **Severity:** Low today, Medium at scale · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: a 30-day export window, then purge, with legal hold blocking.** ENGINEERING task; implementation owed. **Superseded:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** `document_versions_enforce_document_subscription_gate` fires `BEFORE DELETE` and has no system-path exemption. WP-03 settled deliberately that the gate is the boundary and batch callers skip lapsed tenants (`36_subscription_gate_system_paths_test.sql`), so `app.platform_resolve_storage_finding` checks `app.subscription_allows_write` and refuses explicitly rather than tripping an opaque `42501` three layers down. Asserted by `49_document_retention_test.sql` §23.
- **Impact:** a tenant that never returns to good standing keeps its superseded versions, and their storage cost, forever. Canon 28 promises a restricted tenant that its data may be read and exported, which is precisely why the refusal is correct and not a workaround.
- **Decision required:** what ORVION owes a departed tenant's data — an export window then destruction? Indefinite retention as a commercial courtesy? A separate platform-owner purge authority explicitly outside the read-only promise?

### EVT-2 — 42 registered event types have no producer (discovered 2026-08-27, WP-04-D post-package sweep)
- **Category:** vocabulary / observability · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: no producer is invented merely to complete a catalog.** The clusters below stand as the measurement. **Superseded:** BLOCKED (mixed, per cluster) · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** a live scan of every `app` function body (`pg_get_functiondef`) plus every non-internal trigger definition found 43 registered `event_type` values appearing nowhere. The scan is deliberately generous — a code counts as produced if it appears anywhere at all, comments included — so it can only under-report; everything it flags is genuinely unreachable. Pinned by `07_event_vocabulary_registry_test.sql` assertion 2 with `<= 42`, so the number may fall and never rise.
- **Clusters and their real classification:** the auth family (`login_attempt/_success/_failure`, `otp_*`, `totp_*`, `password_*`, `account_locked`, `trusted_device_expired`) is **BLOCKED — ARCHITECTURAL**: those events are produced by Supabase Auth, which ORVION does not intercept (ADR-0017 / canon 34). The `notification_*` family belongs to a package that does not exist yet. `booking_item_cost_*`, `exchange_rate_*`, `permission_granted/_revoked`, `passenger_document_added`, `task_overdue` and the remainder await their owning feature or an explicit producer decision. Distinct from **PH8-6**, which is the same class narrowed to the two offline-conversion codes and keeps its own row.
- **Why 42 producers were not built:** the owner directive forbids inventing features as firmly as it forbids skipping defects. Registering vocabulary the system never speaks is the debt; **measuring** it and preventing its growth is the fix available today.
- **Resolved out of this set:** `role_removed` — an **asymmetry**, not an unbuilt feature, and therefore fixed. See RBAC-1.

### RBAC-1 — every privilege grant made outside one RPC was unaudited, and no revocation was ever audited (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-27, `202607055100`)
- **Category:** security / audit integrity · **Severity:** High · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence (live):** `public.user_role_assignments` carried **no triggers whatsoever** — `pg_trigger` returned zero non-internal rows for it. `role_assigned` was emitted only by `app.assign_user_role`; `role_removed` was a registered event type with no producer anywhere; no `app.revoke_user_role` existed. The table's RLS (`scope_insert`/`scope_update`/`scope_delete`) checks `app.has_permission('MANAGE_USERS')`, while the RPC charges `app.authorize`, which additionally composes MFA.
- **Impact:** (1) no privilege revocation in ORVION's history was ever recorded — an administrator's session could strip a colleague's roles, including the Owner's, and leave the `security`-severity audit stream looking untouched; (2) direct-DML **grants** were equally unrecorded, so grant coverage was never complete either; (3) the destructive path cost no step-up while the safe path did, inverting the incentive the control exists to create.
- **Root cause:** the rule lived in a caller rather than on the table — the WP-00 class this programme has closed repeatedly elsewhere.
- **Remediation (applied):** one `AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE` trigger, `app.emit_role_change()`, is now the **single producer** of both events on every path; `app.assign_user_role` **lost** its own `record_event` call rather than gaining a duplicate beside the trigger's. Session-ful writes are charged `app.authorize('MANAGE_USERS')` (MFA parity); session-less platform paths are exempt from the *check* and never from the *record*. Only a change in **effective access** is audited (`is_active` **and** `ends_at`, since either alone ends a grant), so column churn does not pollute the stream. `app.revoke_user_role()` added — it ends the assignment rather than deleting it, since `24_assignment_history_test.sql` establishes assignment history as evidence.
- **Test requirement (met):** `51_role_change_audit_test.sql` (13) — direct-DML grant audited, RPC emits exactly one, both revocation routes audited, no-op emits nothing, MFA denial **with** its positive control, system path permitted and still audited with no invented actor.

### POL-1 — four RLS policies applied `to public` instead of `to authenticated` (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-27, `202607055000`)
- **Category:** authorization hygiene · **Severity:** Low (not exploitable today) · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** grouping `pg_policies.roles` across schema `public` returned 113 `{authenticated}` and 4 `{public}` — `document_links.scope_isolation`, `subscription_payment_proofs.scope_read`, `subscription_payment_proofs.scope_insert`, `tenant_license_activations.platform_only`. All four were introduced by SPEC-158 and WP-04-C.
- **Root cause:** `create policy … using (…)` with the `to` clause omitted defaults to `PUBLIC`. **PostgreSQL's silent default is PUBLIC, so an omitted clause is a grant rather than a no-op.** The same root cause produced a missing `revoke execute` on `app.document_retention_days()` in the same session, caught by `10_grant_model_test` §5 — two instances in one package is what makes it a class rather than a slip.
- **Impact:** none reachable today (`anon` holds no privilege; SPEC-124 and `10_grant_model_test` keep it that way). The cost is an unreadable authorization surface, and a defect that would arrive the day any role other than `authenticated` is granted a privilege on these tables — without anyone editing the policy.
- **Remediation (applied):** `alter policy … to authenticated`, which changes the role list and provably nothing else. Drop-and-recreate was rejected: retranscribing `document_links`' ten branches by hand for the second time in two migrations is exactly how PP-2 happened.
- **Test requirement (met):** `50_policy_role_scope_test.sql` (5) — the class guard, a positive control so the zero is not drawn from an empty universe, and two assertions that the expressions survived untouched.

### CUR-1 — `integration_cursors` grants contradicted its own RLS (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-27, `202607055100`)
- **Category:** misleading contract · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** the only table in `public` with RLS enabled and zero policies, and invisible to `01_rls_coverage_test.sql` because that guard is driven by NOT NULL `tenant_id` and this table has no tenant column at all — it is the global n8n outbox cursor. `authenticated` held `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on a table it can touch in no way.
- **Impact:** behaviour was already correct and fail-closed (RLS with no policy denies every non-owner role). The defect is that the privilege layer and the policy layer stated **opposite intentions about the same table** — which is how the next engineer reasons their way into a real one, by reading the GRANT and "fixing" the missing policy to match. The grants were also live exposure waiting on one `disable row level security`.
- **Remediation (applied):** `revoke all … from anon, authenticated`, plus an explicit deny-all `platform_only` policy stating the intent where a reader looks (the SPEC-158 shape), plus a table comment.
- **Test requirement (met):** `51_role_change_audit_test.sql` §12–13.

### API-1 — every `app.*` RPC and every `reporting` view is unreachable over HTTP (discovered 2026-08-27, WP-04-E) — **CLOSED 2026-08-28, see the closure record at the end of this file**
- **Category:** architecture / reachability · **Severity:** Critical (the database is complete and no client can call it) · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence (live against Primary, not inferred from config):** `POST /rest/v1/rpc/document_bucket` → `404 PGRST202` *"Searched for the function public.document_bucket … no matches were found in the schema cache"*; the same call with `Content-Profile: app` → `406 PGRST106` *"Invalid schema: app. Only the following schemas are exposed: public, graphql_public"*; control call `POST /rest/v1/tenants` → `401 42501 permission denied for table tenants`, proving the API and schema cache are healthy and the 404s mean "schema not exposed". Before this package `public` held exactly one function — `moddatetime`, from an extension — and zero views. All 8 `reporting` views are in the same position.
- **Root cause:** ORVION deliberately keeps its logic in the private `app` schema (correct), and PostgREST only serves `public` and `graphql_public`. No wrapper layer was ever built, and nothing noticed because **every RPC test in the suite calls `app.something(...)` as a database session**. SQL and HTTP are different doors; only one was ever tried. 600 passing assertions coexisted with an entirely unreachable API.
- **Impact:** no client — web app, mobile, n8n, Edge Function — can invoke `app.upload_document`, `app.create_booking_item`, `app.assign_user_role`, `app.redeem_license_token`, or read `reporting.my_sales_performance`. §25 item 1 ("core CRM workflows work end-to-end") cannot be true until this is closed.
- **Decision required:** none. This is engineering.
- **Dependency:** none blocking.
- **Remediation:** thin `public` wrappers, one per client-facing RPC and view, `security invoker` (NEVER `security definer` — that would check the inner EXECUTE grant against the wrapper's owner and become a privilege-escalation bridge into `app`), granted deliberately per role. Explicitly **rejected**: exposing the `app` schema, which would publish all 137 functions including internal helpers that RLS policies require `authenticated` to execute (`app.has_permission`, `app.current_tenant_id`, `app.visible_branch_ids`, `app.item_financials`) — a permission-probing oracle and a frozen internal API. First slice delivered in `202607055400` (`public.claim_storage_actions`, `public.resolve_storage_finding`) because WP-04-E could not run without it.
- **Test requirement:** `52_public_api_and_executor_contract_test.sql` §1–3 pins the wrapper rule for every current and future wrapper. Reachability itself cannot be proven in pgTAP — it must be proven over HTTP, as `scripts/verify_storage_end_to_end.ps1` does.

### GRANT-1 — the default ACL grants `anon` EXECUTE on every new `public` function (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-27, `202607055400`)
- **Category:** security / privilege model · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** `pg_default_acl` for grantor `postgres`, schema `public`, objtype `f`: `{postgres=X/postgres, anon=X/postgres, authenticated=X/postgres, service_role=X/postgres}`. `revoke execute … from public` removes the PUBLIC pseudo-role grant, which was never present, and leaves `anon=X` untouched. The first version of `202607055400` did exactly that and `52_public_api_and_executor_contract_test.sql` failed with both endpoints executable by `anon`.
- **Root cause:** **SPEC-124's defect class recurring for FUNCTIONS after SPEC-124 fixed it for TABLES** — Supabase's hosted default ACL granting more than ORVION intends. Invisible until ORVION created its first `public` function, which happened in this package.
- **Impact:** not exploitable at discovery — the wrappers are `security invoker`, so `anon` still fails the inner `app.*` EXECUTE check. But that is the second lock holding while the first stands open, and one `security definer` slip in any future wrapper converts it into a real breach. It also leaks endpoint existence through the error.
- **Remediation (applied):** `alter default privileges in schema public revoke execute on functions from anon, authenticated`, so every one of API-1's ~130 future wrappers is private on creation and must be granted deliberately — the same "explicit grants only" model `10_grant_model_test.sql` enforces for tables. Both existing wrappers additionally revoked explicitly.
- **Test requirement (met):** `52_public_api_and_executor_contract_test.sql` §4–7, including the default-privileges assertion itself.

### FND-1 — a failed storage action was permanently hidden (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-27, `202607055300`)
- **Category:** correctness / observability · **Severity:** High · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** WP-04-D registered the resolution code `failed` as *"The executor attempted the storage action and it failed. Stays discoverable."* and shipped a resolver that sets `resolved_at = now()` for every valid code including `failed`. Both were live and contradictory.
- **Root cause:** the contract was written in a catalog description and the behaviour in a function, with nothing consuming either. Found only when WP-04-E built the consumer.
- **Impact:** reconciliation reopens resolved findings only for `missing_object` and `orphan_object` — never `retention_expired`. So a retention action that failed once was marked resolved, never reopened and never retried: the object survives forever with nothing open to say so. **This is PH8-1's shape**, a stranded-and-silent item, which the programme has already paid for once.
- **Remediation (applied):** `failed` is now an attempt record, not a resolution — it increments `attempt_count`, stores `last_error` and `last_attempt_at`, and returns without touching `resolved_at`, so the finding stays open and claimable. The catalog description was corrected to match.
- **Test requirement (met):** `52_public_api_and_executor_contract_test.sql` §13–15 (open, counted, and re-claimed on the very next run) and `scripts/verify_storage_end_to_end.ps1` over HTTP.

### SCHED-1 — nothing can invoke the storage executor on a schedule (discovered 2026-08-27, WP-04-E)
- **Category:** operational · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: n8n Schedule → HTTP → Edge Function; `pg_net` stays uninstalled.** ENGINEERING task, gated behind Phase 8. **Superseded:** BLOCKED — EXTERNAL DEPENDENCY · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** `pg_cron` is installed and cannot make HTTP calls; `pg_net` is **available** (0.20.4) but deliberately **not installed** — see the INTENTIONAL note below. Supabase's dashboard Cron feature schedules Edge Function calls through `pg_net`, so it is unavailable for the same reason. The Edge Function is deployed and ACTIVE on Primary and is invoked by nothing.
- **Impact:** none today (retention is undecided per RET-1, so no `retention_expired` finding can exist, so the executor would have nothing to do). It becomes real the day RET-1 is decided.
- **Decision required:** how the executor is triggered — an owner-configured schedule calling the function URL with the service key, an n8n Schedule Trigger, or invocation from the future client.
- **Why `pg_net` is not simply installed (INTENTIONAL):** it would require storing a service key inside the database to authenticate to the Storage API — ORVION's first stored platform credential — and would make any compromised `SECURITY DEFINER` function an HTTP client holding platform authority. The blast radius is far larger than the scheduling convenience.
- **Test requirement:** once triggered, the executor's own idempotency guarantees already hold (proven, 36 HTTP assertions).

### ORPH-1 — orphan objects are detected and never destroyed (recorded 2026-08-27, WP-04-E)
- **Category:** business / operations · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** `app.claim_storage_actions` hands out only `retention_expired` actions. `orphan_object` findings are surfaced and never acted upon.
- **Root cause / reasoning:** an orphan is bytes no row references, and the reason may be that a metadata transaction failed — which makes that object a customer's document ORVION has forgotten rather than garbage. Destroying it on sight is irreversible and makes recovery impossible. Proven mitigating fact: a tenant **cannot** create an orphan through the API — the storage INSERT policy requires a matching `document_versions` row (`scripts/verify_storage_end_to_end.ps1`, "upload with no metadata row is REFUSED"), so an orphan implies a platform-side failure worth a human look.
- **Decision required:** whether orphans are destroyed after a quarantine period, exported first, or reviewed individually.

### RBAC-2 — `MANAGE_ROLES` and `MANAGE_PERMISSIONS` are held by roles and enforced nowhere (discovered 2026-08-27, WP-04-E sweep)
- **Category:** misleading contract / canon conflict · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** OPEN · **Owner:** owner (canon) then engineering
- **Evidence:** a scan of every `app` function body and every `public` RLS policy for permission-key literals finds no reference to `MANAGE_ROLES` or `MANAGE_PERMISSIONS`. Both are assigned to roles in `role_permissions`. RBAC-1 established that role assignment is guarded by `MANAGE_USERS` — in `app.assign_user_role`, in the `user_role_assignments` RLS policies, and now in `app.emit_role_change`.
- **Impact:** a role holding either permission gains nothing. Canon 28 implies a capability that does not exist, and an administrator reading the permission matrix would reasonably conclude role administration is separable from user administration. It is not.
- **Conflict:** BUSINESS INTENT — role administration is a distinct, more sensitive capability than user administration. CANON — grants these permissions. IMPLEMENTATION — charges `MANAGE_USERS` everywhere. OBSERVED — the two permissions are inert.
- **Decision required:** either (a) role administration begins charging `MANAGE_ROLES` (an engineering change across `app.assign_user_role`, `app.revoke_user_role`, `app.emit_role_change` and three RLS policies, plus a role-permission seed review), or (b) the two permissions are retired as obsolete and canon 28 is amended. Recording, not deciding.
- **Test requirement:** whichever is chosen, `51_role_change_audit_test.sql` gains a positive/negative pair on the governing permission.

### PERM-1 — three permissions are held by no role and checked by nothing (discovered 2026-08-27, WP-04-E sweep)
- **Category:** vocabulary · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** BLOCKED — ARCHITECTURAL DEPENDENCY · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** `ACCESS_API_FULL`, `ACCESS_API_READ_ONLY` and `VIEW_ADVANCED_DASHBOARDS` appear in no `role_permissions` row and in no function or policy.
- **Assessment — premature, not dead.** `ACCESS_API_*` are plan-tier entitlements from canon 09 that can only become meaningful once there is an API surface to gate; **API-1 is that dependency**, and API-1 must decide whether the public endpoint surface is gated by them. `VIEW_ADVANCED_DASHBOARDS` belongs with **PLAN-1** (the three undefined "Limited" plan ceilings).
- **Distinct from RBAC-2**, which is about permissions that ARE granted but enforce nothing. These are granted to nobody.
- **Test requirement:** when API-1 lands, either a gating assertion or an explicit retirement.

### API-1 — CLOSED 2026-08-28 (`202607055500`)
- **Category:** architecture / reachability · **Severity:** was Critical · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Resolution:** executed as a capability audit rather than a mechanical wrapping. 137 `app` functions minus 20 trigger functions, 7 RLS helpers, 4 reporting-view helpers, 6 `platform_*` and 14 not granted to `authenticated` left 86 candidates; **15 were excluded by hand as internal helpers** and **71 exposed**, together with the 8 `reporting` views. Every wrapper is `security invoker` with `set search_path = ''` and named-argument delegation, explicitly revoked from `public, anon, authenticated` and granted to `authenticated`.
- **Why the exclusions were the whole point:** `record_event` is the audit spine's sole writer (WP-00). Exposed, any authenticated user could mint arbitrary registered event types about arbitrary entities in their own tenant — audit forgery through the front door. `authorize`, `mfa_satisfied` and `requires_mfa` would have been a permission-probing oracle. The remaining eleven are pure derivations whose exposure would freeze implementation detail into a public contract. This is the concrete reason "expose the `app` schema" was rejected: one setting publishes all of them.
- **Evidence of completion (live, Primary, correct argument names):** `upload_document` → 401 42501 (live, `anon` refused); `create_customer` → 401 42501; `record_event`, `authorize`, `has_permission`, `current_tenant_id`, `platform_activate_subscription` → 404 PGRST202 (genuinely absent). Local and Primary API surfaces proven identical by hash `731cbd41ce480d714802b3de9a255c7a` over every signature, result type, `prosecdef`, `proconfig` and grantee list (81 objects).
- **Test requirement (met):** `53_api_surface_test.sql` (12) pins the exposed set BY NAME, asserts every exclusion explicitly, forbids `SECURITY DEFINER` on any endpoint, forbids `anon` EXECUTE, requires pinned `search_path`, requires `security_invoker` on all 8 views, and proves behaviourally that a user with no role is refused BY THE ENDPOINT. Reachability itself is proven in `scripts/verify_api_end_to_end.ps1` (29 assertions over HTTP).
- **Permanent consequence for deployment:** any migration that adds or changes a `public` function or view must be followed by `notify pgrst, 'reload schema'`. Until that runs, the new API is invisible while every guard stays green. Recorded in the plan's deployment criteria.

### LEAD-2 — the `lead_source` catalog has no walk-in value (discovered 2026-08-28, Phase B journey)
- **Category:** business vocabulary · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** `lead_source` holds exactly ten values — `google_ads_call`, `google_ads_form`, `direct_call`, `whatsapp`, `website_form`, `manual_entry`, `meta_ads`, `referral`, `repeat_customer`, `other`. There is no `walk_in`.
- **Impact:** the owner directive names "walk-in customer" as a primary scenario for an Egyptian travel agency, and a walk-in is a genuinely distinct acquisition channel from a phone call or a manual back-office entry. Recording it as `manual_entry` (what the journey script now does) loses attribution fidelity: a branch cannot later measure footfall against advertising.
- **Root cause:** canon's lead-source taxonomy was written around digital acquisition.
- **Decision required:** whether `walk_in` becomes a catalog value. Not invented here — canon owns the acquisition taxonomy, and a fabricated source code would silently corrupt attribution reporting.
- **Remediation:** one `catalog_values` insert plus a canon amendment, once decided.
- **Test requirement:** the journey script switches to `walk_in` and asserts the lead's source survives to `reporting.lead_performance`.

### RBAC-3 — an employee cannot assign leads (investigated 2026-08-28, INTENTIONAL)
- **Category:** role model · **Severity:** n/a · **Status:** INTENTIONAL · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** the `employee` role holds `CREATE_LEAD`, `CLOSE_LEAD`, `VIEW_ASSIGNED_LEADS`, `CREATE_QUOTATION`, `SEND_QUOTATION`, `ACCEPT_QUOTATION`, `CREATE_BOOKING`, `CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM`, `UPDATE_BOOKING_ITEM_STATUS` — and not `ASSIGN_LEAD`. The first run of `verify_api_end_to_end.ps1` failed on it.
- **Why it is NOT a defect, checked before concluding:** the `leads` RLS policy grants visibility on `owner_user_id` **or** `assigned_user_id`, and `app.create_lead` stamps the creator as owner. An employee who creates a lead can therefore see it, work it, quote it and book it without any assignment. Deciding who *else* works a lead is a supervisory act, and `assign_lead`/`reassign_lead` are correctly reserved.
- **Recorded because the investigation is the value:** the directive forbids solving a missing permission by granting more privilege. The script now asserts both halves — the employee refused, the owner assigning as the positive control — so a future session cannot mistake this for a gap and "fix" it by widening the role.

### FIN-2 — an employee could not REQUEST finance approval, because requesting was treated as approving (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-28, `202607055600`)
- **Category:** authorization / business capability · **Severity:** High (the workflow was unusable by the only role that needs it) · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence (live, over HTTP, as an employee holding `CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM`):** `POST /rest/v1/rpc/request_finance_approval` → `403 permission denied: APPROVE_FINANCE`. `app.request_finance_approval` charges `CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM` and then sets `booking_items.finance_approval_status_code = 'pending'`; `app.guard_booking_item_financials` charged `APPROVE_FINANCE` on **any** change to that column. `APPROVE_FINANCE` is held by ceo, finance_manager and owner only.
- **Root cause:** the guard treated one column as one privileged act when it carries two — `null/rejected/cancelled → pending` is REQUESTING (the salesperson's act) and `pending → approved/rejected/cancelled` is DECIDING (finance's act). A control that cannot tell them apart must refuse both, and it did.
- **Impact:** the entire finance-approval workflow was dead. Only a holder of `APPROVE_FINANCE` could open a request — i.e. only the approver could ask themselves for approval. Any discount or exception requiring sign-off was unreachable for employees and senior employees.
- **Remediation (applied):** the guard now distinguishes the two transitions. Requesting costs `CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM` plus the same assignment scope every other financial edit on the table requires, so an employee still cannot open a request against a colleague's item. Deciding still costs `APPROVE_FINANCE`. `cost_locked_at` is untouched and remains approver-only.
- **Test requirement (met):** `scripts/verify_journey_branches.ps1` — employee requests ✓, employee cannot approve their own ✓, finance can ✓ (the positive control, without which the denial proves nothing).

### TASK-1 — an employee could create and complete a task but not START one (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-28, `202607055600`)
- **Category:** authorization / business capability · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** `app.advance_task` and `app.status_transitions` both mapped `open → in_progress` and `overdue → in_progress` to `ASSIGN_TASK`, held only by branch_manager, department_manager, ceo and owner. Every other task transition required `COMPLETE_TASK`, which employee and senior_employee also hold.
- **Root cause:** starting a task was conflated with assigning one. They are different acts with different actors; `app.assign_task` already exists and is where `ASSIGN_TASK` belongs.
- **Impact:** `in_progress` was unreachable for the people whose work it describes, turning the task board into a two-state system and making follow-up progress invisible.
- **Remediation (applied):** both `→ in_progress` transitions now require `COMPLETE_TASK`, in **both** sources (see TRANS-1). Nothing else in `advance_task` changed.
- **Test requirement (met):** `scripts/verify_journey_branches.ps1` (create → start → complete as an employee) and `54_transition_permission_parity_test.sql`.

### TRANS-1 — status-transition permissions are duplicated in two sources (discovered 2026-08-28; drift FIXED, duplication OPEN)
- **Category:** duplicate source of truth · **Severity:** High (it already caused a fix to silently fail) · **Status:** IN PROGRESS · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** every status transition's required permission is stated twice — as an inline `VALUES` list inside each `app.advance_*` function (which also carries the EVENT code), and as a row in `app.status_transitions` (which `app.enforce_status_transition`, the BEFORE UPDATE trigger on every status-bearing table, actually reads). Proven by diffing all nine `advance_*` functions against the table.
- **Impact, demonstrated rather than theorised:** the TASK-1 fix changed only the function. The employee was **still** refused `permission denied: ASSIGN_TASK`, because the trigger reads the table — and the two sources were now in live disagreement. A fix that appears applied and is not is worse than no fix.
- **Why the duplication is not simply removed:** `app.status_transitions` has no event column, so the function's list is currently the only home for the event code emitted on each transition. Deleting either copy today loses information.
- **Remediation:** give `app.status_transitions` an `event_type_code` column, make it the single source, and have every `advance_*` read from it. Until then the duplication is **guarded**: `54_transition_permission_parity_test.sql` fails the moment the two disagree, and also fails if a function offers a transition the trigger would refuse.
- **Test requirement (met for the drift, pending for the de-duplication):** parity assertions with a positive control so they cannot pass on an empty parse.

### RLS-1 — a read permission confers write authority on eleven tables (discovered 2026-08-28, Phase C policy sweep) — **MERGED into SEC-1 2026-08-28; see the closure record at the end of this file**
- **Category:** authorization model / misleading contract · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** OBSOLETE (merged into SEC-1) · **Owner:** —
- **Evidence:** `bookings`, `booking_items`, `leads`, `quotations`, `tasks`, `documents`, `document_links`, `complaints`, `conversations`, `customer_notes` and `service_requests` each carry a single `for ALL` policy whose `USING` and `WITH CHECK` are identical and both include `app.has_tenant_wide_read()`, which is `app.has_permission('VIEW_ALL_BRANCHES')`.
- **Impact — stated precisely, because the distinction matters:** **not exploitable today.** Only `owner` and `ceo` hold `VIEW_ALL_BRANCHES`, and both already hold every relevant write permission, so nobody gains anything they did not have. The defect is **latent**: the day any read-oriented role (a read-only auditor, a reporting account, or `system_administrator`, which today holds no permissions at all) is granted `VIEW_ALL_BRANCHES`, it silently acquires INSERT and UPDATE on eleven tables — a defect that arrives without anyone editing a policy.
- **Root cause:** a `for ALL` policy uses one expression for reading and writing, and the expression chosen names the read concept.
- **Remediation:** introduce `app.has_tenant_wide_write()` (identical membership today) and use it in `WITH CHECK`, so the two concepts are separable the day they diverge. Behaviour-preserving. Scoped as its own package because it touches the most heavily tested policies in the system and deserves a full cross-path re-verification rather than a session-end edit.
- **Test requirement:** a fixture role holding only `VIEW_ALL_BRANCHES` must be refused INSERT and UPDATE on all eleven tables, with owner as the positive control.

### TASK-2 — a started task emits `task_assigned` (discovered 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** audit lineage / vocabulary · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION (canon owns the state machines) · **Owner:** owner then engineering
- **Evidence:** `open → in_progress` and `overdue → in_progress` emit `task_assigned`. Nothing was assigned; the task was started by the person who already held it.
- **Impact:** the audit trail misdescribes what happened. Distinct from EVT-2 (registered codes with no producer) — this is a producer emitting the **wrong** code, which is the more misleading half.
- **Decision required:** whether `task_started` joins the event vocabulary. Canon 26 owns the state machines and `202607049100` enforces a closed registry, so adding a code to make a transition read correctly is a vocabulary decision, not a permission bug. Deliberately not invented during the TASK-1 fix.
- **Remediation when decided:** one catalog insert, one registry-array line, and two event codes in `app.advance_task`.

### RBAC-3 — an employee cannot assign leads (investigated 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** role model · **Severity:** n/a · **Status:** INTENTIONAL · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** the `employee` role holds `CREATE_LEAD` and not `ASSIGN_LEAD`; `app.assign_lead` refuses them.
- **Why it is not a defect:** the `leads` RLS policy grants visibility on `owner_user_id` **or** `assigned_user_id`, and `app.create_lead` stamps the creator as owner. An employee therefore sees, works, quotes and books their own lead without any assignment. Deciding who *else* works a lead is a supervisory act.
- **Recorded because the investigation is the value:** the directive forbids solving a missing permission by granting more privilege. `scripts/verify_api_end_to_end.ps1` asserts both halves — the employee refused, the owner assigning as the positive control — so a future session cannot mistake this for a gap and widen the role.

### RBAC-4 — a department manager could not see their own department's bookings (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-28, `202607055800`)
- **Category:** role model / authorization · **Severity:** High (the role could not perform its defining function) · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence (live, over HTTP):** `DEPTMGR bookings=0 pipeline=0` with `VIEW_DEPARTMENT_QUEUE=true`, `visible_depts=1`, `visible_branches=1`, and the booking sitting in exactly that department and branch. The `bookings` policy gates department reads on `VIEW_DEPARTMENT_RECORDS`; `role_permissions` granted it to `employee` and `senior_employee` and **not** to `department_manager`. `branch_manager`, `ceo` and `owner` satisfy the other disjunct, so the omission landed on exactly one role.
- **Root cause:** permissions are seeded role by role, so nothing ever displays the containment relationship between a manager and their staff. The omission is invisible in a permission matrix read top to bottom.
- **Impact:** a department manager saw FEWER bookings than the employees reporting to them — zero, in their own department. The same permission gates booking items and quotations, so their department's sales items and quotations were equally invisible. The three objects the role exists to supervise.
- **Why it is a defect and not policy invented here:** canon 28 defines the role as "Can manage employees and work inside their department and branch only", and its own amendment note introduces `VIEW_DEPARTMENT_RECORDS` as "the department-read gate for those three [bookings, booking items, quotations]". The same note is explicit about where department managers ARE excluded — note 3, `VIEW_BRANCH_DATA`: "Department managers are still excluded" — which is about BRANCH-wide reading and is untouched. The grant restores the scope canon already describes and gives the manager exactly what every employee in that department already held.
- **Remediation (applied):** grant `VIEW_DEPARTMENT_RECORDS` to `department_manager`, idempotently.
- **Test requirement (met):** `55_role_coherence_test.sql` guards the CLASS — any department read gate an employee or senior employee holds, the department manager must hold; and the department manager must still hold no branch-wide read, so the fix cannot creep upward. Plus `scripts/verify_role_journeys.ps1` over HTTP.

### TASK-2 — a started task emitted `task_assigned` (discovered 2026-08-28, FIXED `202607055700`)
- **Category:** audit lineage · **Severity:** Medium (it corrupted assignment counts) · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** `app.advance_task` emitted `task_assigned` for `open -> in_progress` and `overdue -> in_progress`. `app.assign_task` already emits the same code for actual assignment. Two different business actions produced the same event, so the audit stream inflated assignments and could not be used to reconstruct who was given what work.
- **Canon check before acting:** `_ORVION_CANONICAL/26_state_machines.md` describes `open -> in_progress` as "Responsible employee starts work" and `overdue -> in_progress` as "Work resumed on an overdue task" — which independently **confirms the TASK-1 permission fix**. Its Required Events list is five long and contains **no start event**.
- **Remediation (applied):** the two start transitions emit **nothing**. Adding a `task_started` code would be engineering inventing business vocabulary, which the directive forbids; canon owns the state machines and deliberately enumerates five. A missing record is recoverable, a false one silently corrupts every consumer that trusts it, and `tasks.task_status_code`/`updated_at` still record the change. Required one structural change: transition validity now comes from `FOUND` rather than from `v_event is null`, so the two legitimate eventless transitions are not mistaken for invalid ones.
- **Test requirement (met):** `54_transition_permission_parity_test.sql`, whose regex was widened to accept `null::text` in the event slot — a pattern matching only quoted events would have silently stopped comparing exactly those two rows, which is how TRANS-1 arose.

### TASK-3 — canon defines no "task started" event (recorded 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** vocabulary / canon · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** canon 26's Task State Machine lists `task_created`, `task_assigned`, `task_completed`, `task_cancelled`, `task_overdue`. There is no event for entering `in_progress`, and TASK-2 removed the false one that was being emitted.
- **Impact:** the moment work starts is no longer in the event spine. It remains visible in `tasks.task_status_code` and `tasks.updated_at`, so nothing is lost operationally; what is lost is a queryable "when did work begin" in the audit stream.
- **Decision required:** whether `task_started` joins the event vocabulary. This is a canon amendment (canon 26 owns the state machines; `202607049100` enforces a closed registry), not an engineering choice.
- **Remediation when decided:** one catalog insert, one registry-array entry, and two event codes in `app.advance_task`.

### SEC-1 — REPRODUCED 2026-08-28: RLS scopes rows, not capability (RLS-1 merged in)
- **Category:** authorization architecture · **Severity:** High · **Status:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS/ARCHITECTURAL DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence (reproduced live, not theorised):** a role holding only `VIEW_ALL_BRANCHES` and `VIEW_ASSIGNED_LEADS`, with `CREATE_BOOKING = false` and `CREATE_TASK = false`, acting as `authenticated` by direct DML: `update bookings set title=...` **succeeded**; `update tasks set title=...` **succeeded**; `insert into customers ...` **succeeded**. `customers` is not one of RLS-1's eleven `has_tenant_wide_read` tables, so the class is wider than RLS-1 described.
- **Measured exposure:** 59 tables accept a direct `INSERT` from `authenticated`; 19 carry any permission-checking trigger, and most of those cover only archive or status transitions; **40 have no capability check anywhere on the direct write path**.
- **Root cause:** capability is enforced in the `app.*` RPCs — which direct DML bypasses — and in a partial set of guard triggers. RLS was never designed to carry it and does not.
- **Impact:** any authenticated tenant user can create and edit most records regardless of the permissions their role holds. Not a cross-tenant breach (RLS holds the tenant boundary) and not currently reachable by a real role, since every seeded role that can read also holds the corresponding write permissions — but the boundary depends on the seed rather than on the schema.
- **Decision required:** either (a) revoke `authenticated`'s table writes and make the RPCs the only door — which requires converting them to `SECURITY DEFINER`, changing the whole authorization model — or (b) enforce canon 28's matrix on every table with a capability trigger, which requires deciding, table by table, which permission governs creation for objects canon does not name. Both are architectural; neither may be invented.
- **Remediation in the meantime (applied):** the exposure is **capped by assertion** — `10_grant_model_test.sql` asserts at most 59 insertable tables and at most 40 without a capability check. The numbers may fall; they cannot rise without failing the suite.
- **Test requirement:** on resolution, the reproduction above becomes a failing-then-passing test and both ceilings drop to 0.
- **Supersedes:** RLS-1, which is its concrete instance on eleven `for ALL` policies and is closed as merged rather than tracked separately.

### RLS-1 — read scope is write scope on eleven tables — MERGED into SEC-1 (2026-08-28)
- **Category:** authorization model · **Severity:** — · **Status:** OBSOLETE (merged) · **Owner:** —
- **Why merged:** the Phase C reproduction showed the defect is not confined to the eleven `for ALL` policies that name `has_tenant_wide_read()`. The read-only role also inserted into `customers`, whose policy is plain tenant isolation. Fixing the eleven would have left the class intact and created a false sense of closure. The finding, its evidence and its remediation now live under SEC-1.

### FIN-3 — money could be written by anyone who could see the record it belonged to (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-28, `202607055900`)
- **Category:** financial integrity / authorization · **Severity:** High · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence (reproduced before fixed):** an `employee` with `RECORD_PAYMENT = false`, acting as `authenticated` by direct DML against a booking they own, inserted a 999,999 EGP payment. `app.record_payment` charges `RECORD_PAYMENT`, held by ceo, finance_manager and owner only.
- **Root cause:** every money table's policy DID name a permission — `VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS`, a READ permission — sitting in an OR with a pure visibility test. The effective rule was "you may write money about anything you can see". `quotation_items` did not even do that: tenant plus parent visibility. This is the RLS-1 pattern (a read predicate authorizing writes) recurring in the family where it costs most.
- **Why it was an omission and not a design choice:** `journal_entries` and `journal_entry_lines` already required `has_permission('CREATE_JOURNAL_ENTRY')` in WITH CHECK. ORVION knew the correct pattern; it was applied to the ledger and not to the cash.
- **Impact:** any tenant user who could see a booking, quotation or payment could create or alter money against it — payments never received, invoices never agreed, refunds never authorised, quotation lines at any price. Not cross-tenant (RLS held that boundary) and fully audited, but financially wrong.
- **Remediation (applied):** `app.guard_financial_capability`, a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger on payments, payment_allocations, receipts, refunds, invoices and quotation_items, charging exactly the permission each table's own RPC charges — read out of the functions, not chosen. INSERT always; UPDATE only when a monetary column changes, so status advances and verification stamps keep their existing authority. Session-less paths exempt from the check, never from the record. A trigger rather than a policy amendment because these are `for ALL` policies whose long expressions are shared between USING and WITH CHECK, and retranscribing them is how PP-2 lost a branch.
- **Test requirement (met):** `56_financial_write_capability_test.sql` (12) — every denial paired with a control proving the actor could otherwise reach the row (the employee owns the booking), the frontline's own `CREATE_QUOTATION` still works, finance's writes persist, and the system path still writes.

### FIN-4 — an approval request could name someone else as its requester (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-28, `202607055900`)
- **Category:** audit integrity · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** `approval_requests.scope_insert` checks only `tenant_id = current_tenant_id()`, and `requested_by` was an ordinary caller-supplied column.
- **Impact:** any tenant user could open an approval request attributed to a colleague. The approval record is the evidence of who asked for a financial exception, so a forged requester corrupts precisely the trail an approval exists to leave.
- **Remediation (applied):** WP-00's shape — DERIVE, DO NOT VALIDATE. `requested_by` is overwritten with `app.current_user_id()`, making the forgery unrepresentable on the RPC path and the direct path alike, rather than merely refused.
- **Test requirement (met):** `56_financial_write_capability_test.sql` §10–11 — the employee opens a request naming finance as requester and it is recorded against the employee.

### FIN-5 — which permission may OPEN each approval type is undefined (recorded 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** authorization / canon · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: no opening permission is defined for an approval type that has no live producer.** See the 2026-08-29 narrowing. **Superseded:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** `approval_requests.scope_update` switches on `approval_type_code` for the DECISION (`finance_execution_approval` → APPROVE_FINANCE, `subscription_approval` → REVIEW_SUBSCRIPTION_PAYMENT, else REVIEW_APPROVAL_REQUEST). There is no corresponding map for opening a request; `scope_insert` requires only tenant membership.
- **Impact:** bounded by FIN-4 — a request can no longer be misattributed — but any tenant user may still open one of any type. FIN-2 established that a finance-execution request costs `CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM`; the other types have no such precedent.
- **Decision required:** the per-type permission map for opening a request. Deliberately not invented while fixing FIN-4, because attribution needed no decision and this does.
- **Test requirement:** a positive/negative pair per approval type once the map exists.

### SYSADMIN-1 — the `system_administrator` role holds zero permissions (recorded 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** role model · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: the role is RESERVED and documented; invent no permissions for it.** ENGINEERING task — say so, and assert it holds none by design. **Superseded:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** `role_permissions` contains no row for `system_administrator`. Every other seeded role has at least two. The role is referenced by `app.requires_mfa`, which lists it among the roles requiring step-up — so something expects it to exist and to matter.
- **Impact:** none operationally: a user holding only this role can do nothing, which is fail-closed. The defect is that the role is a **misleading contract** — an administrator assigning it would reasonably expect it to confer administrative capability, and it confers nothing.
- **Decision required:** whether the role is intentionally empty (and should say so), obsolete (and should be deactivated), reserved for a future capability, or missing its canon definition. Deliberately not granted anything: inventing a system-administrator capability set would be inventing business policy, and `app.requires_mfa` referencing it is not evidence of what it should be able to do.
- **Test requirement:** once decided, either an assertion that it holds no permissions by design, or its permission set with positive and negative controls.

### SEC-1 (PARTIAL RESOLUTION 2026-08-28, `202607056000`) — write capability applied wherever canon already answers it
- **Category:** authorization architecture · **Severity:** High · **Status:** IN PROGRESS (residue BLOCKED — ARCHITECTURAL DECISION) · **Owner:** owner for the residue; engineering for the rest
- **What was resolved:** FIN-3's rule generalised across the schema. For every table `authenticated` may INSERT with no capability enforcement, every `app.*` function that inserts into it was examined together with the permission that function authorizes. **One RPC / one permission → guarded. Two RPCs / two permissions → the UNION guarded, which is exactly what the existing code permits (requiring both would forbid writes ORVION performs today; picking one would silently retire a working path). No RPC, or an RPC that authorizes nothing → no evidence-based answer, left open rather than guessed.**
- **Nine tables guarded:** `approval_requests` (CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM), `conversation_messages` (SEND_MESSAGE), `customer_contact_methods` (CREATE_CUSTOMER), `customer_identity_signals` (CREATE_CUSTOMER), `customer_identity_merges` (MERGE_CUSTOMER_IDENTITY), `internal_supplier_links` (ASSIGN_SUPPLIER), `offline_conversions` (MANAGE_MARKETING_CAMPAIGN), `document_links` (UPLOAD_DOCUMENT or MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS), `lead_assignments` (ASSIGN_LEAD or REASSIGN_LEAD).
- **The exception, and why it exists:** `approval_requests` carries the guard on INSERT only. `app.review_finance_approval` updates it and `finance_manager` does **not** hold `CREATE_BOOKING_ITEM`, so charging the insert permission on UPDATE would have broken the approval workflow FIN-2 repaired one migration earlier. Its UPDATE is already guarded by `scope_update`, which switches on `approval_type_code`. Verified before writing rather than discovered after breaking.
- **Exposure, re-measured honestly:** tables `authenticated` may INSERT: 59. With no capability **trigger**: 36 → **27**. With **no capability enforcement of any kind** (neither trigger nor a policy WITH CHECK naming a non-VIEW permission): **13**. The previously reported 40 counted triggers only and was wrong; 23 was the corrected figure before this migration.
- **The residue of 13, classified individually rather than counted:**
  - *The caller's own auth artifacts, already owner-scoped by policy* — `otp_challenges`, `totp_enrollments`, `trusted_devices`. Capability here is "being that user", which the policy enforces.
  - *System-written* — `attribution_clicks`, `notifications`, `notification_deliveries`, `offline_conversion_deliveries`, `usage_counters`. Arguably these should not be `authenticated`-writable at all.
  - *No RPC authorizes anything* — `branch_business_hours`, `company_assets`, `financial_accounts`, `holidays`, `lead_interactions`. `app.record_lead_interaction` and `app.capture_attribution_click` charge no permission, so none can be derived; choosing one would be inventing business policy.
- **Decision required (unchanged in kind, narrowed in scope):** each of the three residue branches needs a different call — revoke `authenticated` writes on the system-written set, confirm the auth artifacts are correctly owner-scoped and need nothing more, and decide which permission (if any) governs the five with silent RPCs.
- **Test requirement (met for the resolved part):** `57_write_capability_map_test.sql` (12) — trainee refused and employee permitted on the **same row** so the two differ in exactly one variable; the union proven from both sides; the `approval_requests` UPDATE exclusion proven behaviourally *and* structurally (the trigger has no UPDATE bit); the map proven complete and the guard proven to refuse rather than pass on an unmapped table. Ceilings in `10_grant_model_test.sql` now pin 59 / 27 / 13 — they may fall, never rise.

### SEC-1 (RESIDUE CLOSED 2026-08-28, `202607056100`) — the thirteen were three different problems
- **Category:** authorization architecture · **Severity:** High · **Status:** NARROWED to one table (BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION) · **Owner:** owner for `lead_interactions`; engineering for the rest, and done
- **What the counting hid:** treating the thirteen as one number implied one answer. Investigating each individually produced three, and they required opposite actions — one group needed a permission added, one needed a grant *removed*, and one needed nothing at all.
- **Group 1, system-owned — the GRANT was revoked, not permissioned (five tables).** For `attribution_clicks`, `notifications`, `notification_deliveries`, `offline_conversion_deliveries` and `usage_counters`, every writer in the database is SECURITY DEFINER and **none of them is executable by `authenticated`**: `app.capture_attribution_click`, `app.claim_conversion_deliveries` and `app.record_conversion_delivery_result` are granted to `orvion_integration`; `app.process_lead_sla` to `service_role`; `notification_deliveries` and `usage_counters` have no writer whatsoever. A definer function runs as its owner, so the table grant was never what made those paths work — it was a second door only direct DML used. Revoking it therefore cannot break a legitimate write; it removes forged marketing clicks, forged notifications, forged delivery records and hand-edited usage meters. Two independent corroborations that ORVION already considered these system-owned: three of the five are on the subscription-write-gate exemption list, and canon 28 states plainly that "`usage_counters` is empty and counting is a separate additive mechanism".
- **The one exception inside that group:** `notifications` keeps a **column-level** `update (is_read, read_at)`. Reading and dismissing your own notification is a real user act with no RPC to perform it; revoking the whole UPDATE would have deleted a capability rather than closed a hole. The owner-scoped policy already pins `target_user_id = current_user_id()`, so this cannot reach a colleague's inbox — what it can no longer do is rewrite the title, body or subject of a notification the system sent.
- **Group 2, tenant configuration — guarded with the permission ORVION already charges for the same object (four tables).** These have no writer at all, so direct DML was the *only* path and it was open to every tenant user, a trainee included. `branch_business_hours` → **MANAGE_BRANCHES**, because `branches` — its parent — charges exactly that, and setting a branch's opening hours is configuring the branch. `holidays` → **MANAGE_BRANCHES or MANAGE_TENANT_SETTINGS**, because `branch_id` is nullable and both readings resolve to exactly {ceo, owner}, so the union honours both and widens nothing. `financial_accounts` and `company_assets` → **CREATE_JOURNAL_ENTRY**, because canon 33 groups them with `chart_of_accounts`/`journal_entries` and the finance transaction tables, and that is what those already charge. None of this grants anybody anything new; it narrows an ungoverned write to roles canon 28 already designates.
- **Group 3, INTENTIONAL rather than residue (three tables).** Canon 34 (§ "Applying Principles 1, 6, and 7") states it directly: `otp_challenges`, `totp_enrollments` and `trusted_devices` belong to the Human Identity, are keyed by `auth_user_id`, and "RLS for these tables is simply row-ownership by `auth.uid()`, with no tenant scoping". Ownership **is** the capability and canon says so; inventing a CRUD permission to make a metric reach zero would contradict it. The boundary is now proven behaviourally instead of trusted.
- **CORRECTED 2026-08-28 (same day, `202607056200`):** this entry originally said `lead_interactions` was the last open item because `app.record_lead_interaction` "authorizes nothing". **That was wrong.** The function enforces *"the assigned handler, OR ASSIGN_LEAD, plus MFA"* inline with `app.has_permission` and an explicit raise — an assignment-shaped rule that my permission-shaped detector (`app.authorize('PERM')`) could not see. So `lead_interactions` was never an open question: it was the SEC-1 pattern exactly, and it is now closed. See TRANS-2 below.
- **Exposure:** tables `authenticated` may INSERT **59 → 54**; with no capability trigger **27 → 18 → 17**; with no enforcement of any kind **13 → 4 → 3**. The three that remain — `otp_challenges`, `totp_enrollments`, `trusted_devices` — are INTENTIONAL by canon 34. **SEC-1 has no unexplained residue left.**
- **Test requirement (met):** `58_write_grants_and_config_capability_test.sql` (27) — the revoke proven at privilege level *and* the system path proven still open; the column-level UPDATE proven from both sides (mark-read succeeds, retitle refused); each config table denied to a trainee and permitted to an owner **on the same table**; the auth-artifact boundary proven by a foreign-identity write being refused and a foreign-identity UPDATE changing nothing — asserted on the row, because "no exception raised" would have passed even if the write had gone through. Ceilings in `10` now pin 54 / 18 / 4.

### ATTR-1 — `created_by` was a caller-supplied column on twenty tables (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-28, `202607056400`)
- **Category:** audit integrity / authorization · **Severity:** Medium–High · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** a sweep of every table `authenticated` may INSERT for columns naming an actor found that `archived_by` (10 tables), `document_versions.uploaded_by` and `approval_requests.requested_by` are already derived by triggers — and that **`created_by` was not, on twenty tables**: bookings, booking_items, customers, customer_notes, leads, quotations, invoices, payments, payment_allocations, receipts, refunds, journal_entries, documents, document_links, complaints, service_requests, tasks, catalog_values, exchange_rate_adjustments, user_branch_assignments.
- **Root cause:** every `app.*` RPC sets it from the session; direct DML did not have to. This is FIN-4's defect — the same shape, the same principle — recurring across a class instead of one column, and it was found by sweeping for the class rather than by tripping over an instance.
- **Impact:** any tenant user could create a customer, booking, invoice or payment **attributed to a colleague**, corrupting exactly the attribution an audit trail exists to carry. Not cross-tenant — RLS holds that boundary. On `documents` it is worse than misleading: `scope_isolation` reads `created_by = current_user_id()` as one of its **visibility grants**, so there the column is load-bearing for authorization.
- **Remediation (applied):** `app.derive_created_by()`, a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger on all twenty tables, following WP-00's shape — DERIVE, DO NOT VALIDATE — so the forgery is unrepresentable rather than merely refused. **Immutable on UPDATE**, because deriving only on INSERT would leave the value editable a moment later; safe because **no `app.*` or `public.*` function updates the column**, verified against every function body before the trigger was written. Session-less platform paths keep the attribution they set.
- **Deliberately NOT included, and recorded rather than guessed — ATTR-2:** `invoices.voided_by`, `journal_entries.voided_by`, `approval_requests.reviewed_by`, `subscription_payment_proofs.reviewed_by` / `uploaded_by`. Those are ACTION attributions, stamped when the action happens rather than when the row is created, so an insert-time derivation would be wrong for them. They need the same treatment at the moment of the action — a different trigger shape, and a separate package.
- **Test requirement (met):** `61_created_by_is_derived_test.sql` (8) — the employee CAN write the row (so the assertion is about attribution, not permission) while naming a colleague, and the row is attributed to the actual author; the value proven immutable on UPDATE; `documents` proven separately because there it is an authorization value; the session-less path proven to keep its own attribution; and **the class pinned** — every insertable table with a `created_by` column must carry the trigger, so a table added later without one fails the suite.

### ATTR-2 (RESOLVED 2026-08-28, `202607056500`) — action attributions, read one at a time instead of as a group
- **Category:** audit integrity · **Severity:** Low–Medium · **Status:** FIXED (2 of 5) · **Owner:** engineering
- **The point of the entry:** reading the five columns individually rather than treating them as one group split them three ways, and only two were attribution defects at all. Sweeping all five in because they matched a name pattern is how a fix becomes a defect.
- **DERIVED (`202607056500`):** `subscription_payment_proofs.uploaded_by` — set by `app.upload_subscription_payment_proof` from the session, and an INSERT-time attribution after all; it was excluded from ATTR-1 only because the column is not called `created_by`, which is a naming accident rather than a difference in kind. And `approval_requests.reviewed_by` — set by `app.review_finance_approval` alongside the status, now derived **on UPDATE when it changes**, so an unrelated edit to an already-decided request does not silently re-attribute the decision to whoever touched the row last.
- **NOT an attribution defect — see VOID-1:** `invoices.voided_by` and `journal_entries.voided_by`. Nothing in the database writes them, and `app.status_transitions` has no rows for either table. Voiding is unimplemented; deriving an attribution for an action nobody can perform would dress a missing capability as a solved one.
- **STRUCTURALLY UNFILLABLE — see SPP-3:** `subscription_payment_proofs.reviewed_by`.
- **Test requirement (met):** `62_action_attribution_test.sql` (9) — finance decides the request by direct DML naming the owner as reviewer and the row records FINANCE; an unrelated update by the owner leaves the reviewer unchanged (the assertion that justifies the on-change condition); the uploader derived on the same shape; the session-less path proven to keep what it sets.

### VOID-1 — voiding an invoice or a journal entry is unimplemented (discovered 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** missing capability / misleading contract · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** `invoices` carries `voided_at`, `voided_by`, `void_reason` and `journal_entries` the same, with an FK on `(tenant_id, voided_by) -> users`. **No function anywhere writes any of them**, and `app.status_transitions` has no `invoices` or `journal_entries` rows, so the transition trigger governs neither table.
- **Impact:** an agency that raises an invoice in error has no supported way to void it. The columns are a contract nothing honours — an implementer would reasonably assume the capability exists. Bounded today because the columns are simply never populated.
- **Decision required:** whether voiding is a capability ORVION offers at all; if so, which permission it costs (canon 28 defines none), which states it is legal from, and whether a voided invoice is corrected by a credit note instead — which is the more usual accounting answer and would make the columns themselves wrong.
- **Test requirement:** once decided, the lifecycle with positive and negative controls, plus a derivation for `voided_by` under ATTR-2's rule.

### SPP-3 — a payment-proof review records no reviewer, and structurally cannot (discovered 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** audit integrity / schema · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: a small nullable platform-reviewer representation.** ENGINEERING task; PP-1 is retired as this row's duplicate. **Superseded:** BLOCKED — ARCHITECTURAL DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** review is `app.platform_review_payment_proof`, a SECURITY DEFINER **platform** action. `subscription_payment_proofs.reviewed_by` has FK `(tenant_id, reviewed_by) -> public.users`, the TENANT membership table — and a platform operator has no row there. The function correctly does not attempt to fill it, so the column stays null after every review.
- **Impact:** the platform's decision on a tenant's payment proof carries no reviewer in the row. Whether it is recorded elsewhere depends on the event spine, not on this table.
- **Decision required:** whether the platform identity belongs in this column (which would need a different FK or a nullable platform-identity column), in a separate column, or only in `events`. Not guessed — it is a question about where platform identity lives, which canon 34 addresses for humans but not for platform operators acting on tenants.
- **Test requirement:** once decided, a review that records its reviewer, with a control proving a tenant user cannot set it.



### SLA-1 — the manager escalation canon makes MANDATORY had never fired (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-29, `202607056600`)
- **Category:** operational completeness / canon compliance · **Severity:** High · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **The canonical requirement, quoted rather than inferred.** canon 04 § SLA Escalation Rule: *"If a lead is not responded to within 15 minutes: Notify the assigned employee. **Notify the employee's manager.** Record an escalation event."* canon 10 § Lead Notifications repeats it and adds the second step: *"After another 15 minutes without response: Notify reassigned employee. **Notify manager.** Record reassignment event."* canon 10 also lists **"Manager escalation" among the notifications a user CANNOT MUTE** — mandatory operational behaviour, not a preference. The 15/30-minute windows already matched `app.process_lead_sla`'s defaults, so no timing was invented here either.
- **Evidence (reproduced before fixing, with a positive control).** Standard configuration — an employee, a `branch_manager` and a `department_manager`, all placed in the branch/department through `user_branch_assignments`, all holding TENANT-scoped roles, which is exactly what `app.assign_user_role` produces (`p_scope_type` defaults to `'tenant'`, leaving `branch_id`/`department_id` NULL). One SLA pass produced: employee **1** notification, branch manager **0**, department manager **0** — while a positive control in the same transaction proved the managers **can see the lead** (RLS returns it). A notification failure, not an unreachable row.
- **Root cause — the recurring shape, this time in the operational layer rather than a guard.** The manager lookup asked `(ura.branch_id = r.branch_id or ura.department_id = r.department_id)` of `user_role_assignments` ALONE. ORVION's authoritative definition of governance is `app.visible_branch_ids()` / `app.visible_department_ids()`, each a **UNION** of tenant-wide read, the user's `user_branch_assignments` **placement**, and scoped `user_role_assignments`. The SLA lookup used only the third — the one that is NULL for every role assignment ORVION's own RPC creates by default. **Two definitions of one concept, and the narrower one sat in the place that mattered.**
- **Second instance, same defect:** the reassignment branch notified only the NEW assignee. Canon 10 requires the manager to be told there too, and that branch had **no manager notification at all** — not mis-scoped, simply never written.
- **Why no existing test caught it:** `34_event_write_path_test` and `36_subscription_gate_system_paths_test` both exercise `process_lead_sla`, but they assert the system-path survival and the subscription-gate isolation — that the *event* is emitted for the right tenant. **Neither asserts who is notified.** This was a genuinely uncovered canonical requirement rather than a guard that lied.
- **Remediation (applied):** `app.lead_responsible_managers(tenant, branch, department, exclude_user)` holds the scope rule ONCE and is used by BOTH paths, because a second copy of a predicate is what produced the defect. It mirrors `visible_branch_ids`/`visible_department_ids` for an arbitrary user (those read the session; this runs with none), and adds the `is_active` / `starts_at` / `ends_at` bounds the authoritative functions apply, which the old inline predicate omitted (SPEC-148: revocation is complete). The tenant-wide-read source is deliberately absent — it resolves to ceo/owner, who are not in canon's escalation path.
- **Deliberately NOT changed:** which roles escalate (still `branch_manager` / `department_manager` — widening to ceo/owner would be new policy); the 15/30 windows; the warn-before-reassign ordering; the qualifying-interaction definition; the round-robin choice of the next assignee; the subscription-gate skip; the event vocabulary; the immutability of assignment history.
- **Test requirement (met):** `63_sla_escalation_test.sql` (11) — a positive control proving the manager can SEE the lead; both managers notified on warning; **a negative control proving a `branch_manager` who governs ANOTHER branch is still NOT notified**, so the scope test was widened rather than removed; one event, not one per notification; a second pass warning nothing, so managers are not spammed every minute; and both halves of the reassignment notification.
- **A correction to my own first version of that test:** it passed assertion 10 **for the wrong reason.** The reassignment pool includes the managers, the tie broke on `u.id asc`, and the branch manager's own *"reassigned to you"* notice was being counted as a manager escalation. The fixture now pins the round robin to the colleague and asserts the new assignee explicitly, so the two cannot overlap. Recorded because it is exactly the vacuous-assertion shape this programme exists to catch.

### LEAD-3 — the SLA reassignment pool includes managers (discovered 2026-08-29)
- **Category:** operational policy · **Severity:** Low–Medium · **Status:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence (observed while proving SLA-1, not theorised):** `app.process_lead_sla`'s candidate query is *everyone placed in the lead's branch and department* who is active and is not the current assignee, ordered by least-recently-assigned then `u.id`. Managers are placed there too, so they are candidates — and in a team where nobody has held a lead yet, every `last_at` is NULL and the tie breaks on `u.id`, which handed the lead to the **branch manager** in a clean fixture.
- **Impact:** an overdue lead can be auto-assigned to a manager rather than to a front-line handler.
