- **Remediation (applied):** the test now parses the VALUES BLOCK of every function (delimited, so `p_to_status in ('cancelled','void',…)` — a real line in `advance_booking` that a looser parser did read as a transition — cannot be mistaken for one), FAILS if any of the ten stops parsing, and adds the direction never checked before: **every transition the trigger permits must be offered by a function or named to the RPC that owns it.** That reverse check is exactly where TRANS-2 was hiding. 101 rules, 10 tables, 0 mismatches, 3 named exclusions.
- **Decision still required:** whether to make `app.status_transitions` the single runtime source and where the per-entity extras then live.

### DOC-EXP-1 — an expiring passport notifies nobody (discovered 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** operational completeness · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: thresholds deferred until notification rules exist.** ENGINEERING task on that trigger. **Superseded:** BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence (proven over HTTP):** `app.expiring_documents(p_within_days)` works and the window is a real filter — a passport expiring in 10 days is found at 30 days, excluded at 5, and one expiring in 400 days is never returned. But `document_expiry` exists in the `notification_type` catalog and **nothing produces it**: the only writer of `notifications` in the whole database is `app.process_lead_sla`. Asserted in `scripts/verify_lifecycle_branches.ps1` as zero notifications, so the gap is pinned rather than assumed.
- **Impact:** an agency learns a customer's passport is about to expire only if someone opens the report. Passport and visa expiry is the single most common way an Egyptian travel agency loses a departure, so the read path existing without the push path is a real operational hole — not a cosmetic one.
- **Decision required:** who is notified (the assigned employee, the branch manager, the customer), how far ahead, and how often a reminder repeats. Canon's SLA machinery answers all three for leads and none of them for documents. Deliberately not invented — a notification cadence is business policy.
- **Test requirement:** once decided, the producer with a positive control (a document inside the window notifies exactly the intended recipients) and a negative one (a document outside it, and a second run, do not re-notify).

### AUTH-1 — ORVION's own OTP and TOTP tables are unimplemented, and Supabase Auth is the live mechanism (discovered 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** duplicate source of truth / misleading contract · **Severity:** Low · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-01 (OWNER-1) — DECIDED BY THE OWNER: Supabase Auth is the sole MFA/OTP/TOTP source; ORVION builds no second factor-state machine.** ENGINEERING task — retire the two unused tables. **Superseded:** BLOCKED — ARCHITECTURAL DECISION · **Owner:** owner
- **Evidence:** nothing in the database reads or writes `otp_challenges` or `totp_enrollments` — no function, no trigger, no view, no report. Meanwhile `app.requires_mfa()` gates step-up on the JWT's `aal` claim, which is **Supabase Auth's own** MFA state, not these tables. `trusted_devices`, the third table in the same canon-34 group, *is* genuinely used (`app.record_trusted_device`, `app.revoke_trusted_device`, `app.my_trusted_devices`, three event triggers).
- **Impact:** none today — the tables are empty and correctly owner-scoped, so they are fail-closed. The defect is that two tables exist as a contract nothing honours: an implementer would reasonably assume ORVION owns OTP and TOTP state when Supabase Auth does.
- **Decision required:** implement them as the source of truth (and reconcile with `aal`), or retire them and let Supabase Auth own factor state outright. Deliberately not guessed: both are defensible and the choice changes where authentication state lives.
- **Test requirement:** once decided, either the enrolment/challenge lifecycle with positive and negative controls, or an assertion that the tables are gone.

### TEST-1 — forty fixture subqueries depended on the rest of the database (discovered + FIXED 2026-08-28)
- **Category:** test integrity · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence (reproduced, not theorised):** `38_class_a_events_test` assertion 9 failed with `payment_allocations_invoice_id_fkey` violated. Its fixture read `from public.payments p, public.invoices i limit 1` — an unscoped cross join which, running as `postgres` with RLS off, saw every tenant's rows and paired this fixture's payment with an invoice `scripts/verify_role_journeys.ps1` had left behind. The code was correct; the fixture was measuring the wrong thing.
- **Root cause:** a test that selects its own fixture row by a non-unique attribute (`full_name`, `title`, `name`, `event_type_code`, `finding_type_code`) with no tenant predicate is bounded only by RLS — and only while the actor happens to be `authenticated` and the fixture happens to be the sole match. Both conditions are accidents, not design. Under `reset role` neither holds.
- **Class search (the point of the fix):** a scan of all 58 test files found **40+ such subqueries across 11 files** — 17, 19, 24, 34, 37, 38, 39, 46, 49, 52, plus the three event counts already corrected in 27/38. Reference lookups (`catalog_values`, `countries`, `roles`, `subscription_plans`) were left alone: those tables are global and keyed uniquely, so they carry no such dependency.
- **Remediation (applied):** every one scoped to its own fixture's `tenant_id` (or `auth_user_id` for `trusted_devices`, or the two fixture tenants for `49`). Nothing was weakened; the assertions are unchanged, they now measure what they claim to.
- **Verification requirement (met, and it is the real fix):** the suite is now proven **order-independent** — 58 files / 672 assertions / 0 failures both on a fresh `npx supabase db reset` **and** re-run immediately after all four HTTP suites have deposited their (deliberately non-teardownable) residue. Running pgTAP only against a clean database is what let this class survive.

### RECOVER-1 — Primary ran four migrations this working copy did not have, and every guard said CLEAN (discovered 2026-09-03)
- **Category:** parity / process · **Severity:** **Critical** · **Status:** **CLOSED 2026-09-03** (recovered + guarded + mutation-proven) · **Owner:** engineering
- **CAUSAL CORRECTION, 2026-09-03 reconciliation — read this before the account below.** This row was written from a clone that had not fetched, and it says the four migrations were *never committed*. **They were.** `b72caff`, `46a0cfc` and `1193643` carried them to `origin/main` on 2026-09-02 while this clone worked forward from `4b67d3f` in parallel and never fetched, so `git log --all` and `git fsck` were searching a repository that genuinely did not contain them — every observation below was accurate, and the *inference* from it was not. The recovery was nonetheless exact: `git rev-parse` proves all four reconstructed files are **byte-identical blobs** to the committed originals (`de526e7`, `570ae33`, `af88090`, `a54648e`). **What the incident demonstrates is a FETCH gap, not an orphaning.** The finding itself stands unchanged in substance — Primary ran ahead of a working copy for a day and no guard noticed — and so does everything built to close it; only the story about *where the commits went* was wrong, and it is corrected here rather than left to stand.
- **What was found, by measurement rather than report.** A live read of Primary returned **188 migrations** while `supabase/migrations/` held **184**. The four — `202607059600` (SUP-2), `202607059700` (SUP-3), `202607059800` (RBAC-5 — the migration's own prose says RBAC-3; see that row's ID NOTE), `202607059900` (SUP-4a) — were applied to Primary on 2026-09-02 by `platplustours@gmail.com` and were **absent from this working copy**. `git log --all` (526 commits), the reflog, the stash and `git fsck` contained no trace of them, and the commit the owner's directive cited as their home, `1193643`, **was not a valid object in this clone** — because it lived on `origin/main`, unfetched. See the causal correction above.
- **Why nothing caught it, and this is the finding.** `check_repository_consistency.ps1` reads files only and says so. `check_database_parity.ps1` compares the repository against **local**, and against Primary **only when a human pastes three values in** — so a session that did not run it, or ran it without the Primary arguments, learned nothing. Its Check 9 sibling compares the manifest's claimed count against the repository's file count: both said 184, consistently and wrongly. **No guard in ORVION compares the repository's migration COUNT against Primary's on its own initiative**, so Primary silently ran ahead for a day with a security-relevant authorization refactor in it.
- **Blast radius while it lasted.** The repository could not rebuild Primary. `npx supabase db reset` produced a database four migrations behind the one being served, so every local proof — pgTAP, smoke, the HTTP suites — was evidence about a *different schema*. The manifest's published live-state hashes described the 184-migration world and would have told a fresh session that parity was PROVEN.
- **Remediation (applied).** All four recovered from `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations.statements` and written to `supabase/migrations/`, each verified **byte-identical by md5 against the statement Primary actually executed** (`c45fbd1b…`, `fc8f8a53…`, `d338a3c2…`, `6bd9b19f…`) rather than by reading them and judging them equivalent. `npx supabase db reset` then applied all 188 cleanly, and the structural surface hash now matches Primary exactly (3,442 objects, `7f327405…`).
- **What was NOT recoverable, and is the lasting cost.** A database returns SQL; it does not return tests, documents or decisions. Lost permanently with the uncommitted commit: the pgTAP files for SUP-2/SUP-3/SUP-4a/RBAC-3, the register rows, **ADR-0027**, the `verify_database.sql` table-count update, the HTTP suite updates, and the manifest/plan/session-report edits. Their absence was **proven, not assumed**: on the first run after recovery the suite failed 5 tests across 5 files, every failure traceable to the recovered migrations. All have been rewritten (`90_…`, `91_…`, ADR-0027, these rows) — reconstructed from the migrations' own reasoning, which is unusually complete, but they are **new work, not the originals**.
- **THE GUARD, BUILT AND MUTATION-PROVEN 2026-09-03 — and the root cause restated correctly first.** The closure pass re-derived the cause from evidence rather than from this row's first draft, and the first draft was wrong in an important way. `check_database_parity.ps1` was **not** the guard that lied: run with no Primary values it already exits 2 and prints *"UNPROVEN — PRIMARY WAS NOT CONTACTED … This is NOT a pass"*, reproduced live. The real hole was narrower and worse: **nothing in the repository RECORDED whether Primary had ever been read at this HEAD.** Parity was asserted in a terminal and then lost, so the question was unanswerable from the repository — and an unanswerable question is indistinguishable from a satisfied one when nobody asks it. The parity guard also needs Docker and a live local stack, so it cannot run in the doc-only CI job or with the stack down, which are precisely the sessions that skipped it.
- **What was built.** `reports/evidence/primary-ledger-evidence.json` — a durable, HEAD-attributed record of Primary's **entire** ledger — plus `scripts/check_primary_ledger.ps1`, invoked as **Check 19** of `check_repository_consistency.ps1` (it was written as Check 18; the reconciliation of 2026-09-03 found COLD-3 had independently taken that number on `origin/main`, and this one — the unpublished of the two — renumbered), i.e. inside the guard that runs on every commit, in CI, and at Stage B of the boot sequence. Four properties make it materially stronger than the pasted value it supplements: (1) the **whole ledger** is compared as a set, so a same-count/different-identity swap fails where a count comparison passes; (2) `migration_count` and `ledger_fingerprint` are **recomputed** from the recorded ledger, so editing a number without forging all 189 entries is detected and a pasted number carries no authority of its own; (3) `repository_head` must be an **ancestor of HEAD**, so evidence from an abandoned branch or rewritten history is refused; (4) it **fails closed** — absent, unparseable, incomplete or disagreeing evidence is an issue, never a skip.
- **Live read evaluated first and rejected on constraints, not taste.** `.mcp.json` exposes `supabase-primary` as an HTTP MCP endpoint with no reusable local secret; `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD` and `PGPASSWORD` are all absent; `supabase/.temp/` holds only `cli-latest`, so the CLI is not linked and `supabase migration list --linked` has nothing to authenticate with. Giving the script a credential would mean committing one or planting one, which `AGENTS.md §6` forbids. **Faking a live read would have been worse than admitting an evidence-based one.**
- **Residual, stated rather than papered over (MEAS-1).** Check 19 cannot prove the recorded ledger was read *from Primary* rather than generated from the repository — that is **GUARD-1**'s class, and no repository-local mechanism closes it. What it does close is the state this finding actually occupied: no Primary reading for the current migration set, every guard green. That is now a hard CI failure.
- **Proven, not asserted:** `scripts/test_primary_ledger_guard.ps1` — 13 assertions over isolated sandboxes, covering an extra migration on Primary (RECOVER-1 itself), an extra in the repository, a same-count/different-identity swap, a foreign HEAD, a non-existent HEAD, missing evidence, a hand-edited count, a hand-edited fingerprint, malformed JSON, a missing field, and **both** direction controls. The integration was proven separately by removing the evidence file and confirming `check_repository_consistency.ps1` itself exits 1.
- **Related:** GUARD-1 (the parity guard proving Primary from a value it was handed) is this defect's near neighbour and was fixed by requiring the values to be read from Primary — which does not help when the guard is never run with them.

### SUP-2 — a ceiling you may not READ cost less to SET (discovered + resolved 2026-09-02, `202607059600`)
- **Category:** authorization · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** SUPERSEDED by SUP-3 (the hole stays closed) · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence (reproduced live before the fix).** `suppliers.credit_limit_amount` is withheld from `authenticated` by column grant (SUP-1) and served only by `app.supplier_credit`, which requires VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS. Its WRITE required only ASSIGN_SUPPLIER. Measured role sets: ASSIGN_SUPPLIER to {branch_manager, ceo, department_manager, owner, senior_employee}; VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS to {ceo, finance_manager, owner}. **branch_manager, department_manager and senior_employee could set a ceiling they were forbidden to know** — and an actor who sets a value knows it, so the read gate withheld nothing from them.
- **Why two packages that both examined this exact column missed it:** SEC-1c closed the write half *against a trainee*, who holds neither permission; SUP-1 closed the read half and recorded the write path as untouched. Both statements were true. The middle of the role ladder was never the subject of either proof — a green guard proving only the property it actually measures (`AGENTS.md §6`).
- **Resolution:** superseded eight hours later by SUP-3's dedicated permission. The three roles still cannot set the ceiling, so nothing reopened.

### SUP-3 — supplier credit management becomes its own permission (OWNER DECISION 2026-09-02, `202607059700`)
- **Category:** authorization / business policy · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** RESOLVED — OWNER-DECIDED · **Owner:** owner (decided)
- **The owner's two rules:** (1) `finance_manager` MUST be able to modify `suppliers.credit_limit_amount`; (2) supplier credit-limit management MUST have its OWN independent permission, not an incidental consequence of ASSIGN_SUPPLIER.
- **Implementation:** `MANAGE_SUPPLIER_CREDIT`, minted through canon 25's established `insert … on conflict (key) do nothing` route, `required_feature_code = 'finance_lite'` (derived from VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS, which governs KNOWING the same figure, so a plan cannot entitle the write without the read). Granted to {owner, ceo, finance_manager} — canon 28's role set for EDIT_LOCKED_COST, the nearest restricted financial authority ORVION has.
- **Orthogonality is the part with teeth, and it is enforced rather than asserted.** `guard_write_capability` learned that a write touching ONLY the ceiling is a different act, compared by ROW IMAGE rather than a column list so a future column cannot silently fall out of date. Neither permission implies the other in either direction. Proven on both doors: `91_supplier_credit_permission_test.sql` (26 assertions) and `verify_role_journeys.ps1`. *(Pointer corrected 2026-09-04, GOV-13: this row named `90_supplier_credit_authority_test` (`.sql` omitted deliberately — it is a dead artifact, not a live reference), the RECOVER-1 reconstruction the `d02b702` merge superseded with the committed originals. It has not existed since that merge; the assertions it named live in the two files above.)*
- **Visibility deliberately untouched:** an actor holding MANAGE_SUPPLIER_CREDIT and not VIEW_FINANCIAL_DOCUMENTS may SET the ceiling and still cannot READ it. Write authority does not become visibility.

### SUP-4a — a ceiling with no currency is not an amount (discovered + resolved 2026-09-02, `202607059900`)
- **Category:** schema / money standard · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **The measurement that settles it as engineering rather than policy.** Eleven `public` tables carry a money-amount column; **ten carry `currency_code` beside it, and `suppliers` was the only one that did not.** Canon 30's money standard is explicit: "Amounts should be stored as numeric" / "Currency code should be stored separately" / "`currency_code` values must reference `currencies.code`". This was ORVION's own standard unapplied to exactly one column — derivable from canon, therefore fixed rather than referred upward.
- **Why it blocked enforcement.** `app.supplier_balance` returns `outstanding_payable` **one row per currency** (reproduced live as EGP 8,000 and USD 600 for one supplier) while the ceiling was a single currency-less scalar. "Is 8,000 EGP + 600 USD over 10,000?" was not a hard question — it was a **malformed** one.
- **Fix:** `credit_limit_currency_code` referencing `currencies.code` ON DELETE RESTRICT, plus `check ((credit_limit_amount is null) = (credit_limit_currency_code is null))` — the conditional form of the standard, because the amount itself is legitimately nullable. Both guards widened to treat the ceiling as the pair, so re-denominating EGP to USD costs the same permission as changing the number. `app.create_supplier` and `app.supplier_credit` both carry the currency.
- **No FX was introduced and none is implied.** ADR-0020's shipped precedent (`app.advance_booking`) compares per-currency and never converts; `tenants.default_currency_code` was measured to have a producer and **no consumer at all** and was deliberately not pressed into service as a base currency.

### SUP-4b — does the credit ceiling REFUSE anything, and what may override it? (OPEN OWNER DECISION, raised 2026-09-02)
- **Category:** business policy · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** **BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION** · **Owner:** owner
- **State today, stated plainly: `CREDIT LIMIT ENFORCED = NO.`** The ceiling is stored, non-negative, well-formed (SUP-4a), read-gated (SUP-1) and write-gated (SUP-3). **Nothing anywhere compares supplier exposure against it.** A search of every migration for `credit_limit_amount` returns the table definition, the two guards, the RPC, the gated reader and the tests — and no comparison.
- **What the evidence DOES settle, so the owner is asked only what cannot be derived:**
  - *Currency semantics* — **DERIVABLE, and now implemented.** Per-currency, no conversion: `exposure[limit_currency] + new_exposure <= credit_limit`, other currencies ignored for this limit. This follows ADR-0020/ADR-0021's shipped rule that financial amounts are compared per currency and never collapsed.
  - *Exposure definition* — **AUTHORITATIVE and already implemented.** `app.supplier_balance`: locked booking-item cost (`cost_locked_at is not null`), excluding cancelled/no_show and archived items, minus `supplier_payment` payments. Cost is provisional until finance approval locks it (`app.review_finance_approval`), so **`cost_locked_at` is the earliest authoritative moment supplier exposure becomes real** — derived from the domain model, not chosen.
  - *Supplier scope* — **DERIVABLE: all suppliers.** The column is on `suppliers` with no type/status/category exclusion anywhere; `supplier_type_code` is a descriptive catalog, not a financial one. A supplier with a NULL ceiling simply has no ceiling. No new supplier type is needed and none should be invented.
- **What genuinely requires the owner, and it is three narrow questions, not a redesign:**
  1. **Does the ceiling REFUSE, or WARN?** ADR-0020's precedent for a balance-constrained operation is *not* a refusal: `app.advance_booking` requires an override permission and records a `risk` event. Whether supplier credit behaves the same way is a commercial-relationship decision — some agencies want a hard stop, others want the booking to proceed with a flag.
  2. **Which operation does it govern?** The engineering answer is the moment exposure becomes real, i.e. finance approval locking cost. But an agency may want the block earlier (at supplier assignment, so a seller is stopped before promising a customer) — that is an operational-workflow preference, not a derivable fact.
  3. **Is there an override, and who holds it?** If refusal is chosen, `OVERRIDE_SUPPLIER_CREDIT_LIMIT` would be modelled as an independent capability exactly like MANAGE_SUPPLIER_CREDIT — but minting it before knowing whether a refusal exists would be inventing a capability for a rule that may not exist. Canon names no supplier-credit event type either.
- **Explicitly NOT blocked on this:** everything derivable has already shipped. What remains is one commercial-policy question, and **concurrency is part of the answer, not a separate item** — if a refusal is chosen, `exposure + new <= limit` must hold under concurrent transactions, which in PostgreSQL means the check must serialize against the rows it sums (the smallest correct mechanism, no external locking infrastructure).

### RBAC-5 — capability grants become per-USER, not only per-role (OWNER DIRECTIVE 2026-09-02, `202607059800`, ADR-0027)
- **Category:** authorization architecture · **Severity:** High (capability gap) · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering (owner-directed)
- **ID NOTE, read this before citing the migration.** `202607059800`'s own prose calls this finding **RBAC-3**, and `202607060000`'s calls its sibling **RBAC-4**. Both ids were **already taken** in this register — RBAC-3 is "an employee cannot assign leads" (INTENTIONAL) and RBAC-4 is "a department manager could not see their own department's records". The collision is a direct consequence of RECOVER-1: the package was written against a register it never got to update, so it allocated ids that a reader of this file would have seen were in use. The register is the SSOT for finding ids (`GOVERNANCE.md §2`), so the rows are **RBAC-5** and **RBAC-6** and the migrations are left byte-identical to what Primary executed rather than rewritten to match — the historical record of what ran is worth more than tidy prose, and a duplicate id would make Check 2's cross-Master status comparison report a contradiction that does not exist.
- **What was missing, measured:** there was **no path from a user to a permission except through a role**. That single gap made four owner requirements impossible — per-user grant, per-user revoke, explicit deny, and "a role is a bundle you can override".
- **The decision, and why REBUILD was rejected on evidence.** `app.has_permission` is resolved by **60 RLS policies, 61 triggers and 76 functions**, and `role_permissions` is the ONLY foreign key into `public.permissions`. Every enforcement site already delegates to one function, so the end-state is a change to how that ONE function RESOLVES a grant, not to how anything ENFORCES it. A rebuild would rewrite ~197 enforcement sites, each a chance to lose a rule earned by a defect (SEC-1b, SEC-1c, PAR-4, BOOK-1, ADMIN-1, FIN-10) — maximum risk for zero security gain. **Verdict: REFACTOR THE GRANT MODEL, PRESERVE THE ENFORCEMENT PLANE.** No RLS policy, guard trigger, RPC or grant was touched.
- **Delivered:** `user_permission_grants` (grant|deny in one table, because precedence is a property of the pair), composite tenant-qualified FKs per TENANT-1, per-command RLS charging MANAGE_PERMISSIONS, `derive_created_by`, and `permission_granted`/`permission_revoked` events that had been registered vocabulary with no producer since seeding (EVT-2's class). Resolution order: **active DENY, then user GRANT, then role GRANT, then the PLAN gate, always last.**
- **Proven, not asserted** (`92_capability_grant_model_test.sql`, 26 assertions, plus HTTP coverage in `verify_role_journeys.ps1`): with the table empty the answer is byte-for-byte the old answer (**no privilege was expanded**); a grant opens one capability to one person; a deny beats both a role grant and a direct grant; **a starter tenant's own owner holding an explicit grant still cannot use a `full_finance` capability** — the commercial boundary holds; and revocation takes effect on the **next request with the same unexpired token**, which is the property that decided against JWT claims.
- **RBAC-2 is superseded by this row.** Its 2026-08-29 resolution reasoned that `MANAGE_PERMISSIONS` "gates a capability ORVION deliberately does not offer to tenants". ORVION now offers it, and that permission is its gate.

### RBAC-6 — the explainer the dashboard was built for could not be reached from the dashboard (discovered + resolved 2026-09-03, `202607060000`)
- **Category:** API surface · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** FIXED · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** RBAC-3 created `app.effective_permissions` explicitly "for the dashboard and for an audit" and granted EXECUTE to `authenticated` — but never created the `public` wrapper, and **PostgREST serves only `public`**. Measured: `public.tenant_capabilities` and `public.supplier_credit`, its two nearest siblings, both exist; `public.effective_permissions` did not. The single capability RBAC-3 exists to enable was unreachable from any browser.
- **Consistent with the recovery:** the package was never committed, never tested and never had its API contract regenerated — which is exactly how a wrapper goes missing without anything noticing.
- **Fix:** the wrapper, following API-1's model — SECURITY INVOKER, `search_path` pinned, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `authenticated`. **`app.has_permission` remains deliberately unreachable** and `verify_api_end_to_end.ps1`'s "no permission-probing oracle" assertion is untouched: `has_permission(key)` is an arbitrary-key probe, while `effective_permissions` is curated and self-gating (own permissions freely; another user's costs MANAGE_PERMISSIONS). Proven over HTTP in both directions, including the empty-set-is-authorization control.

## Decision-debt closure pass — 2026-09-04

Owner directive: *"Do NOT leave any discovered Owner Decision / Open Decision unresolved merely because it belongs to a later phase… Do NOT escalate a question to the owner merely because a document labels it 'Owner Decision'."* Every item below was re-derived from the implementation, the catalog and canon **before** its label was trusted. Six items labelled "BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION" or "OWNER DECISION" turned out not to be business decisions at all.

### FIN-7 — RESOLVED 2026-09-04 AS ENGINEERING: the invoice state machine already exists; it is unenforced on ONE of the two doors
- **Category:** authorization / second door · **Severity:** Low–Medium · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — ENGINEERING; implementation scheduled** · **Owner:** engineering (was: owner)
- **The label was wrong, and the evidence is in the RPCs.** `app.issue_invoice` **refuses any invoice whose `status_code <> 'draft'`** (`raise 'only a draft invoice can be issued (is %)'`). `app.record_payment` **refuses unless `status_code in ('issued','partially_paid','overdue')`**, and then does not *choose* the next state at all — it **derives** it: `v_new_status := case when v_new_total >= v_inv.total_amount then 'paid' else 'partially_paid' end`. So "which changes are legal" is already decided, already implemented, and already owner-approved by use.
- **What is actually missing is the second door, not a policy.** `app.status_transitions` holds **zero `invoices` rows** and `invoices` carries **no `enforce_status_transition` trigger** (verified against `pg_trigger`; `documents` has one, so the pattern is established). FIN-6 governs **who** may write the column; nothing governs **which** move is legal on the direct PostgREST door. This is the exact class already fixed five times — **BOOK-1, ADMIN-1, FIN-8, FIN-10, QUO-1** (`AGENTS.md §6`, "Test both doors").
- **The transitions, READ OFF the implementation rather than invented:** `draft to issued` · `issued to partially_paid` · `issued to paid` · `partially_paid to paid` · `overdue to partially_paid` · `overdue to paid`.
- **Two states deliberately excluded, each for a measured reason.** `overdue` has **no producer anywhere** (no function in `app`/`public` writes the literal) — EVT-2's class, and registering a transition nothing performs would invent a capability. `voided` is **VOID-1**, which remains a genuine owner decision (below).
- **Safe to enforce:** `app.enforce_status_transition` returns early when `v_new is not distinct from v_old`, so the `partially_paid` to `partially_paid` case a part payment produces is not a transition and cannot trip it.
- **Why implementation is scheduled rather than done here:** attaching a fail-closed trigger to a live financial table must carry positive controls for every RPC path (`AGENTS.md §6`, no vacuous security tests) and a full `§5a` run. That is a package, not a line in a decision pass. **No owner input is required for it.**

### VOID-1 — NARROWED AGAIN 2026-09-04: the register's own dichotomy is false; Egyptian e-invoicing uses BOTH, on a clock
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 — DECIDED BY THE OWNER AND IMPLEMENTED (`202607060400`)** · **Owner:** engineering (was: owner). The owner **rejected draft-only as a final answer** and ratified the architectural principle: the **internal ORVION invoice lifecycle** is modelled **separately** from the **external Egyptian ETA fiscal lifecycle**, with the boundary represented now so a future ETA integration maps onto it without redesigning the internal model. No ETA window (3/7/60 days) is to be encoded as a universal ORVION rule.
- **The framing "void OR credit note" is not how the jurisdiction works.** Egypt's ETA e-invoicing permits **cancellation within 7 days** (with buyer approval, never after 60 days, and never once a credit note exists), and requires **credit notes / debit notes referencing the original UUID** for corrections after that window. Validated invoices cannot be edited. Both mechanisms exist and are separated by **time and issuance**, not by preference.
- **Mapped onto ORVION's own columns:** `voided` on a **never-issued draft** has no tax consequence and is uncontroversial. `voided` on an **issued** invoice is the ETA cancellation window. Corrections after it need a **credit note**, an entity ORVION does not model at all — new canon, which is precisely why this stays with the owner.
- **`journal_entries` is NOT the same question and must not inherit the answer.** It carries `voided_at`/`voided_by`/`void_reason` with **no catalog state behind them**. Standard double-entry practice reverses a posted entry with a compensating entry; it never voids one. Engineering recommendation: those three columns are wrong for `journal_entries` regardless of how the invoice question resolves.
- **Exact owner choice:** (A) draft-only voiding now, credit notes deferred; (B) draft voiding **plus** an ETA-shaped cancellation window on issued invoices; (C) full credit-note model. **Recommended: (A)** — it is the whole of what today's columns can honour, invents no entity, and leaves (B)/(C) open once the agency's ETA obligations are known.

### VERIFY-1 — RESOLVED 2026-09-04 AS DOCUMENTATION: canon 07 makes verification and approval ONE act
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — NO NEW CAPABILITY** · **Owner:** engineering (was: owner/engineering)
- **The question was "is payment verification a distinct capability from finance approval?" Canon 07 answers it.** Its *Transfer Proof Approval* flow reads "the responsible employee uploads the transfer receipt… **Finance verifies the bank account… Finance approves or rejects**" — one workflow, one actor, one act. ORVION implements it through `approval_requests`, whose `requested_by`/`reviewed_by` are already derived by `app.derive_approval_requester`/`app.derive_approval_reviewer`.
- **Confirmed inert in all three directions:** no writer, no reader, and **no `VERIFY_PAYMENT` permission exists in `public.permissions` at all**, so no role could ever have been authorized to perform it.
- **Resolution:** `payments.verified_by`/`verified_at` are **redundant with the `approval_requests` path and must NOT be given a writer** — doing so would build a second, competing two-person money control. Columns are **kept, not dropped** (dropping is a destructive migration, and DEAD-1's precedent keeps inevitable structure), and are now recorded as closed-by-design so no future implementer reads them as an unbuilt feature. Pinned by assertion 22.

### TRANS-1 — RESOLVED 2026-09-04 AS ENGINEERING: the duplication is kept, because the proposed cure is worse and the disease is already contained
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — NO CODE REQUIRED** · **Owner:** engineering (was: owner, "BLOCKED — ARCHITECTURAL DECISION")
- **Measured state:** ten `advance_*` functions vs 104 `app.status_transitions` rows, compared exhaustively — **zero live disagreement**, with three table-side extras already named to the RPCs that own them.
- **The decision was "make `status_transitions` the single runtime source, and where do the per-entity extras live?" The evidence answers it against the change.** The inline lists carry more than a permission: `advance_lead` carries `is_closure`, `advance_booking_item` carries sub-status handling, `advance_refund` uses a single constant permission. Unifying requires a **wide sparse table or a jsonb payload** — trading a duplication that is *machine-checked in both directions* for a polymorphic payload no constraint can check. That is less inspectable authority, not more, and it contradicts the repository's own REL-1 reasoning (a polymorphic shape is kept only where canon demands it).
- **DRY is a principle, not an absolute.** A duplication with (a) zero live drift, (b) a guard that fails the moment the two disagree **and** the moment the trigger permits something no function offers, and (c) a cure requiring schema polymorphism, is a duplication you **keep and guard**. `54_transition_permission_parity_test.sql` parses all ten functions (101 rules, 10 tables, 0 mismatches, 3 named exclusions).
- **Re-open trigger:** if a per-entity extra ever needs to be read at runtime by something other than its own `advance_*` function, this is re-decided.
- **GOV-15 (below) records that this finding carried two contradictory detail blocks until today.**

### LIC-1 — RESOLVED 2026-09-04 AS ENGINEERING: the attack the missing audit would detect is infeasible by construction
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — NO CODE REQUIRED (accepted residual, with trigger)** · **Owner:** engineering (was: owner/engineering)
- **The question was "is an out-of-transaction audit hop worth its dependency?"** In a managed Supabase environment the only ways to write an audit row that survives `raise` are `dblink`/`pg_background`-style autonomous transactions — an extension dependency on the security path — since `NOTIFY` is itself transactional and rolls back with everything else.
- **What the audit would buy, measured against what it costs:** the residual is *absence of evidence about failed attempts*, never a bypass — redemption is single-use, so replay is closed, and the token is **128-bit**, making the enumeration attack that failed-attempt logging exists to detect infeasible. Adding an extension to the license path to detect an attack nobody can mount is a net **increase** in attack surface.
- **Resolution: accept the residual**, documented in the function body and pinned by an assertion in `43_license_activation_test.sql`. **Re-open trigger:** if token entropy is ever reduced, if tokens become user-chosen, or if a rate-limitable public endpoint is put in front of redemption.

### DELIV-1 + PH8-2 — RESOLVED 2026-09-04 AS ENGINEERING: this is observability, and the states it would report already exist
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — ENGINEERING; one reporting view, implementation scheduled** · **Owner:** engineering (was: owner then engineering)
- **PH8-2's own text already says it: "the gap is observability, not enforcement."** The in-DB consent gate is correct and stays untouched. Nothing about consent policy, delivery policy or retry policy changes.
- **The supposed decision — "what should a delivery-health surface report?" — is answered by the data, not by the owner.** Four states already exist and are already distinguishable: **delivered** · **failed** (durably `failed` with `error_message` after five attempts) · **in flight** (SPEC-123 lease) · **never eligible** (`consent_ad_user_data` not `granted`, so the claim join never matches and no delivery row is created). A read-only view reporting those four invents nothing.
- **Precedent, not innovation:** eight `reporting` views already exist on the Phase-9 Tier-A pattern. This is a ninth, and the first operator-facing one.
- **Why it is a real gap and not tidiness:** a GTM/consent-capture regression that zeroes consent on every click is **today indistinguishable from having no conversions at all**. That is the failure mode the view exists to make visible.
- **Implementation scheduled** (view + tests). **No owner input required.** DELIV-1's "subsumed by PH8-2's decision" is discharged: the decision is made.

### QUO-4 — RESOLVED 2026-09-04 AS DOCUMENTATION: canon's own scope column and the more recent owner directive both say department
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — DOCUMENTATION ONLY, zero behaviour change** (owner may reverse cheaply) · **Owner:** owner, ratified by evidence
- **This row already carried the recommendation; it is now applied.** Canon 28's scope column for `CREATE_QUOTATION` reads **"assigned/department"** — department is *inside canon*, not an invention — and the **2026-08-24 owner directive granted department continuity deliberately**, which is the more recent authority. Copying SPEC-154-A's `is_my_booking_item` precedent was rejected on evidence: `mine or tenant-wide` would block a *department manager* from editing their own team's quotation, which over-reaches.
- **Resolution: the department reading stands and current behaviour is correct.** Nothing changes in code. Pinned by assertion 15 of `84_quotation_line_integrity_test.sql`, so a future change is loud.
- **Residual owner veto:** if the owner wants strict "assigned only", it is one predicate and one assertion. Recorded as reversible rather than left open.

### RET-1 — RECLASSIFIED 2026-09-04: the safe answer is already in force; only a *finite* period needs counsel
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 — MECHANISM IMPLEMENTED (`202607060500`); the VALUES remain counsel's** · **Owner:** engineering for the mechanism; owner + counsel for the values. Retention is to be represented **per `document_type_code`**; `NULL` keeps its meaning of *no deletion policy currently defined*; fail-closed is preserved; **no legal period is invented or guessed**. The mechanism must accept legally approved values later without schema redesign.
- **Nothing is blocked and nothing is at risk.** `app.document_retention_days()` returns NULL, NULL means *retain forever*, the retention scan's `WHERE` is unsatisfiable, and **no document is ever selected for destruction**. The conservative outcome is the **default**, not a validation someone must remember to run — asserted by `49_document_retention_test.sql` sections 1–2, where a mistaken `0` fails the suite.
- **Therefore this must not be carried as a blocking owner decision.** The only thing a decision buys is *storage-cost reduction*; the only thing a wrong answer risks is destroying a record Egyptian law requires. **Recommendation: leave NULL.** Re-open when either (a) counsel states a retention period, or (b) superseded-version storage becomes a measurable cost. One line of one migration when it happens.

### SUP-4c — NARROWED 2026-09-04: every piece of the mechanism already exists; only the rate INSTANT is a policy choice
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 — ENGINEERING (see the resolution review at the end of this file)** · **Owner:** engineering (was: owner)
- **The previous framing said this "needs a base currency, a rate source and an as-of rule." All three already exist and were found by measurement, not assumed:**
  - **base currency** — `public.tenants.default_currency_code` exists today;
  - **rate source** — `public.exchange_rates` (`tenant_id, from_currency_code, to_currency_code, rate, effective_at, set_by`): ORVION has **already decided** rates are *tenant-maintained*, not fetched from an FX provider, and `set_by` records who set them;
  - **as-of rule** — `effective_at`, whose "latest rate at or before X" had two answers until **DUP-1** made the pair-instant unique; it now has exactly one.
- **So the standing prohibition — do not invent an FX provider, exchange-rate table, base-currency architecture or conversion policy — is satisfied without inventing anything.** `exchange_rates` still has **zero readers**; this would be its first.
- **The single irreducible question:** when a supplier's exposure spans currencies, at which instant is it converted for comparison against a ceiling stated in one currency — **(A) the evaluation instant** (latest rate at or before now; exposure re-values as rates move), or **(B) each item's own cost-lock instant** (historical rate per item; exposure stable but not a current figure)? **Engineering recommends (A)** — a credit ceiling is a *present-risk* control and SUP-4b already re-evaluates on every write, so (A) matches the mechanism that exists. **(B) is the right answer for accounting**, which is DC-11's realized-FX question, not this one.
- **Not blocking:** SUP-4b compares in the ceiling's own currency today and warns correctly for single-currency suppliers, the overwhelming majority case.

### CUST-3 — NARROWED 2026-09-04: engineering is pre-solved; the business question is untouched
- **Status:** 📋 **OPEN — BUSINESS DECISION** · **Owner:** owner
- **The engineering half needs no decision and no research:** if customer receivable ceilings are wanted, **SUP-4b's shipped pattern applies unchanged** — two columns (`credit_limit_amount`, `credit_limit_currency_code`) mirroring `suppliers`; `app.customer_balance` already exists as the exposure reader; and the warn-never-refuse semantics, the event-ledger idempotency and the role-addressed notification are all already built and tested. One migration, no new architecture.
- **The irreducible business question is unchanged and must not be inferred from SUP-4b:** does a travel agency want a *receivable* ceiling on customers at all, and does exceeding it warn (SUP-4b's answer for suppliers) or actually **stop** a booking? A payable ceiling and a receivable ceiling are different commercial instruments; the owner decided the first and said nothing about the second.

### DOC-LC-2 — RESOLVED 2026-09-04 AS A MODELLING QUESTION: a VERSION supersedes; a DOCUMENT does not
- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — recommend canon 26 amendment** (owner ratification advised; no code change either way) · **Owner:** owner to ratify / engineering answered
- **This is a data-modelling question with an evidenced answer, not a business policy.** Every writer of `documents.lifecycle_status_code` was checked and **not one sets `superseded`**. What ORVION actually does is emit `document_superseded` from `documents_emit_superseded`, keyed on **`current_version_id` changing** — supersession is an event about the **version pointer**.
- **The implementation is right and canon 26 is wrong.** A document ("Ahmed's passport") is not superseded when a new scan arrives — it remains the active passport record; its *version* is superseded. This matches every mature document model and is why `is_current` lives on the VERSION row.
- **Recommendation: amend canon 26 to remove `active to superseded` from the DOCUMENT machine**, leaving the two transitions DOC-LC-1 wired. **DOC-LC-1 was right to refuse to register it** — registering a transition nothing writes would let direct DML move a document into a state nothing reads. Pinned by assertions 3 and 5 of `69_document_lifecycle_test.sql`, the second measuring the *absence of a producer*.

### DOC-LC-3 — SPLIT 2026-09-04: the integrity defect is fixable WITHOUT deciding un-archiving
- **Status:** 📋 **INTEGRITY HALF: engineering, implementation scheduled · BUSINESS HALF: OPEN** · **Owner:** engineering + owner
- **The row assumed the two halves were one, and they are not.** It reasoned that "synchronizing the fields in either direction deletes one of those positions" — true of *synchronizing*, false of *refusing divergence*. A constraint rejecting the impossible pairs (`archived` with `is_archived=false`, and `active` with `is_archived=true`) closes the integrity hole and **takes no position on whether un-archiving exists**, because it constrains states, not transitions.
- **Engineering half — CLOSED, implementation scheduled:** forbid the divergent pair. This ends the reproduced defect (a document nothing can re-version while everything reading the boolean reports it as not archived) without touching either authority.
- **Business half — genuinely open:** *does un-archiving exist?* Canon 26 lists no transition back into `active`; `app.enforce_archive_authority` states "restoring is the same authority as archiving". Only after that is answered can the two fields be collapsed into one.

### PLAN-1 — NARROWED 2026-09-04: exactly three numbers are missing, and the counter must not be built before them
- **Status:** 📋 **OPEN — BUSINESS DECISION (pricing), narrowed to three values** · **Owner:** owner
- **The engineering half is already answered and needs no decision:** the gate sits inside `app.has_permission`, which every RLS policy and every `app.authorize()` already calls, so all surfaces are covered; `app.plan_limit` reads the ceilings; `app.tenant_capabilities()` publishes the picture. 66 entitlement rows are seeded from canon 28 + canon 17.
- **Building `usage_counters` now would be speculative work, and the register should say so plainly:** a counter counts *against a ceiling*, and three of the ceilings **do not exist as numbers**. Canon marks **Basic Reporting (Starter)**, **Integrations (Professional)** and **Offline Conversion (Professional)** as "Limited" and defines no value anywhere. Seeded **uncapped**, the safe default — a missing ceiling never wrongly refuses.
- **Exact owner input: three integers**, or an explicit "these three are unlimited". Nothing else is blocked.

### CANON-26-1 — NARROWED 2026-09-04 with a recommendation: one-step suspension is the abuse-response path
- **Status:** 📋 **OPEN — BUSINESS DECISION (canon amendment)** · **Owner:** owner
- **Measured:** canon 26 admits `suspended` only from `read_only`, while `active to cancelled` is available. SPEC-157 implemented canon exactly rather than inventing the edge, which is why this is a canon question.
- **The consequence is specific, not theoretical:** an active tenant that must be stopped immediately — fraud, chargeback, abuse, legal order — has to be moved to `read_only` first, and **`read_only` still permits reads and the export canon 28 promises**. The two-step path leaves a window in which a tenant you have decided to suspend is still transacting.
- **Recommendation: admit `active to suspended`.** Standard SaaS abuse response, it removes the window, and it costs one canon line plus one `app.status_transitions` row. It weakens nothing: suspension already exists and is already permission-gated; only the path to it is shortened.
- **Why it stays with the owner:** suspending a paying customer without a warning state is a commercial-relationship decision, not an engineering one.

### TAX-1 — NEW 2026-09-04: `invoices.external_submission_status_code` is guarded against a catalog type that does not exist, so the column can only ever be NULL
- **Category:** dead structure / vocabulary · **Severity:** Low today, High the day e-invoicing is wired · **Status:** 📋 **OPEN — engineering** · **Owner:** engineering
- **Found while resolving FIN-7/VOID-1.** `invoices_enforce_catalog_codes` is declared as `app.enforce_catalog_codes('status_code','invoice_status_code','external_submission_status_code','tax_submission_status')`, but `public.catalog_types` **has no `tax_submission_status` row** and `public.catalog_values` holds **zero** values for it.
- **Behaviour, read from the guard rather than assumed:** it does `continue when v_value is null`, then raises if the code is absent from `catalog_values`. So **NULL is accepted and every non-NULL value is refused** — the column is structurally unusable, and a guard that looks like it validates a vocabulary is validating an empty set.
- **Why it matters beyond hygiene:** this is the column Egyptian ETA e-invoicing submission state would live in (see VOID-1). The first attempt to record a submission status fails with a catalog error naming a catalog nobody has ever seen.
- **Fix (no decision needed):** either seed `tax_submission_status` with the ETA lifecycle, or remove the unbacked pair from the trigger declaration until e-invoicing is scheduled. **The choice of ETA vocabulary is deferred with VOID-1; removing a guard argument that guards nothing is not.**

### GOV-15 — NEW 2026-09-04: TRANS-1 carried two detail blocks with contradictory Status AND Owner, and no guard could see it
- **Category:** governance / registry integrity · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** 📋 **OPEN — engineering** · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** TRANS-1 has a block at the 2026-08-28 position reading `**Status:** IN PROGRESS · **Owner:** engineering`, and a second at the 2026-08-29 position reading `**Status:** BLOCKED — ARCHITECTURAL DECISION (narrowed) · **Owner:** owner`. One finding, two blocks, disagreeing on **who owns it** and **whether it is blocked**.
- **Why Check 2 is blind to it:** Check 2 (repaired by GOV-11) detects an OPEN form contradicting a RESOLVED form. `IN PROGRESS` and `BLOCKED` are **both** open forms, so no contradiction is reported — while a reader is told both that engineering is working on it and that it awaits the owner. That is GOV-12's class (one finding, two identities) in the status dimension rather than the id dimension.
- **Fix:** extend Check 2 to flag a single id whose blocks disagree on `Owner:`, or on mutually exclusive open states. Not done here — it is a guard change and belongs with a mutation proof in both directions.

### GOV-16 — NEW 2026-09-04: nothing binds the manifest's open-decision line to the register's open rows in the direction that matters
- **Category:** governance / boot integrity · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** 📋 **OPEN — engineering** · **Owner:** engineering
- **Evidence:** Check 11 verifies that every id the **manifest** raises resolves in the register, and Check 14 that none is already decided. **Neither checks the converse.** A census of this register found roughly forty-five non-closed rows, including the entire **DC-star** family (DC-6, DC-8, DC-10 through DC-29) and **PH8-3 through PH8-8**, none of which the manifest surfaces.
- **Why this is a real gap and not a definitional quibble:** the manifest's line is what a cold-start agent reads as "the open decisions". Several of those DC rows are labelled `PENDING (OPTIONAL / NEEDS MORE EVIDENCE)` — an evidence gap, which the owner's 2026-09-04 directive explicitly says **must not remain an owner decision**. They have sat unexamined since 2026-07-11 precisely because no guard makes them visible at boot.
- **Fix:** a check that every register row in an OPEN/BLOCKED state carrying an `owner:` action field appears on the manifest's line, or is explicitly classified engineering-owned. Not done here for the same reason as GOV-15.

## Decision census, pass 2 — 2026-09-04: every non-closed row dispositioned

The 2026-09-04 closure pass triaged the fifteen ids the manifest surfaced and reported "~45 non-closed rows, ~30 untriaged". Both figures were wrong and the framing behind them was wrong too. A positional census — reading **column 9 (`Status`)** rather than pattern-matching the whole row — finds **237 distinct ids**: **131 closed**, **35 `DESIGN-READY`**, **71 open or other**, i.e. **100 non-closed rows**, not 45.

### The central finding: `Owner Decision` is a SCHEDULING column, not a question column

Most of the apparent debt is an artifact of reading one column's name as its meaning. This register's own header states the policy:

> *Findings are classified **Architecturally Required** (evidence proves it belongs in a complete modern Travel ERP/CRM/Revenue platform → its **design** must exist now) or **Architecturally Optional**… **Implementation timing belongs only to the owner.***

and its legend states the vocabulary: **`Status:` OPEN · DESIGN-READY · RESOLVED · VERIFIED**.

So a row reading `Required · OPEN · Owner Decision: pending · Batch 4` does **not** mean "the owner must answer a question". It means **the design is accepted and the build is unscheduled** — the `pending` is *timing*, which the policy line says is the owner's and nobody else's. The **2026-07-11 Validation reclassifications note** (authoritative, §"Validation reclassifications") already dispositioned the DC family on exactly this axis, moving seven rows to `PENDING (OPTIONAL / NEEDS MORE EVIDENCE)` and confirming the rest as *accepted-required*.

**Sixty-nine of the hundred are that class** — DC-6/8/10/11/12/14/15/17/18/19/20/21/22/23/25/26/27/28/29, B2/B6/B7/B8, OPS-1, AUDIT-5, PH8-3…PH8-8, RPC-1, ATTR-1, and the 35 `DESIGN-READY` rows (BF-\*, CDD-\*, RC-\*, FOE-\*, R-series). They are a **product backlog**, not decision debt, and carrying them as owner decisions is what made the debt look three times larger than it is.

### TAX-1 — RETRACTED 2026-09-04. It was never a defect, and the error was mine.

**TAX-1 claimed `invoices.external_submission_status_code` was guarded against a catalog type that does not exist, making the column structurally unusable. That is false.**

The catalog family is **`tax_submission_status_code`**, it **exists**, and it carries five active system values — `pending`, `submitted`, `failed`, `accepted`, `rejected`. The trigger declares that exact name.

**How the error was made, stated plainly:** the previous pass read `pg_get_triggerdef` output that was **truncated in the terminal at the display boundary**, losing the `_code` suffix. It then queried `catalog_types` for the truncated string `tax_submission_status`, got zero rows, and recorded a finding. **A finding was built on a truncated string** — the exact "static analysis is a lead, never a verdict" failure this register catalogues, committed while writing about that class.

**Proven behaviourally, both directions, in a rolled-back transaction:**

```
update public.invoices set external_submission_status_code = 'submitted'       -> ACCEPTED
update public.invoices set external_submission_status_code = 'totally_made_up' -> REFUSED
   "totally_made_up" is not an active value of catalog family "tax_submission_status_code"
```

**Generalised rather than spot-fixed:** all **65** `enforce_catalog_codes` (column, family) pairs across every table were resolved against `catalog_values`. **Zero mismatches.** There is no defect here and no class behind it. The five values are a generic submission lifecycle, not ETA policy — **no tax rule is encoded anywhere, and none was invented.**

### FIN-7 — ✅ IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-04 (`202607060200`), DEPLOYED TO PRIMARY

- **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — implemented and deployed** · **Owner:** engineering
- **Re-verified before implementing, as instructed, and the evidence held.** The illegal move was **reproduced live** first: with the table door unguarded, `draft → paid` (skipping `issued`, with no payment) was **ACCEPTED**. `authenticated` holds `INSERT, SELECT, UPDATE` on `public.invoices`, so PostgREST exposes that door.
- **Six transitions, read off the two RPCs including their permissions** — `draft→issued` (CREATE_INVOICE, what `app.issue_invoice` charges) and five payment moves (RECORD_PAYMENT, what `app.record_payment` charges). Nothing invented.
- **`-> overdue` and `-> voided` deliberately unregistered**: the first has no producer anywhere; the second is VOID-1, still an open owner decision. Both are now **refused** at the table door, which is strictly safer than before.
- **Proven by `94_invoice_state_machine_test.sql` (21 assertions)**: the full RPC path still works end to end with the trigger live (create → issue → part pay → part pay → settle), each positive control asserting the row *moved* rather than merely not throwing; the second part payment proves `partially_paid → partially_paid` is treated as a non-transition; and three table-door refusals are backed by **defect injection (PAR-4)** — the trigger is dropped, the same skip is proven to SUCCEED, and the trigger restored.
- **`88_parent_state_on_every_door_test.sql` flagged a sixth pair, investigated rather than suppressed.** Registering `invoices` made it a parent PARENT-1's detector examines, surfacing `record_payment -> payments (invoices)`. It is a **false positive, provably**: `public.payments` has **no `invoice_id` column**, so a table-door guard there would have no invoice to read; the invoice link is `payment_allocations`, which already carries `payment_allocations_guard_parent_state` (PAY-1, `202607059500`). Recorded as the sixth verified non-defect **with that evidence**, not by relaxing the guard.
- **Smoke CHECK 5g** correctly refused the run at "expected 11 status-transition triggers, found 12"; the **pin was updated to 12**, never the guard weakened.

### GOV-17 — NEW: my own closure blocks used a `Status` form Check 2 cannot read

- **Category:** governance / guard coverage · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04** · **Owner:** engineering
- The 2026-09-04 closure pass wrote seven verdicts as `**Status:** ✅ **DECISION CLOSED — …**`. Check 2's resolved anchor is `\*\*Status:?\*\*\s*\*{0,2}\s*(?:✅\s*)?(RESOLVED|FIXED|IMPLEMENTED|CLOSED)\b` — it permits `**` **before** the tick, not after, and its vocabulary is four words that do not include `DECISION CLOSED`. **All seven failed the anchor**, tested against two known-good controls that matched. So seven closures were invisible to the guard, and five of them sat in live contradiction with their own table rows while Check 2 printed *"no contradiction"*.
- **This is GOV-11's class, one week after GOV-11 was found and fixed, committed by the same hand that fixed it.** A guard repaired against one wording is still a guard whose vocabulary the next author must guess.
- **Fixed:** all seven rewritten to `**Status:** **✅ RESOLVED — …**`; readable resolved-status lines rose **46 → 53**. The five contradictory table rows now carry the new verdict with their superseded text retained.

### GOV-18 — NEW: Check 2 sees a row as OPEN only when the cell is the bare word `OPEN`

- **Category:** governance / guard coverage (MEAS-1) · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** **✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-04 — IMPLEMENTED and mutation-proven** · **Owner:** engineering
- **Fixed 2026-09-04, and the population was re-measured against the current file first: the bare form covers 20 of 84 open rows, not 19 of 54** — the earlier figure counted a narrower substrate. Check 2's contradiction pass, and through `$xOpen` AUD-04's cross-Master pass, were blind to **76%** of the open population.
- **Two changes, and the first is what makes the second safe.** (1) **The status cell is found from its table's own header**, so each row is judged on the cell that actually holds its status — index 9 in this register, but 2 in `MASTER_CERTIFICATION_STATUS` and 5/6 in `MASTER_INTEGRATION_CATALOG`. Without it, widening the vocabulary would have read the `Cert` column (a bare ✅) and the `Owner Decision` column (the bare word `pending`), calling nearly every row open **and** resolved at once. Measured before trusting: across all 15 Masters, **zero** rows have a non-status cell leading with a resolved marker while their status cell does not, so narrowing resolved-detection to that cell changed no existing verdict. (2) **The open vocabulary was derived from the files**, by enumerating every distinct leading token of every status cell: the register's own legend plus the deferral forms in use. Terminal verdicts stay out deliberately.
- **Detail blocks are deliberately NOT scanned for `open`, and the asymmetry is the design.** A `###` block is an append-only narrative, so `BLOCKED` in an early block followed by `RESOLVED` in a later one is correct history — TASK-3, ORPH-1, PERM-1, RBAC-2 and LEAD-2/3/4 all have that shape, and treating a block as open would manufacture contradictions out of every one of them. The table row is the current-state record, so `open` is read only there; `resolved` is read from either substrate (GOV-11), which is what makes a stale row detectable.
- **Proven, not asserted:** `scripts/test_status_contradiction_guard.ps1` — **25 assertions, 0 failures**, over isolated sandbox copies, in **both directions**. Ten open forms must be flagged (`BLOCKED`, `DESIGN-READY`, `PENDING (…)`, `OPEN — …`, `IN PROGRESS`, `PARTIALLY RESOLVED`, `TRIGGER-DEFERRED`, `VALIDATED-REQUIRED`, `MOVED→PENDING`, bare `OPEN`) and the suite separately proves the **pre-GOV-18 detector saw only one of the ten**. Eleven terminal verdicts must NOT be flagged, including the `**✅ RESOLVED … Superseded text:** **BLOCKED …**` shape seventeen real rows carry. Two controls bracket the suite — and the first control **caught a real defect in the harness itself** (a case-insensitive filter that matched Check 2's own banner) before the suite certified anything.
- **It immediately found two real defects on the live register, both fixed in the same change:** `ATTR-1` and `CAT-5` each carried an older row still reading `OPEN` while a newer row or block recorded the resolution — two of GOV-19's four identity splits, rediscovered independently by the widened detector. Each superseded row now states its resolution with the evidence verified live (`offline_conversions` carries the snapshot columns; `app.sub_status_family` plus the `booking_items` trigger exist).
- **Superseded text:** `$rowOpen = $line -match '\|\s*OPEN\s*\|'` — a *padded cell containing exactly* `OPEN`. Deliberate when written, to kill prose false-positives. The consequence was never measured: **19 rows are open in that bare form; 35 are open in a richer one** — `BLOCKED — …`, `OWNER DECISION — …`, `OPEN — …`, `PENDING (…)`, `IN PROGRESS`, `PARTIALLY RESOLVED`. Check 2's contradiction pass is therefore blind to **65% of the open population**, which is exactly how GOV-17's five contradictions printed CLEAN.
- **Not fixed here, deliberately.** Widening `$rowOpen` changes what the repository's central contradiction guard reports and must arrive with a mutation proof in both directions (a row it must flag and one it must not) — `AGENTS.md §6`. Registered rather than silently deferred.
- **Recommended fix:** anchor on the leading verdict of the status cell (the discipline Check 14 already uses) rather than on cell equality.

### GOV-16 — UPDATED: the population is now measured, and most of it is not decision debt

GOV-16 recorded that nothing binds the register's open rows to the manifest's decision line. That remains true and unfixed. What this census adds is the **shape** of the gap: of the 100 non-closed rows the manifest does not surface, **69 are scheduling backlog and only 11 are genuine owner business decisions**. A register→manifest guard must therefore compare *decisions*, not *open rows* — a naive check would have demanded the manifest carry 100 ids and made the boot line useless. **That is why the guard is still not written**, and it is a better reason than the one GOV-16 first gave.

### GOV-19 — NEW 2026-09-04: CAT-6 exists under two identities with opposite statuses, and Check 14 found it by refusing my own edit

- **Category:** governance / registry integrity · **Severity:** Medium · **Status:** 📋 **OPEN — engineering** · **Owner:** engineering
- **How it surfaced:** the census classified **CAT-6** as a genuine owner decision from its standalone row, and adding it to the manifest's open-decision line made **Check 14 fail** — *"manifest lists 'CAT-6' as open, but the register marks it RESOLVED 2026-08-24 (SPEC-141)"*. The guard was right and the census was wrong.
- **Evidence:** the register carries **two rows whose subject includes CAT-6**. The combined row `| CAT-5/CAT-6 | Conditional sub-status family … | **RESOLVED 2026-08-24 (SPEC-141)** |` resolves what its own text describes as *CAT-5's* mapping, while a **separate `| CAT-6 |` row** — five categorical columns (`branches.branch_type`, `company_assets.asset_type`, `catalog_types.ownership_type`, `user_role_assignments.scope_type`, `feature_entitlements.feature_code`) with no catalog family — is still **OPEN**. One id, two rows, opposite verdicts.
- **This is GOV-12's class** (one finding under two identities) in its mirror image: there, two ids for one finding; here, one id spanning two findings because a combined `A/B` row was resolved on A's evidence while B rides along in the id string.
- **Why Check 2 could not see it:** the standalone row's status is `OPEN - not fixed by SPEC-136 …`, not the bare cell `OPEN`, so **GOV-18** makes it invisible to the contradiction pass. Two guard defects compounding — which is precisely the argument for fixing GOV-18 first.
- **CAT-6 is therefore NOT on the manifest's decision line** until the split is resolved: with the register self-contradictory, promoting it to the boot surface would publish a contradiction rather than a decision.
- **Fix (engineering, no owner input):** split the combined row so `CAT-5` carries its own resolution and `CAT-6` carries its own open status, or rename the combined row to `CAT-5` alone. Then re-assess whether CAT-6's five catalog-less columns are a decision or a defect.

## Decision census RECONCILIATION — 2026-09-04: the counts did not close, and the reason was a second substrate

The pass-2 section above stands as the record of what was measured, and its central insight — that `Owner Decision` is a scheduling column — is confirmed here. **Its arithmetic and its owner-decision list are superseded by this section.** Three defects, each in the method rather than in the register:

**1. The totals never closed.** Pass 2 reported `237 = 131 closed + 35 DESIGN-READY + 71 open`, then called the non-closed population **100**. `35 + 71 = 106`, and `71` alone is 71. **No reading of its own components yields 100**, so every bucket beneath it was assigned against a total that was never computed — which is why the buckets summed to 101 in one place and 100 in another.

**2. The census read one substrate; the register has two.** `Check 11` — the guard that decides whether a finding *exists* — defines a finding as **the first cell of a table row OR a `###` detail heading**. Pass 2 enumerated only the table. Re-run over both substrates, as Check 11 itself defines them:

| Substrate | Distinct ids |
|---|---|
| table rows | 250 |
| `###` detail blocks | 112 |
| **union (Check 11's own definition)** | **314** |
| **defined ONLY by a detail block** | **64** |

**`VOID-1`, `RET-1` and `RET-2` have no table row at all.** They were on the manifest's decision line while being invisible to the census that claimed to have dispositioned everything.

**3. The bucket counts and the named list were computed over different populations.** `G` was counted as **10** — which is exactly the number of *table rows* among the genuine decisions — while the prose beside it named **13**, adding the three detail-block ids. The `13`/`14`/`10`/`11` discrepancy was never a miscount; it was two populations wearing one label.

### The finding that matters: five settled decisions were put back on the manifest

**`OWNER-1` records that on 2026-09-01 the owner ratified eighteen decisions**, naming each and its answer, and closes: *"Each remains an ENGINEERING task where implementation is owed; none is an open QUESTION."* It then lists what stayed open: `RET-1 · FIN-7 · VOID-1 · TRANS-1 · DELIV-1 · PH8-2 · PLAN-1 · DOC-LC-2 · DOC-LC-3 · CANON-26-1 · LIC-1`.

**Proven from git history**, not inferred — the manifest's decision line at `45ba216` and at every revision back to `a52b5c7` reads `OWNER-1 QUO-4 SUP-4c CUST-3 RET-1 FIN-7 VOID-1 VERIFY-1 TRANS-1 DELIV-1 PH8-2 PLAN-1 DOC-LC-2 DOC-LC-3 CANON-26-1 LIC-1 CONV-3`. **`BLOCKED-4`, `BLOCKED-5`, `AUDIT-4`, `RET-2` and `A3` are absent** — they had been removed *because the owner decided them*. `b958691` then re-added `RET-2`, and `33a86d6` re-added the other four plus `BOOK-2`, under the claim that six ids *"had never been on the manifest line before — GOV-16's real cost, now measured"*.

**That claim is true of exactly one id: `BOOK-2`.** The other five were absent by decision. Re-adding them re-created the precise defect `OWNER-1` exists to record: **settled questions presented to a fresh session as live blockers.** The stale-decision class, five days after the guard against it was written.

### GOV-20 — NEW: a ratification recorded in one row is invisible on the row it decides

- **Category:** governance / guard coverage (MEAS-1) · **Severity:** High · **Status:** 📋 **OPEN — engineering** · **Owner:** engineering
- **Why Check 14 passed while five stale decisions sat on the line.** Check 14 derives its decided-set from **each id's own status cell**, deliberately and correctly: its own comment states that an exemption list *"would be one more thing to go stale, which is the defect this check exists to catch."* But `BLOCKED-4`, `BLOCKED-5`, `AUDIT-4` and `A3` still read `BLOCKED — BUSINESS DECISION` / `OPEN` on **their own** rows, last `Updated` **08-29** and **08-16** — *before* the 2026-09-01 ratification. `RET-2` has no row at all. The owner's answer lived only inside **`OWNER-1`'s** status cell, as prose about other findings. **A guard that reads status cells cannot see a verdict stored in a different row's prose.**
- **This is the ratification's own recording defect, not Check 14's logic.** Eighteen decisions were minuted in one place and never written back to the eighteen rows they settled, so the register has since 2026-09-01 asserted both that they are decided and that they are blocked.
- **Corrected here, at the source:** all seventeen recoverable ids now carry the verdict on **their own** status cell or detail block, in a form Check 14 and Check 2 both read — `BLOCKED-4`, `BLOCKED-5`, `AUDIT-4`, `A3`, `PH8-3`, `PH8-5`, `PP-1` (rows) and `DEL-1`, `RET-2`, `EVT-2`, `SCHED-1`, `FIN-5` ×2, `SYSADMIN-1`, `SPP-3`, `DOC-EXP-1`, `AUTH-1` (blocks). `SEC-1` already carried it and was the model.
- **Proven by mutation, both directions:** with the corrected rows in place, re-adding `BLOCKED-4` to the manifest line makes **Check 14 fail** (`STALE OWNER DECISION`), and removing it makes Check 14 pass. Before this correction the same mutation was **invisible** — which is the whole finding.
- **What remains for GOV-20 proper:** nothing binds a *ratification record* to the rows it ratifies. The recommended guard reads the decided-ids named inside a reconciliation row's status and asserts each names its own verdict on its own row. Not built here — it is a new guard, and `AGENTS.md §6` requires a mutation proof in both directions.

### GOV-19 — WIDENED: the identity split is four ids, not one

The measured MIXED set — one id carrying **opposite verdicts in two rows** — is `ATTR-1`, `CAT-5`, `CAT-6`, `SEC-3`. `CAT-6` was found only because Check 14 refused an edit; the other three were never surfaced. All four are the same defect and are fixed the same way: split the combined row, or mark the superseded one as superseded. `CAT-6` stays off the manifest decision line until its split is resolved.

### Reconciled totals — these close, in both directions

**Population: 314 distinct findings** (Check 11's own definition). **163 closed · 151 non-closed.**

| Disposition | Table rows | Detail-only | **Total** | What it means |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **C — scheduling backlog** | 74 | 2 | **76** | Design accepted, batch assigned. Not a question. (44 `DESIGN-READY` + 30 `OPEN`-form + `CONV-3`, `DC-7`) |
| **I — DECIDED 2026-09-01, implementation owed** | 8 | 9 | **17** | The `OWNER-1` set. Engineering tasks, **not** open questions |
| **E — superseded / terminal by an earlier verdict** | 15 | 2 | **17** | `ACCEPTED RISK`, `TRIGGER-DEFERRED`, `OBSOLETE (merged)`, `INTENTIONAL` |
| **D — engineering defect** | 11 | 5 | **16** | incl. the 4 identity splits (GOV-19) and GOV-15/16/18/19/20 |
| **J — closed in substance, unreadable in form** | 15 | 0 | **15** | `PROVEN NOT A DEFECT`, `CONTROL APPLIED`, `BUILT`, `CORRECTED`, `RECONCILED`, `DECIDED AND IMPLEMENTED` — terminal verdicts no guard can read. **This is GOV-18's population, now measured** |
| **G — genuine owner business decision** | 6 | 1 | **7** | SUP-4c · CUST-3 · PLAN-1 · DOC-LC-3 · CANON-26-1 · BOOK-2 · **VOID-1** |

`74+17+17+16+15+7+2+1+0 = 151` ✓ · `151 + 163 = 314` ✓ · every non-closed finding carries exactly one disposition.

**Genuine owner input = 8: seven business decisions (G) + one compliance decision (H).** That is the manifest's decision line, and it is now the same eight ids in both documents.

**The 69-backlog claim is confirmed and slightly enlarged to 76.** Spot-checked against the register's own policy line — a `Required` finding's design must exist now and *"implementation timing belongs only to the owner"* — and against the 2026-07-11 Validation reclassifications note. Two rows were pulled OUT of backlog on evidence: `DC-27` keeps its ADR artifact owed, and `RPC-1` is a programme underway (10 of 37 closed), neither an unanswered question. No row in the backlog set carries an unresolved business choice; every one carries an accepted design and an unscheduled build.
